=== 2026-06-23/24 — Deck-cal corruption fixes, Studio UX, cartridge backfills, OT-2 scanners, run-control + param reliability ===
Full Ralph-method context reload on the deck calibration system; reconciled progress.txt
(stops at the 2026-06-18 Studio) with the CODE: NATIVE-CALIBRATION-SYSTEM PRDs 1-6 had since
shipped to master (RobotDeckOffset/TipCalibratorFixture models, calibrate-tip bridge cmd,
PRD-6 RTP cutover). The on-robot daemon is scripts/ot2-bridge.py IN THIS REPO (not separate);
bridge command kinds grew to 7 (…+restart_robot_server, auto_resume_run, calibrate_tip).

DECK-CAL Z-SHIFT CORRUPTION (root-caused from data):
- savePosition returns the pipette CRITICAL POINT, which drops by the tip length (~49mm) when
  a tip is picked up — so the "home value 200 → 150.73" is EXPECTED, not a bug. The Studio's
  captureOffset did dz = liveZ − nominal.z without locking tip state, so a tip change between
  Move-to-hole and Capture leaked the tip length into saved deltas → corrupted geometry.
- DATA proof: gen4deck_gen7cartridge_004 well-z had crept 8.8mm → 16-82mm over 7845 edits
  (5955 with |Δ|>5mm; A1 timeline showed +44/±20 swings). Siblings 001/002/003 clean.
  Inflated max well-z (82) made safeArcZ = max(wellZ)+80 = 162 → +tip ≈211mm > gantry max →
  "FailedToPlanMoveError: Arc out of bounds in Z". RECOVERED deck-004 to baseline (earliest
  `before` per well, z 8.5-9.1) via scratchpad recover script; corrupted state backed up.
  NOTE: robot still holds the old bundle until Sync; local lab-Mac JSON mirror also stale.
- FIXES (master): (1) tip-state lock — nominal stamped with hasTip; captureOffset refused on
  tip change; nominal/tipAdjust cleared on pickup/calibrate/close. (2) safeArcZ from labware
  dimensions.z clamped to a machine ceiling (115mm) instead of corruptible well-z. (3) #6 —
  "Move to hole" now uses ABSOLUTE moveToCoordinates from live Mongo coords via a once-derived
  slot origin → edits apply in the OPEN run, no close/reopen (the run caches loaded geometry;
  Mongo IS source of truth and only reaches the robot on Sync). (4) physical-bounds guard in
  apply-edit (rejected the 15mm magnitude cap per operator — corrections can be large — kept
  only 0≤x≤xDim, 0≤y≤yDim, 0≤z).

DECK-003 "Input should be a valid integer" on B14 (looked robot-specific):
- It was a corrupted DEF, not the robot/positions. Calibration had pushed deck-003 row-A wells
  to y=-0.5/-0.2 (off the front edge). OT-2 labware schema requires well coords ≥0, so
  registering the def 422'd (int|float≥0 union → "valid integer"), breaking move-to-hole
  wherever 003 was loaded — only "B14" because that's where 003 was selected. The "crazy dance"
  was the search-raster fallback firing on every miss. FIXED: clamped the 8 wells to y=0 in
  Mongo; extended the apply-edit guard to enforce x/y physical bounds (was Z-only).

STUDIO UX: 0.25mm reference grid (1mm + 10mm bold lines, SVG pattern, toggle); live hover
coordinate readout (cursor x/y + nearest hole z); 0.5mm jog step; "Set position → one hole"
(absolute x/y/z, prefilled, computes delta under the engine); session multi-level Undo of the
last applied shift (offset/global/set-position).

CARTRIDGE BACKFILLS (direct Mongo, cartridge_records, _id = barcode):
- "ready for reagent filling" = status wax_ready (reagent intake gate hard-checks status===
  'wax_ready'). Backfilled 24 scanned carts → wax_ready (22 created, 1 advanced wax_stored→
  wax_ready, 1 skipped because completed) + 1 replacement cart; minimal authentic wax shape +
  waxQc Accepted + backfill marker + AuditLog. (The larger 583-cart bucket backfill was paused
  — 136 already existed incl. completed/cancelled; report CSVs handed to operator to reconcile.)

OT-2 SCANNERS (new Waveshare units): B14 scanner "not turning on" + danced every barcode.
Real cause (from B14 daemon log): serial EIO — `Scan attempt failed (5, 'Input/output error')`
at every slot → "0 scans, 24 errors". A *connection* fault, not positions/illumination.
(My initial under-voltage theory was wrong — B14 does uniquely under-volt, but hammering the
scanner didn't worsen it; the EIO was the proximate fault.) Operator swapped the USB cable;
restarted ot2-bridge to drop the stale handle; confirmed clean reads + a live barcode decode
(f4c59368-…). R04 new scanner: clean out of the gate (throttled 0x0, no EIO, decoded
9d1325d6-…). Diagnostic pattern: `(5,'Input/output error')` in the daemon log = serial/cable.

RUN-CONTROL RELIABILITY (PRD docs/prds/OT2-RUN-CONTROL-RELIABILITY.md): pause/resume/stop on
the wax/reagent run screens were spotty (driven from Vercel → status polls AND actions share
the serialized bridge queue). Fixed EmbeddedRunController.svelte + the actions endpoint: Stop
always enabled while live; handle finishing/blocked-by-open-door/awaiting-recovery; endpoint
returns {conflict, robotStatus} for 4xx (action invalid for current state) so the client treats
it as benign and reconciles by re-poll instead of a 502; sticky optimistic status (polls don't
overwrite during an action) kills the flicker; skip polling during an action + reconcile right
after; AbortSignal timeouts (poll 6s / action 12s) + destroyed guard.

RUN PARAMS: bool RTPs ignored operator toggles — an unchecked HTML checkbox posts no field, so
turning a default-on param OFF (e.g. tracer) dropped param_<name> and startRun's "absent → use
default" fallback ran the default. FIXED ProtocolStartPanel.svelte: bool params now post via a
hidden input that ALWAYS carries true/false (same pattern already used for tiprack_refilled);
the visible checkbox just drives state. Affects wax + reagent (shared panel).

All committed + pushed to master; npm run check stayed at the 11-error baseline throughout.

DECK-CAL / RUN-CONTROL "FUNCTION_INVOCATION_TIMEOUT" (Vercel): on the deployed app the Studio
jog/move + run-control calls go over the bridge (each robot round-trip up to ~30s), but several

move-to(60 — used by "Move to hole"'s absolute-coordinate path), position(45), load-labware(60),

already had it.) Note: the bridge itself still caps a single robot call at 30s (BRIDGE_TIMEOUT_MS)
— a separate ceiling if a robot op (e.g. home over the bridge) genuinely needs longer.

QUICK-SEAL SHORTCUT (PRD docs/prds/QUICK-SEAL-WAXQC-TO-SEALED.md): new page
/manufacturing/cart-mfg/quick-seal (+ "Quick Seal" sidebar entry after Cut Top Seal). Bulk-scan
cartridge barcodes into a free-text <textarea> (scanner Enter = newline, focus retained — scan
many without clicking) and flip them from wax_qc/wax_ready straight to `sealed` (the state before
the reagent pic), shortcutting the verdict + reagent-fill steps for an out-of-BIMS workflow.
Decisions: accept wax_qc OR wax_ready; just flip to sealed (operator+timestamp+priorStatus+note+
AuditLog, no top-seal lot); per-cart reject (not found / wrong status) with reasons, valid carts
still process and rejects stay in the box. Direct CartridgeRecord.updateOne (sacred middleware
doesn't block — finalizedAt never set). Cartridge status flow reference (cartridge-record.ts enum):
backing → wax_filling → wax_stored → wax_qc → wax_ready → reagent_filling → reagent_filled →
sealed → reagent_qc → reagent_ready → cured → stored → released → shipped (+ wax_rejected/
reagent_rejected branches; wax_filled & 'inspected' are legacy/unused; reagent intake gate hard-
checks status==='wax_ready'; capture flips only wax_stored→wax_qc).

All committed + pushed to master; npm run check stayed at the 11-error baseline.

=== 2026-06-30 — Deck-cal: "Set position" now works on the whole highlight selection ===
PRD docs/prds/DECK-CAL-SET-POSITION-SELECTION.md. The Deck Calibration Studio
(/manufacturing/cart-mfg/deck-calibration) "Set position → one hole" panel was hard-gated to
selection.size===1. Lifted that cap so the operator can type an exact x/y/z for ANY highlighted
group (click / shift-click / box-drag).
SEMANTICS: a deck is distinct holes, so forcing every selected hole to the same coordinate would
collapse them — the only sound multi-hole "set position" is an ANCHOR-relative rigid translate.
The typed x/y/z is the anchor hole's new position; the whole selection shifts by the same delta
(relative spacing preserved). Single-hole selection = byte-identical to the old behavior. Mirrors
the existing "Shift whole grid" anchor pattern but scoped to the selection + driven by an absolute
target instead of typed dx/dy/dz.
ANCHOR: most-recently-clicked hole while it stays selected; else falls back to the first selected
hole in deck-definition order so the anchor is always defined. Inputs prefill from the anchor's
current coords on anchor change; the panel shows the active anchor ("+N more move with it").
UI-ONLY, reuses the existing engine — NO server/model/API change. applyAbsolute now accepts
selCount>=1 and routes the anchor delta through applyDelta → ?/applyBatch (applyDeckEditBatch),
inheriting bounds-guard, DeckCalibrationEdit history, AuditLog, single-undo (inverse delta),
live-run update, and "Sync for real fills" semantics unchanged. Out-of-bounds members still
report via failed[].
FILES: src/routes/manufacturing/cart-mfg/deck-calibration/+page.svelte (anchorWell state + anchor
derived; toggleWell stamps anchor on add; setX/Y/Z prefill off anchor; applyAbsolute rewrite;
"Set position → selection" panel — title/help/anchor readout/button). + the PRD.
npm run check: 11-error baseline (0 new in deck-calibration/+page.svelte). Committed +
pushed to master (3be991ac).

=== 2026-07-01 — Deck-003 "holes off again" diagnosed + one-click "Re-baseline deck" ===
Operator reported deck-003 "Move to Hole" was exact yesterday, off today; wanted to know if the
work saved/uploaded. DATA (read-only Mongo, prod .env): all 3219 deck_calibration_edits intact,
nothing reverted; yesterday's edited wells unchanged; +168 edits today. Robot B07 (hidden-leaf):
NOT rebooted (up 5d20h), Opentrons deck cal unchanged (lastModified 2023-11-21), pipette offsets
zero — robot frame did not move. Clarified: Studio "Move to Hole" loads the deck def LIVE from
Mongo into a maintenance run — it does NOT depend on the fill-protocol Sync (Sync only affects
real wax/reagent runs). ROOT CAUSE: whole-deck placement drift. Today's 60 re-tuned holes moved by
a tight mean (0.4,-1.5,0)mm (±0.3) = a uniform shift, not per-hole drift; history has 83 uniform
bulk-shift batches (~12 on the 6/30 evening alone), almost all ~1mm in -Y. So the deck goes off by
~1mm/session (systematic -Y — either a non-repeatable physical mount OR a systematic offset in the
STALE-prod Move-to-Hole math that the fixed master code may not have; re-test after prod promote).
Operator had been correcting it 12 holes (one cartridge) at a time — the time sink.
FIX (PRD docs/prds/DECK-CAL-REBASELINE-WHOLE-DECK.md): added rebaselineDeck() + a prominent
"⇱ Deck moved? Re-baseline ALL 576 holes" button to the Offset panel — applies the captured/typed
dx/dy/dz to EVERY hole (both wax + reagent, ignoring the role filter) in one click via the existing
applyDeckEditBatch (bounds guard + history + AuditLog + single Undo + live-run update). Complements
the existing applyGlobalShift (which only hits the active role → easy to half-shift the deck).
UI-only, no server change. npm run check: 11-error baseline (0 new in deck-calibration/+page.svelte).
Reaches operators only after the stale prod alias is promoted to master.

================================================================================
2026-07-01 — FIX: reagent/wax run "succeeds" instantly without filling (B07)
================================================================================
Root cause (evidence-backed, see memory project-reagent-empty-run-calibrator):
NOT a connection/bridge bug and NOT a parameter-parsing bug. Verified the robot
received well_2=true correctly. The run bailed BEFORE the fill logic: the protocol
searches /dev/ttyACM* for the tip-calibrator once at startup; the calibrator (a USB
serial device) enumerates a few SECONDS after the run starts, so the cold probe
missed it → protocol.pause('Unable to find calibrator serial port') then `return`
→ run ends with 16 setup commands, zero fills. BIMS auto-resumes that first pause
(off-deck auto-resume) → the empty run lands as "succeeded". Evidence: run 6763d05c
command stream ended at waitForResume; /dev/ttyACM0 (Particle calibrator) mtime was
16:44, ~AFTER the ~16:43:50 probe; calibrator answers 'C'->'=-1.0:1:0' fine now.

Fix (protocols/Reagent_Filling_GEN7.py + Wax_Filling_GEN7_Cartridge.py, identical):
- Extracted the port probe into _probe_for_calibrator(); added _find_calibrator()
  that POLLS for the calibrator for ~25s (handles late USB enumeration) instead of
  one cold probe.
- Replaced the silent `pause(...); return` with a retry-on-resume loop: pause with a
  clear "Tip calibrator missing — Resume to retry" message, and each Resume re-probes
  and CONTINUES the run (never returns to an empty success). Matches the operator's
  "error pops, resume/re-run, then it fills" workaround — now automatic.
- Analysis pass (is_simulating) kept single-probe + return so upload/analysis can't
  hang on the retry loop. py_compile clean on both.
PENDING DEPLOY: the fix only takes effect once the .py is re-uploaded to B07 (POST
/api/opentrons-lab/robots/[id]/protocols → new opentronsProtocolId; select it in the
Start Run panel). Repo edit alone does nothing on the robot.

2026-07-01 (follow-up) — reagent/wax "empty succeeded" round 2: offset-read bail
The calibrator-not-found retry fix worked (calibrator now found), but the run still
bailed at the NEXT trap: after finding the calibrator it reads the 'C' offset, and on
B07 the calibrator returns a persistent bad value `=-1.0:1:0` (verified via SSH, 5/5;
unchanged after a USB replug — the probe/limit-switch measurement is stuck, though 'I'
particle-ID reads fine and correctly IDs deck 003). The old code did pause+return on that
parse failure → empty "succeeded" run again.
Fix (both protocols): the offset-read failure no longer returns — it pauses ("Could not
read offset calibration … Resume to START THE RUN ANYWAY"), sets offset x=y=0, and
CONTINUES (per-tip X/Y probe still runs; bims_native global offset applied via set_offset).
Matches operator experience: resume past the "missing offset data" error and the run fills
fine. Also converted the particle-ID bail to retry-on-resume (never return-to-empty), with
an is_simulating guard so analysis can't hang on the loop. Redeployed to all 3 robots.
Calibrator 'C' is still returning -1.0 (hardware/probe issue, worked Jun 24) — flagged for
physical follow-up, but runs no longer depend on it.

2026-07-01 — feat/cartridge-photos-agent-api — cross-app cartridge photos API (M1 of CARTRIDGE-PHOTOS-01)
================================================================================
First milestone of the CARTRIDGE-PHOTOS-01 PRD (docs/prds/CARTRIDGE-PHOTOS-01-barcode-
enrichment.md): surface each cartridge's photos + tags + notes in the brevitest-research
app, next to every barcode, grouped by the capture STATE (phase) they were shot in.

M1 (this branch, BIMS side): new agent endpoint
  GET /api/agent/cartridge/[barcode]/photos
- Guarded by AGENT_API_KEY via requireAgentApiKey() ($lib/server/api-auth).
- connectDB(); queries BOTH CartridgeRecord (photos[]/notes[]) and cv_images
  (cartridgeTag.labels/notes, qcLabel, thumbnailPath, imageUrl) for the barcode
  (= CartridgeRecord._id / scanned UUID).
- Merges the two sources, dedupes by imageId / cartridgeImageNumber (embedded photos[]
  wins for the authoritative stored r2Url; cv_images enriches labels/notes/qc/thumbnail),
  groups by phase into photosByState (sorted oldest->newest), unions labels+qc verdicts
  into card-level tags[], and returns operator notes[] ({body, phase, author, createdAt}).
- Unknown barcode -> 200 { found:false, photosByState:{}, tags:[], notes:[] } so the
  research proxy has one simple success path. r2Url/thumbnailUrl are public R2 Worker URLs
  (getR2Url) — image bytes never transit this server.

npm run check: 11 errors / 428 warnings — all pre-existing baseline; ZERO from the new
endpoint. Not deployed (no push yet).

research proxy endpoint (server-to-server with AGENT_API_KEY) + BarcodeCard/CartridgePhotos
components wired into the 4 barcode display sites.

2026-07-07 — feat/cv-pipeline-v2 — CV pipeline V2 PRD (train/verify/deploy redesign)
=========================================================================
Investigated why saving a CV project deployment to the post_mortem phase reports
success but the post-mortem-inspect page says no model is assigned. Root cause
(verified against the production DB and an empirical Mongoose 9 probe): the CV
Mongoose schemas on master never declared most fields the CV code writes —
deployAtPhases/activeModelVersion/shadowModelVersion/trainedModels/classifier on
cv_projects, embedding/label on cv_images, plus a status-enum mismatch on
cv_inspections — and strict mode silently strips them from every create/$set.
Reads (.lean()) bypass the schema, so legacy May/June data still renders, which
masked the breakage. A partial schema fix exists on the main lineage (18b5b953f,
2026-06-15) but never merged to master.

Wrote docs/prds/CV-PIPELINE-V2.md: five-stage redesign — phase-scoped capture
(station step picks the model), unified labeling (capture-time OR stream UI, one
qcLabel field), training-set assembly that checks cartridge status/tags/phase,
versioned immutable trainedModels[] entries with per-version training-set
manifests (exact imageIds, newSincePrevious) so iterations improve without
erasing prior models, a holdout verification gate + explicit Deploy button, and
the human-review loop feeding the next iteration. Includes the schema fixes,
migration script spec, and a 4-PR implementation plan. Docs-only commit; npm run
check not applicable.

================================================================================
2026-07-07 — feat/cv-pipeline-v2 — CV-PIPELINE-V2 implemented (schemas, versioned train/verify/deploy, review queue)
================================================================================
Implemented the full CV-PIPELINE-V2 PRD (docs/prds/CV-PIPELINE-V2.md) via a
multi-agent workflow, then hand-verified. Changes:

- Schemas (the strict-mode unblock): cv-project.ts declares deployAtPhases/
  activeModelVersion/shadowModelVersion, trainingFilter, verifyGate, and the
  versioned trainedModels[] (immutable per-run entries carrying their own
  classifier weights, training-set manifest with exact imageIds +
  newSincePrevious, and holdout verification). cv-image.ts declares the
  embedding cache (select:false) + projectId/sampleId and relaxes cartridgeTag
  for project-scoped uploads; qcLabel is now the single label field.
  cv-inspection.ts status enum fixed to queued/running/completed/failed +
  isShadow/triggeredBy/At/confidenceThreshold/errorMessage declared + needs-
  review index. cartridge-record.ts photos[] gains verdictSummary.
- Training core: cv-bridge triggerTraining assembles by qcLabel + phase +
  trainingFilter (cartridge status/tag join), holds out a stratified ~20%
  sample, scores balanced accuracy against verifyGate (defaults 10/0.80), and
  APPENDS a trainedModels[] version (status verified when the gate passes).
  runInference resolves weights per version (shadow runs use shadow weights);
  non-shadow verdicts mirror {verdict, inspectionId, modelVersion} onto the
  cartridge's photos[] entry. /api/cv/train now trains in-process, audits, and
  returns version + gate result (stale GitHub-dispatch path removed).
- Deployment tab: version-history table with status pills + holdout metrics,
  gate-checked Deploy button (server re-checks verification.passed), rollback
  via redeploying older passed versions, shadow set/clear, per-version
  scorecard (model-vs-human agreement) on History. Fixed a pre-existing train
  action bug (read data.version off the wrong nesting; claimed async).
- Capture: optional Pass/Fail verdict at capture (writes qcLabel, audited,
  auto-resets per shot) + station assignedPhase-vs-phase mismatch warning.
- Review: new /cv/review needs-review queue (verdict but no humanLabel,
  shadows excluded) with Agree/Overrule that sets humanLabel and mirrors
  qcLabel, project/phase filters, and a count badge in the CV nav.
- scripts/migrate-cv-pipeline-v2.ts: raw-driver migration (label->qcLabel,
  legacy classifier wrapped as trainedModels v1, inspection status normalize);
  DRY-RUN by default, --apply to write. NOT yet run against prod.

npm run check: 10 errors / 428 warnings vs baseline 11/428 — zero errors in
any touched file (all 10 pre-existing: r2.ts, AskBimsWidget, assembly page).
This push creates a Vercel preview build for the branch (URL in the Vercel
dashboard under this commit).

project cards. PRD + diagram updated. Typical setup: two projects per phase
(e.g. Post-mortem Top / Post-mortem Bottom).

2026-07-09 — feat/cv-pipeline-v2 — barcode view auto-classification + 3-phase options + backfill
================================================================================
- Barcode auto-classification: cartridge barcodes are only visible in TOP
  photos, so when the operator leaves the /capture Top/Bottom toggle unset,
  the capture endpoints (capture + capture-ingest) run presence-only barcode
  detection (new dep zxing-wasm, sharp-downscaled frame, 3s cap, decoded value
  discarded): barcode ⇒ view 'top', none ⇒ 'bottom', stamped viewSource
  'barcode-auto'; manual toggle wins ('manual'); detector failure leaves the
  photo untagged and NEVER blocks a capture. Side mapping is one constant in
  src/lib/server/services/barcode-detect.ts. Functional round-trip self-test
  (zxing writer → composite → detect) passes.

  the deploy-at checkboxes (legacy phases stay visible on projects that have
  them).

  with the same detector (idempotent; skips tagged; failures left untagged).
  Smoke run (16) verified; four samples visually confirmed (tops show QR,
  bottoms show label back). Full run over 4,695 photos launched.

2026-07-08 — master (working tree) — R04 reagent "not running" = calibrator log-flood at particle-ID read; robust-ID fix + R04 deploy script
================================================================================
Operator report: reagent test fill on R04 "not running". Diagnosis from the robot's own run record
(run da4543fa, 2026-07-08 20:22Z): R04 ALREADY had the fixed protocol w/ use_tip_calibration=false
(Alejandro uploaded 20:17Z, protocol 23786f24) — the run was stuck in the retry-pause loop at the
particle-ID read. Root cause: R04's calibrator floods its USB serial with Particle system-log lines
("0014455145 [system] ERRO/WARN/INFO ..."); the protocol's single readline() after 'I' returns a log
line instead of the deck ID → "Unknown particle ID" pause. Operator pressed Resume 7x over 3 min
before a clean line landed; run then filled normally (deck 001, tip-cal probe skipped).
FIX (protocols/Reagent_Filling_GEN7.py):
- _read_particle_id(): drain input buffer, send 'I', scan EVERY line arriving in a 2.5s window for a
  KNOWN carriage ID (substring match against the carriages dict), 4 internal attempts before the
  operator-facing pause. Survives log-line interleaving.
- When use_tip_calibration is false, skip the 'C' offset-baseline serial read entirely (its result
  only feeds the per-tip X/Y probe, which is already skipped) — removes that pause path too.
FILES: protocols/Reagent_Filling_GEN7.py; scripts/deploy-reagent-to-r04.cjs (new; mirror of the B14
deploy script — Mongo-bundled labware, analysis gate on use_tip_calibration, repoints R04 robotId
CCyX8FjTRGvYOd9vISGvi reagent entry). Deploy to R04 pending the live run finishing (won't analyze on
the Pi mid-fill); also refresh stored opentrons_protocols op-9a81e595ea21a04b fileContent so UI
re-uploads/deck-cal sync don't revert the robust-ID fix. npm run check: n/a (protocol .py + one-off
.cjs script only; no src/ changes).
DEPLOYED 2026-07-08 ~20:45Z (run da4543fa was stopped by operator 20:32Z, robot free): R04 reagent
slot repointed 23786f24... -> 6eeb16e4-bba6-44cf-b9cb-72f494fe42d7 (analysis completed, 27 params,
use_tip_calibration present default=false). Stored opentrons_protocols op-9a81e595ea21a04b
fileContent refreshed to the robust-ID repo version (60.5KB -> 61.8KB) — UI re-upload/deck-cal sync
no longer reverts. B07/B14 untouched (pre-robust-ID version still on them; redeploy via their
scripts if the log-flood symptom appears there).

2026-07-08 — master (working tree) — ROOT CAUSE: B14 deck-004 "Studio-perfect but reagent fill misses" = RobotDeckOffset (-0.1, -2.4, 0) applied at fill time but never in the Studio
================================================================================
Operator calibrated deck-004 on B14 via the Deck Calibration Studio (723 edits on 7/08; Move-to-hole
and Fill-motion both aligned), but real reagent fills missed holes entirely (bent tip), with different
error between two back-to-back tests. Diagnosed from the robot's own run records (runs 2e33e8ea 20:50Z
+ bcaacf69 21:22Z, protocol 927e2325 uploaded 20:41Z) + Mongo:
- BOTH runs ran bims_native=True with offset_x=-0.1, offset_y=-2.4, offset_z=0 — the RobotDeckOffset
  row for B14 (rdo-156341190168018b), captured by alejandro 2026-07-01 20:58Z (overwriting the 0,0,0
  PRD-6 seed; the row's note text still says "seeded ... 0,0,0" — stale). The .py applies this to ALL
  labware (deck carriage + tube rack + TIP RACK).
- The Studio's Move-to-hole / Fill-motion / pick-up-tip NEVER apply RobotDeckOffset (verified in
  deck-calibration/+page.svelte — currentOffset is display/save only; moveToWellSafe/fillMoveAbs use
  pure Mongo geometry). So a deck tuned to look perfect in the Studio fills ~2.4mm off in -Y. WYSIWYG
  breaks whenever the selected robot has a nonzero global offset.
- Run-to-run variance explained: the offset also shifts TIP PICKUP at slot 11 by -2.4mm Y → tips seat
  crooked/bend (operator saw a bent tip) → variable extra error per pickup. use_tip_calibration=False
  in both runs (per-tip probe skipped), so that layer was NOT the variance source.
- Secondary: both runs used the 20:41Z protocol snapshot; Studio edits at 21:18+ (incl. two 288-well
  z batches at 21:32) were not on the robot until the 21:37Z sync (adbf11b3, now in robot.protocols[]).
- Both runs targeted identically (same moveToWell stream) — robot-side math did not drift between tests.
Fix options (not yet applied): (1) zero B14's RobotDeckOffset since deck-004 geometry is now tuned
on-B14 to physical truth (any deck whose tuning compensated for -2.4 would shift back, check per deck);
(2) code fix: Studio should apply (or at minimum loudly warn about) the robot's global offset in
Move-to-hole/Fill-motion/tip-pickup so Studio == fill. Diagnosis only this entry; no code changed.

2026-07-09 — master (working tree) — Deck-cal: "Align selection → anchor hole" (straighten every line in a cartridge)
================================================================================
PRD docs/prds/DECK-CAL-ALIGN-CARTRIDGE-TO-HOLE.md. Operator-reported defect: some fill
lines within a cartridge are shifted slightly vs the others. New Studio tool: jog-verify
ONE good hole, box-select the whole cartridge, click the good hole LAST (it becomes the
anchor), one button snaps every other selected hole of the SAME role onto a clean grid
ruled by the anchor's row + line: new x = x(anchorRow, thisCol) (every column line
perfectly straight), new y = y(thisRow, anchorCol) (every row level). Z untouched.
Opposite-role holes are skipped + reported (wax/reagent rows are staggered ~one row
pitch by design — re-run with an anchor of the other type). Semantics ("straighten every
line", not cross-only or rigid-per-line) confirmed with the operator.
- apply-edit.ts: new applyDeckEditsPerWell() — per-well deltas in ONE Mongo write, with
  the same bounds guard, per-well DeckCalibrationEdit history (each row carries its own
  delta), one summary AuditLog (mode:'per-well'), best-effort local labware-JSON mirror.
- deck-calibration/+page.server.ts: new applyPerWell action (manufacturing:write),
  edits = JSON [{wellName,dx,dy,dz}].
- deck-calibration/+page.svelte: rowOf() helper; applyPerWellEdits() client path;
  undoStack entries extended with optional per-well edits (undoLast inverts each — one
  Undo reverts the whole alignment); alignSelectionToAnchor(); new cyan "Align selection
  → anchor hole" panel between Set-position and Robot-global-offset.

2026-07-09 — feat/train-timeout-fix — training 504 fixed (embedding cache + time budget)
================================================================================
User's first real training run 504'd. Root cause: /api/cv/train had no
maxDuration (short serverless default) while triggerTraining sequentially
fetched+embedded every cache-miss labeled image from R2 (~200 images = minutes;
each failed attempt persisted some embeddings, so retries crept forward).
Fixes:
1. Pre-warmed the cache operationally: scripts/backfill-embeddings.ts (new,
   idempotent, --labeled / --limit flags) — ran over ALL prod photos; every
   image now carries a cv-color-spatial-v1 embedding, so training is fit-only
   (seconds).
2. /api/cv/train: export config maxDuration 300 — a cold cache can't 504.
3. triggerTraining: cache-miss embedding now runs at bounded concurrency (6)
   instead of sequentially.
4. /api/cv/capture: embeds the photo at capture time (buffer already in
   memory, ~150ms, best-effort never blocks) and stores it on the CvImage —
   new photos always arrive pre-embedded.
npm run check: 10 errors / 434 warnings — baseline, zero in touched files.

================================================================================
2026-07-09 — feat/train-timeout-fix — defects cast error on FAIL verdicts fixed
================================================================================
First FAIL verdicts from the freshly trained wax v1 model failed to persist:
"Cast to [string] failed ... at path defects.0". Root cause: cv-inspection.ts
declared defects: [{ type: String, ... }] — bare `type` makes Mongoose read
the subdoc as "array of String". Wrapped it ({ type: { type: String } }).
Two failed wax_filled inspections in prod carry this error; re-photograph
those carts after deploy. npm run check: 10 errors — baseline.

Deployment: production — https://vercel.com/alejandro-valdezs-projects/bioscale-operations-system-mongodb/2ugtJ4R6dKLcqM8Mwqv4Xk9DL7Ld
Source: branch master @ commit 0661da69b (merge of PR #23, feat/train-timeout-fix)
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout master at commit 0661da69b
What this deployment is for: training 504 fix (capture-time embedding, concurrent cache-miss
embedding, maxDuration 300 on /api/cv/train, backfill-embeddings script — cache already
warmed for all ~4.7k prod photos) + defects.0 cast-error fix so FAIL verdicts persist.

state persists in localStorage (cv-projects-guide-collapsed), same pattern as
the stream guide. Pure .svelte change, no server load changes.

Fix 2026-07-22: thermocouple parser rewritten (new parse-thermo.ts) — the logger export
("温度 datasheet" title row, then Date | CH4 | ℃) defeated header detection; single-cell
title row triggered the single-column fallback and column A's Excel date serials (~46,000,
seen on the SPU 212 upload preview) parsed as temperatures. Old prod sessions
THERMO-000005/6 show the sibling bug (row indexes 1..493 as temps). Parser now considers
ONLY columns A-C, classifies by content (units/dates/serials/indexes/plausible temps) with
header hints as tiebreakers, handles "HH:MM:SS YYYY-MM-DD" stamps, shows which columns it
used. Verified vs real RT_data export (1,914 readings 23.7-45.3C) + 5 variants. Server-side
guard: uploads with >20% of values outside -100..1000C are rejected, not recorded.

2026-07-20 — feat/optical-confirmation-analysis-profile — per-channel F7/F3 analysis in optical-confirmation log + spectrophotometer tab removed
================================================================================
VALIDATION-04. Surfaces the research app's "Single Scan Cortisol" analysis profile
(per-well A/B/C F7/F3 ratio) on cartridges run through Manufacturing > Validation >
Optical Confirmation. The assay run is unchanged — "Gen 5 Optical Scan" (A9EB41AD)
loaded onto the cartridge as before; "cortisol" is only the NAME of the analysis
profile that interprets the resulting optical data.

Data path: brevitest-cloud writes raw fluorometer readings onto
cartridge_records.rawData.readings[] (channel A/B/C, f1..f8, clear, nir) after a run.
test_results collection is empty in prod — NOT used. We DERIVE the analysis on read;
nothing is written to the DB (fully non-destructive, and it sidesteps the strict
Mongoose schema which doesn't declare rawData/analysis).

Formula (ported verbatim from research analysis.ts, single-scan case): per channel,
sum f3/f5/f7 over the whole run, then F7/F3 = Σf7/Σf3 (ratio of SUMS, not means;
guard Σf3=0 → null), also F5/F3. Displayed to 1 decimal in the log, 2 in the detail
table — matching the research experiment view. Single-scan means NO scan-group
detection is needed (one group = whole run == research single-scan profile [0]).

Files:
- src/lib/server/optical-analysis.ts — pure computeOpticalAnalysis(readings) + the
  OpticalAnalysis/OpticalChannelAnalysis contract. + optical-analysis.test.ts (vitest,
  4 cases: ratio-of-sums, Σf3=0→null, empty→null, non-numeric skip) — PASS.
- optical-confirmation/+page.server.ts — derive analysis per cartridge on read.
- optical-confirmation/+page.svelte — new "F7/F3 (A/B/C)" column (A: x.x B: x.x
  C: x.x), barcode now links to the detail view.
- optical-confirmation/[id]/+page.server.ts + +page.svelte — per-cartridge detail
  view (per-channel table Well|F3 sum|F7 sum|F5 sum|F7/F3|F5/F3|n + collapsible raw
  JsonTree), ported from feat/optical-cartridge-data-view and refactored onto the
  shared module. + src/lib/components/JsonTree.svelte.
- Removed the Spectrophotometer tab: nav item + getSectionName branch in
  validation/+layout.svelte, the whole routes/validation/spectrophotometer subtree,
  and the two orphaned SpectrophotometerCapture/Result components (safety-checked:
  no external importers). Default /validation redirect goes to magnetometer, so
  nothing breaks.

46 errors / 430 warnings — all pre-existing master baseline, ZERO in touched files.

2026-07-27 — feat/optical-confirmation-analysis-profile — optical analysis TOOL v2: critical values + flagging + multi-cartridge group analysis
================================================================================
Turns the v1 F7/F3 log column into an analysis tool an engineer uses to judge a
run. Data path CONFIRMED live: cartridge_records.rawData.readings[] is populated on
completed Gen 5 Optical Scan carts (probe: 4 of 19 optical carts have readings,
A/B/C=42 each, baselineScans=0 -> single scan group; test_results empty). Derive-on
-read, never writes the DB.

Stats (per user): computed over an ENDPOINT WINDOW = last windowK=10 readings per
channel (readings are a kinetic curve, so whole-run SD measures the reaction not
precision). Per channel we show F7/F3 (mean of per-reading f7/f3), MODE, SD, and the
in-range band = MEAN +/- 1sigma (user picked mean-driven over mode-driven; mode is
shown but doesn't drive the band), plus F3 avg, F7 avg, ratio CV. Flags (research
parity): per-channel ratio CV>15%, per-reading |z|>2sigma, cross-well CV>15% (names
rogue channel). GROUP (net-new): select N cartridges -> per-channel across-cartridge
mean/mode/SD/band + flag any cartridge outside the group's mean+/-1sigma; cross-
cartridge CV>15% note.

Verified end-to-end on real prod data via tsx: single-cart bands tight (~0.5 ratio,
SD ~0.01) with z-outlier flags firing; group correctly flags the ratio~28 cart as the
outlier among ~0.5/~9/~28 carts.

  {ratioByChannel,warning,crossWellCv}.

  JsonTree.

  per-channel group stats table + per-cartridge table w/ outlier highlighting.

typecheck). npm run check: 46 errors / 430 warnings — all pre-existing master

================================================================================
2026-07-30 — feat/optical-group-comparison — optical confirmation: compare groups
of SPUs against each other, robust median/MAD outliers
================================================================================
Extends optical-confirmation validation from "analyze one ad-hoc list of
cartridges" to "compare named groups against each other, get a dataset, and see
outliers within a group". Built on deploy/optical-analysis-on-master, NOT master
— the v2 engine, the [id] detail page and the analyze/ route exist only there.

Two bugs found first, both of which blocked or invalidated the feature:
  * --color-tron-amber is not defined anywhere in layout.css. analyze/+page.svelte
    styled its outlier row tint and warning glyph on it, so the outlier
    highlighting on that page had NEVER actually rendered. Switched to Tailwind's
    built-in amber-400/500, matching what the [id] page already does.
  * The Ask BIMS widget is pinned bottom-LEFT at z-index 40 and mounted globally,
    so it sat on top of the first column of every full-bleed table — which in BIMS
    is the checkbox column. It had already blocked selecting an 8th row in this
    very log. Moved to bottom-right; mobile bottom-sheet unchanged.

Probed prod read-only before building. spus.opticalCalibration is real: 17 of 68
SPUs, shaped channels.{A,B,C}.{rawF3,factor} plus setName/setDate/cartridgeId —
the timestamp field is setDate, not calibratedAt. Both cartridge-to-SPU joins hit
32/32 (device.id to particleLink.particleDeviceId, device.name to udi). Readings
are exactly 126 per cartridge across 193 cartridges. cartridge_groups had 13 docs,
none with cartridgeIds or purpose, no duplicate names.

Engine (src/lib/server/optical-analysis.ts):
  * robustStats/robustZ — median, MAD, 1.4826-scaled MAD, IQR, robust CV, and a
    median +/- k*scale band. MAD=0 is common with clustered replicates and would
    give +/-Infinity for every non-median point (JSON.stringify(Infinity) is null,
    so the UI would blank silently) — hence a REPORTED fallback chain
    mad -> iqr -> sd -> none, surfaced in the UI whenever it is not mad.
  * ChannelAnalysis gained ratioSeries, the per-reading F7/F3 dataset, which
    previously never escaped analyzeCartridge.
  * analyzeGroupRobust + compareGroups. Outliers flag at 3.5 robust SDs from the
    group median instead of mean +/- 1 SD; groups under minGroupN=5 are not
    flagged at all. Between-group output is deliberately descriptive: median
    difference, percent difference, band overlap, separation ratio.
  * NO p-values / t-test / ANOVA. At n~7 there is no power, so a non-significant p
    reads to an operator as "these SPUs agree" — absence of evidence reported as
    evidence of absence. The repo tTest is also pooled-variance (equal-variance,
    violated the moment a group holds an outlier) and its p-values route through
    an incompleteBeta that its own source labels not production-grade.
  * compareGroups emits the raw-calibration caveat from the ENGINE whenever groups
    span more than one SPU, so the comparison cannot be rendered without it.
  * analyzeGroup deleted (deprecated one commit earlier, nothing calls it).

CORRECTION to the 2026-07-27 entry in this file: mean +/- 1 SD did NOT fail to
catch that 8.85 outlier — the band was [-1.39, 4.88], so it WAS flagged. What
mean/SD got wrong was the SPREAD statistic, 180-205% CV, driven entirely by that
one member. Robust CV on the same vector is 7.7%. Do not repeat the "masking"
framing; someone will check the arithmetic.

Persistence: membership cannot live on OpticalTestCartridge.groupId — only
BIMS-assigned cartridges get such a doc, and the log deliberately also lists ~93
COMPARATOR cartridges matched by assayId alone. It lives on
CartridgeGroup.cartridgeIds with a purpose discriminator (assign_batch vs
optical_analysis). Five audited actions, soft delete only, one group per cartridge
(double-counting would corrupt between-group medians), 409 on a name collision
rather than the silent merge the assign endpoint has been doing. No unique index
on name — the assign endpoint has always used a bare findOne by name. Reads use
a ?? [] fallback because .lean() does not apply schema defaults. cartridge_records
is never written.

UI: "Save as group" on the log next to "Analyze selected"; a Group column whose
pill selects that group's rows; a chips row to pick what to compare plus a
separate Show filter. The comparison view extends analyze/ (single group = the
N=1 case) with per-well tables, a hand-rolled SVG strip plot (no new dependency:
chart.js is canvas and kanban-only, uplot is a dead dep and the wrong shape),
long-format CSV export with proper quoting AND formula-injection defusing, an
"Excluded from statistics" card naming every cartridge that contributed nothing
and why, and a MethodLegend stating the rule in plain language. The band is
labelled "Expected range", never "In-range"; deltas are "difference", never
"bias".

Also fixed a latent bug the new filter would have introduced: toggleAll and
allSelected iterated data.cartridges, so with a filter active "select all" would
have checked hidden rows. And analyzeSelected used window.location.href, forcing
a full page reload on every analyze.

Verified against prod via tsx (script deleted after): 193 cartridges over 32 SPUs,
grouped one group per SPU. compareGroups flagged cartridge
026b84f1-83c2-4b60-9846-d8696df73a0e at F7/F3 28.61 vs a group median of 9.45
(13.3 robust SDs) with a readable reason, found two low outliers on well B of
another SPU, produced between-group separation ratios, emitted the multi-SPU
caveat, and round-tripped through JSON with no non-finite values.

Caveat worth carrying forward: cartridges sharing an SPU are NOT replicates —
they are different runs over time — so a whole-history SPU group legitimately
shows robust CV over 100%. That is exactly why groups are hand-curated rather
than auto-faceted by SPU. Also, this does NOT resolve the parity check this file
paused on: until BIMS vs research-app F7/F3 is confirmed on the same barcodes,
these flags are review signals, never a pass/fail release gate. The UI says so.

Tests: the existing optical test file was run by NO npm script (test:contracts
only includes tests/contracts/** and logs into a running app), so it was
decoration. Added test:unit plus tests/vitest.unit.config.ts with no DB or server
dependency. 22 tests passing, including a side-by-side assertion that on a clean
7-cartridge group the old rule flags 2 and the new rule flags 0.

npm run check: 46 errors / 430 warnings — unchanged from the master baseline,
zero in touched files.

Files: src/lib/server/optical-analysis.ts, optical-analysis.test.ts,
optical-constants.ts (new), src/lib/server/db/models/cartridge-group.ts,
src/lib/components/validation/optical/GroupPill.svelte, OutlierMark.svelte,
CalibrationPill.svelte, MethodLegend.svelte, GroupStripPlot.svelte, csv.ts (all
new), src/routes/validation/optical-confirmation/+page.server.ts and +page.svelte,
.../analyze/+page.server.ts and +page.svelte,
src/routes/api/validation/optical-confirmation/assign/+server.ts,
src/lib/components/ask-bims/AskBimsWidget.svelte, package.json,
tests/vitest.unit.config.ts (new)

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-feat-op-9547c3-brevitest.vercel.app
  (immutable alias: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-36gohpeb3-brevitest.vercel.app)

2026-07-09 — data fix + diagnosis — deck-001 (R04) "anchored A-row" + wax z inconsistency
================================================================================
ANCHORED ROW: operator's cartridge-8 batch (-30.5,-11.5,+7) moved rows B/C but row A was
rejected by the physical-bounds guard — target y = 11.499-11.5 = -0.001, one micron below
the y>=0 limit (guard exists because a negative coord makes the robot reject the whole def —
June deck-003 bug). The apply message did report "4 rejected (out of bounds)" but it's easy
to miss. FIXED: A2/A4/A6/A8 given the same shift with y clamped to 0 → (51.128/57.128/
63.128/69.128, 0, 39.45), consistent 6mm col pitch + 9mm row pitch vs B(y 8.999)/C(17.999).
Z INCONSISTENCY (diagnosed, not yet fixed): deck-001 left-bank wax z is now 3.40 (VWX/STU/
MNO/JKL/GHI), 4.40 (PQR), 7.45 (DEF), 39.45 (ABC) after two days of block-wise z batches
(+5/-4.5/+21/-30/+25/+7...). Operator's own reference points: DEF physically correct at
~13.2 (stored 7.45, Δ5.75), JKL previously good at 9.15 (stored 3.40, Δ5.75) — i.e. original
uniform wax z (~7.4) sat ~5.75mm below physical truth, and per-block +z chasing created the
"inconsistent scale between runs". Compounding: the Studio jog session's slotOrigin.z is
derived from the FIRST hole moved-to, so with inconsistent stored z the session's z frame
shifts depending on which hole you start from — the moving-target feeling. Proposed fix
(pending operator choice): reset ALL deck-001 wax z to one uniform value (13.2 = the jog-
verified physical height, or 7.45 baseline + one global wax-only dz), then never per-block z.
Robot note: R04 still runs the 7/08 20:33Z protocol bundle — NONE of today's deck-001 edits
are live for fills until a Sync/redeploy.

alphabetical default is how this mixup happened.

================================================================================
2026-07-09 — bugfix (code) — deck-cal Studio "Pick up tip" LocationIsOccupiedError self-heals
================================================================================
Operator hit "loadLabware failed: [LocationIsOccupiedError] Labware cosmas_and_damian_biotix_
96_200ul_tiprack is already present at slotName=SLOT_11" when picking up a wax tip. Root cause:
the Studio keeps ONE maintenance run across steps; calibrating the reagent (biotix) rack loaded
it at slot 11, then a wax pickup needs the 20uL rack in the same physical slot. loadLabwareInRun
was idempotent only for the SAME loadName (reuses the id) but fell through to loadLabware for a
DIFFERENT one → LocationIsOccupiedError. OT-2 has no gripper and moveLabware/offDeck PAUSES the
run (confirmed live: manualMoveWithPause hung 25s + left the run 'paused', and a paused
maintenance run blocks new runs + resists DELETE — had to restart R04's robot-server to clear;
that dead-end is why the pause approach was rejected).
FIX: (1) maintenance.ts — new typed SlotOccupiedError; loadLabwareInRun throws it when a
different labware occupies the slot. (2) pick-up-tip endpoint — catches it, returns 409
{code:'SLOT_OCCUPIED'}. (3) deck-calibration/+page.svelte — pickUpTipAction (new doPickUp helper)
sees the 409, transparently reopens the run (stopMaintenance→startMaintenance, fresh empty deck)
and retries ONCE (allowRecover guard prevents loops). Operator now just clicks Pick up tip; it
self-heals instead of erroring.
VERIFIED against real R04 (scratchpad/verify-fix.cjs mirroring the server calls): with biotix at
slot 11, guard fires SLOT_OCCUPIED; after reopen slot 11 is empty and the wax rack loads (labware
id returned). npm run check: 11-error baseline, 0 new in the 3 touched files. NOT committed.

2026-07-10 (cont.) — R04 deck-001: cornerOffsetFromSlot frame re-baseline (+6mm y headroom) — permanent front-row fix
================================================================================
Root cause of the recurring "front row won't adjust further / scrambles when shifted" (identical to
B07): deck-001's front-row holes sit at the deck's y~0 coordinate edge — reagent front row A17 at
y=0.80mm, only 0.8mm of headroom. The OT-2 rejects any well at a negative coordinate, so a forward
cartridge shift can't move the front row and the physical-bounds guard rejects/mangles it (row A
gets left behind — the +31x/+3y scramble).
FIX (permanent, net-neutral): re-baselined the labware frame — cornerOffsetFromSlot.y 0 -> -6 and
+6 added to every well.y. Robot applies the corner offset to all wells in BOTH the Studio's
moveToWell-derived origin and the fill's carriage[well] lookup, so physical positions are unchanged
(verified: A17 phys stays 0.80). Front rows now have 6mm of forward headroom before hitting y=0.
Range check: new well-y 6.80..262.62, well within yDimension 276.4. Reversible (audit_log
deck_frame_rebaseline, margin=6). Validated on B07 that negative cornerOffsetFromSlot passes robot
analysis clean. Applied to Mongo only; NOT yet deployed — operator to Studio-verify net-neutrality
(a back-row hole must land unchanged; front row must now jog forward) before wax redeploy.
Side effect: every y value shown in the Studio for deck-001 reads +6 higher; physical unchanged.

--- REVERTED (same day): operator asked not to re-baseline the whole deck, only the last row.
cornerOffsetFromSlot back to 0,0,0; all wells -6. Deck-001 unchanged from 6f24a53b geometry.
Note: cornerOffsetFromSlot is a deck-wide property — there is no per-row frame offset, and the
robot rejects negative WELL coords, so a single row cannot gain forward-of-edge headroom on its
own. Awaiting operator's target physical position for the front row.

================================================================================
2026-07-22 — master — Wax R04: 4 wells per aspiration + real cartridge-wall clearance
================================================================================
Operator raised the wax gate volumes (gates 4/3/2 -> 2.2uL, gate 1 stays 1.6uL) and R04 started
filling in ragged 2-then-3-well groups, then bent/broke the tip crossing from the last well of one
cartridge to the first well of the next — and skipped that first well.

ROOT CAUSE (one cause, two symptoms). dispense_reagent() sizes each aspiration purely by volume:
available = maximum_volume_per_aspiration(20) - aspirate_remainder(13.5) = 6.5uL usable per trip.
  - At the OLD uniform 1.6uL, 4 x 1.6 = 6.4 <= 6.5, so a batch was ALWAYS exactly 4 wells, which is
    exactly one cartridge row (cols 2,4,6,8 = gates 4,3,2,1). Every row/cartridge crossing therefore
    happened on the trip back to the wax tube in slot 10 — a cross-labware move the API arcs
    deck-high. Nothing ever traversed the carriage at low height. That alignment was luck, not design.
  - At 2.2/2.2/2.2/1.6 the batches become 2,3,3,2,3... and no longer land on row boundaries. Batches
    now OPEN mid-row, so the pipette traverses row-to-row (20.1mm) and cartridge-to-cartridge
    (22.5mm+) on the API's default in-labware arc of well-top +5mm — not enough to clear the raised
    wall between cartridge bodies. Simulated over a full deck: 36 low crossings, 12 of them
    cartridge-wall crossings. The V8 -> U2 crossing in batch 5 is exactly the break the operator saw.
The protocol HAS a wall-clearance lift (move to well.top(60), settle, drop to well.top(5)) but it was
gated on `jump_count % jump_frequency == 0` with jump_frequency = 288. There are 288 wax wells on a
deck, so it fired once at the very first dispense and never again — the guard was dead code. (The
reagent protocol does this lift before EVERY well, which is why reagent never had this failure.)

FIX (protocols/Wax_Filling_GEN7_Cartridge.py):
 1. Batch builder now also breaks at a cartridge-row boundary, so an aspiration never spans two rows.
    Every row/cartridge crossing is back on the deck-high source-tube trip, as in the 1.6uL era.
 2. jump gate replaced with a real predicate: lift only for a crossing WITHIN a batch (idx > 0 and
    row_key changed) plus the first dispense of the run. idx == 0 arrives from the source tube and is
    already arced high, so this costs no extra time in the normal case, and saves the tip in the
    degraded case where volumes are too big to fit a row in one trip.
 3. New RTP `aspirate_remainder` (default 11.5uL, was hardcoded 13.5). This is the only knob that
    sets wells-per-trip: usable = 20 - remainder, and a 2.2/2.2/2.2/1.6 row needs 8.2uL. 11.5 gives
    8.5uL usable -> a full 4-well row per aspiration, aspirating 19.7uL against the p20's 20uL max.
    NOTE: this is a liquid-handling constant that has NOT been bench-validated at 11.5 — it is an RTP
    precisely so it can be put back toward 13.5 at the bench without a redeploy. Safety does not
    depend on it: at 13.5 the run is still crossing-free, just 108 trips instead of 72.
 4. New helpers get_well_row()/row_key() keyed to the real carriage geometry (24 cartridges in a
    3x8 grid; x-blocks cols 1-8 / 9-16 / 17-24; three rows per cartridge in full_rows_reverse order).
    Verified against gen4deck_gen7cartridge_001.json: within-row steps are all exactly 6.0mm,
    row->row 20.1mm, cartridge->cartridge 22.5mm+. The keying lands on the real physical gaps.
 5. Run log now prints the aspirate budget (pipette max - remainder = usable, vs the row total) and
    warns when a row won't fit, so a short-filling run is diagnosable from the log; the previously
    silent "no well fits" break now says why it stopped.

VERIFICATION: opentrons_simulate analysis completes clean (RTP accepted, within Opentrons' name and
description caps). Batching simulated against the real labware JSON across 5 gate-volume configs:
intra-batch crossings drop from 36 (12 cartridge-wall) to 0 for the operator's config, and stay 0 for
every other config tried. Trips per deck return to 72 — the same as the old 1.6uL config, i.e. the
higher volumes now cost nothing in wax-tube trips. Motion itself is NOT robot-verified; wants a
dry-run (no cartridges, or tip-only) on R04 before a production deck.

DEPLOYMENT LINEAGE — READ BEFORE SHIPPING THIS. Queried R04 (172.16.28.144) live while chasing this.
The wax protocol R04 is actually running (opentronsProtocolId 85e3d4b4-661c-46d8-a17d-600aca7b1bd7,

sibling, bundling the BIMS Mongo `labware_definitions`. On R04 that rule is BACKWARDS and would have

                               — BIMS Mongo copy:   x=51.054 y=253.221 z=2.800
That is ~31mm in x and 4.6mm in z, on EVERY well of EVERY carriage (max deltas: 001 36.6mm,
002 24.7mm, 003 33.2mm, 004 31.4mm), plus 6.6mm on the tiprack. R04 runs deck 1 (operator confirmed),

retired 005/006 while this lineage still loads 005, which surfaced first as a FileNotFoundError.
The first verify upload (4cac98f8) carried the Mongo defs and was deleted from the robot.
Rewrote the script to bundle the Opentrons app labware folder — the same source that produced the
working analysis — and added a hard gate that PROVES it: it pulls the labware definitions back out of
the robot's analysis of the protocol R04 actually ran (from /runs history, NOT the Mongo entry) and
out of the fresh upload, and aborts + deletes the upload if any well moved more than 0.001mm.
Also gates: analysis completed with zero errors; exactly 11 RTPs; dispense_depth and bims_native ABSENT.

ROOT CAUSE (proven from data, not tip length): the 2026-07-28 BIMS-native wax uploads

R04 BIMS cutover) bundle the Mongo labware_definitions. In those defs the EVEN-column

that's the clean control. Run eb492b3f (19:45, bims_native=true, carriage 001) dispensed
X2/X4 at the corrupt coordinates → tip lands ~25mm off the wax hole and ~4.5mm below the
believed rim → snap. The operator's "4mm" observation matches the dz corruption, which is
why it presented as a tip-length problem. The ODD-column (reagent) wells are legitimately
Studio-taught (small dz) and in active use — NOT touched.

Note on tip length itself: cosmasanddamian_96_tiprack_20ul declares tipLength 45.66 (= well
depth, no tipOverlap) which almost certainly overstates the physical filter tip — the
zCalWax/zCalReagent gap (34.491 vs 40.8 = 6.31mm at one physical switch) says as much. But
that error is a self-consistent frame convention baked into EVERY taught wax value (app deck
defs, z_cal, probe constants); it cancels teach-vs-fill. "Fixing" tipLength alone would push
every wax motion ~4-6mm DEEPER and break more tips. Left at 45.66 deliberately; a correction
requires a coordinated re-teach of all wax-frame data (documented in memory).

2026-07-29 (later) — master — CORRECTION: B14 reagent reverted to raw frame; tip-cal unusable on B14 until probe system is fixed
================================================================================
The tip-zero shift from earlier today was wrong to apply — a fill immediately after it still
missed. Audit-trail archaeology (audit_log: deck_calibration_edit_batch / calibrate_tip /
deck_calibration_sync) explains why: since 07-27, carriage 003's reagent wells have been through
repeated ±6mm re-teaches — sometimes with the Studio tip-cal adjust active, sometimes not —
flipping regions between tip-zeroed and tip-neutral frames across sessions (e.g. +6/+1.5 batches
07-27T15:30, +6.2/+3.2 on all 288 wells 07-27T21:57, then A-row and per-region tweaks on 07-28/29).
The frame of any given region is therefore not reconstructable numerically, and blanket shifts
(mine included) just continue the ping-pong.
Decisive finding from the probe data: the STUDIO bridge probe and the IN-RUN protocol probe
disagree systematically (~-6.4 vs ~-5.5 in x, ~0.8mm apart) and overall probe scatter spans
~1.5mm x / ~2.5mm y — against 1.8mm-diameter reagent holes. Tip calibration on B14 currently
ADDS more error than the per-tip bend it corrects. It cannot be made reliable by re-teaching.
ACTION: restored 003's odd wells exactly from
backups/gen4deck_003-backup-2026-07-29-pre-tipzero-shift.json (the state the operator raw-verified
in the Studio: "tip went into the hole exactly"); redeploy deduped to the SAME upload d6709dea the
operator verified against; B14 reagent repointed back to it. Reagent fills on B14 must run with
use_tip_calibration=OFF (the default) until (a) the calibrator device baseline is re-zeroed on
B14 and (b) the bridge-vs-protocol probe discrepancy is root-caused and fixed.

2026-07-29 (final) — master — B14 reagent: root cause was the Studio tipAdjust leak + bridge-vs-run probe mismatch; small counter-shift deployed, tip-cal ON restored
================================================================================
Full resolution of the B14 saga. The operator confirmed the pre-shift state was "Studio perfect,
fills slightly off (right/front)". Chain of causes:
1. The deck WAS correctly tip-zeroed (taught with Studio calibrate-tip, per design).
2. The operator's post-fill "raw" Studio verification was NOT raw: startMaintenance() resets
   hasTip but NOT tipAdjust (+page.svelte ~514 vs 537), so a session killed externally (fill run
   start) leaks the last adjust into the next connect. Their "hit exactly" check silently applied
   the 16:44 adjust — which misled this session into the wrong +5.9mm "tip-zero" shift (reverted).
3. The real residual: the bridge daemon probe and the in-protocol probe differ systematically —
   bridge mean adjust ≈ (-6.36, -1.35), in-run mean ≈ (-5.53, -1.68) → fills land ≈ (+0.8, -0.35)mm
   off (right/front) — confirmed by operator observation of the miss direction.
FIX: shifted 003's 288 odd/reagent wells by (-0.8, +0.35) to cancel the probe mismatch; deployed
+ repointed B14 reagent → protocol 13558e8d. Fills now run with use_tip_calibration=ON.
EXPECTED QUIRK: Studio checks WITH calibrate-tip now sit ~0.8mm off (opposite direction) — the def
is in the RUN-probe frame, not the bridge-probe frame. Do NOT "fix" that in the Studio; verify by
running a fill. Backups: gen4deck_003-backup-2026-07-29-pre-tipzero-shift.json (raw-verified state),
gen4deck_003-backup-2026-07-29-pre-probe-mismatch-correction.json (pre-final-correction).
TODO (real fixes): (a) startMaintenance() must clear tipAdjust/nominal on fresh connect — one-line
guard, page has unrelated uncommitted edits pending; (b) root-cause the 0.8mm bridge-vs-protocol
probe discrepancy (daemon carries the .py motion "verbatim" — it measurably is not equivalent);
(c) zero the calibrator devices on-robot fleet-wide.

2026-07-29 — master — B07 "calibrator fixture not found" = bend-reply parser, fixed + deployed
================================================================================
Studio Calibrate-tip on B07 failed with "calibrator fixture not found — no /dev/ttyACM device
answered the 'C' bend query (scanner skipped)". Diagnosis over SSH: calibrator (Particle Argon,
ttyACM1) present and answering — but B07's fixture prefixes its bend reply ("= -0.2:2.3" vs
R04/B14's bare "-4.5:0.8"), and _parse_bend's positional str(bytes)[2:] slicing choked on the
prefix -> daemon skipped the real calibrator. (Also explains B07's long "flaky calibrator"
reputation.) The device had ALSO been off USB 16:14->18:45 today (genuinely flaky cable), but was
back before the failing attempts. 'I' (particle id) replies are clean — only 'C' is prefixed.
FIX: _parse_bend now extracts x:y by regex (tolerant of prefixes; self-tested against both reply
styles). Patched the ROBOT'S deployed daemon lineage (which was NEWER than the repo copy — it
carries the 7/13 scanner-reenumeration + /maintenance_runs/current_run fixes that never landed on
master), deployed to B07 (service restarted clean 19:05), and synced that lineage + fix into
scripts/ot2-bridge.py so the repo is no longer behind the fleet.
STILL OPEN: (a) R04/B14 daemons run the old parser — fine for their bare replies; roll the patched
daemon fleet-wide at a quiet moment for consistency. (b) The FILL protocols parse 'C' with the same
fragile slicing — on B07, fill-time baseline reads will fail/pause ("Could not read offset
calibration") until the .py parsing gets the same tolerance or the device's "= " prefix is cleared.
(c) B07 calibrator USB drop 16:14-18:45 = physically flaky connection; reseat/replace cable.

================================================================================
2026-07-29 — master — Run-start deck-calibration FRESHNESS GATE + fill-protocol 'C' parser fix; B07 zero-baseline mystery solved
================================================================================
Operator: "make sure reagent fill always runs the newest deck calibration — sometimes it reverts
to old ones"; then two B07 reagent runs in a row differed with nothing changed.

STALENESS BUGS FOUND (the "reverts"):
1. A run executes the def SNAPSHOT bundled at protocol upload — Studio edits don't reach runs
   until a Sync re-upload.
2. The fill pages posted the protocol id chosen at page LOAD — a page opened before a Sync starts
   the older upload (still present on the robot) with its stale defs.
3. Studio Sync re-uploads the stored opentrons_protocols .py — which deploy scripts never updated,
   so Sync could silently DOWNGRADE the protocol lineage (stored wax .py was months old).

FIXES SHIPPED:
- NEW src/lib/server/opentrons/protocol-freshness.ts — ensureFreshRunProtocol(): at EVERY wax +
  reagent startRun, resolves the robot's current entry server-side (posted id no longer trusted),
  fetches that upload's own on-robot analysis, diffs every BIMS-managed labware def well (x/y/z)
  against live Mongo, and on ANY mismatch auto re-uploads the stored .py with live defs, repoints,
  re-verifies the fresh bundle, audit-logs (run_start_auto_resync). A run can no longer start on
  geometry it can't prove current. Wired into reagent-filling + wax-filling startRun.
- Stored opentrons_protocols .py refreshed to the DEPLOYED lineages (kills the sync-downgrade).
- Fill protocols' calibrator 'C' baseline parsing made regex-tolerant (same fix as the daemon):
  B07's "= -0.2:2.3" reply crashed the old slicing -> every B07 tip-cal run paused ("Could not
  read offset calibration") and continued with ZERO baseline -> per-tip adjust wrong by the whole
  baseline (2.3mm y vs 1.8mm holes) + fresh-tip probe scatter each run = the observed
  different-every-run misses (adjusts -1.0/-2.9, -0.7/-3.1, -0.8/-2.9 across 3 unchanged runs).

2026-07-29 — feat/arm-pi-connection — ARM-01: arm connection health + task runner
================================================================================
Wrote docs/prds/ARM-01-pi-connection-and-task-control.md and implemented stories
S1-S5, S7 and S8 against it. The PRD was grounded by probing the live arm-pi
rather than by reading the schema, which changed what the work turned out to be.

Key finding: the BIMS -> Pi path already worked. arm-pi is on the tailnet and
Tailscale Funnel is up, so https://arm-pi.tailf65a70.ts.net/health answers 200
from the public internet, auth is enforced (401 without x-api-key, 200 with),
and GET /tasks lists two registered tasks. So this was never a networking
problem: robot-arm-client.ts wrapped 17 Pi endpoints and none of them were the
task endpoints, so running a task was reachable by curl and unreachable from the
product. That gap is what S1/S5 close.

Three live misconfigurations were found on the Pi and are NOT fixed by this
commit (they are ops work, story S0, deliberately left for explicit approval
because restarting the service has a physical-motion consequence):
  1. Local .env pointed ROBOT_ARM_BASE_URL at arm-pi:8001, which is a stray
     hand-started uvicorn, not the systemd service on :8000 that the Funnel
     proxies. Two servers, one serial bus.
  2. The Pi's ~/robot-arm/.env says DRY_RUN=false and LEADER_PORT=/dev/ttyACM1,
     but the running service still reports DRY_RUN=true and the old
     /dev/buslinker-* paths — the .env was corrected and the service was never
     restarted. The only thing holding the arm in dry-run is an un-restarted
     process; a reboot arms live motion.
  3. Pi BIMS_WEBHOOK_URL points at a dev workstation's tailnet IP on a Vite
     port, so production BIMS receives no arm events at all.

What was built:
- robot-arm-client.ts: added preflight() (8s), listTasks(), startTask() (15s,
  matching the existing allowance for the slow calibration calls), plus
  ArmPreflight / ArmTask / ArmTaskStarted types.
- robot-arm-lock.ts (new): deriveArmHolder(). The "who holds the arm" lock is
  DERIVED, not stored — the arm is held iff a RobotArmRun exists in a
  non-terminal state, and the holder is that run's existing triggeredBy. No
  schema change, no new collection, no write path, covered by the existing
  {status, createdAt:-1} index. A stored currentOperator field would be a second
  source of truth that can drift; capture_stations.currentOperator is the
  cautionary tale in this repo (no expiry, cleared by hand). A derived lock
  cannot strand the arm, because the terminal webhook event that finalizes the
  run IS the release.
- robot-arm-run.ts: widened the type enum with 'task' (additive only). The
  webhook was already receiving task.* events and filing them as 'teleop' with a
  "treated as teleop for now" comment; it now infers 'task' correctly. Added to
  the runs/ filter whitelist.
- control/+page.server.ts: safePreflight()/safeTasks() folded into the existing
  Promise.all, plus a startTask action and a stop action that now audit-logs
  both holder and stopper.
- RobotArmConnectionPanel.svelte (new): renders the Pi's own preflight verdict
  and its `diagnosis` string verbatim rather than re-deriving the logic in
  TypeScript, so the two cannot drift. Four states (ok / degraded / unreachable
  / LIVE MOTION), with the configured base URL shown alongside failures.
- RobotArmControlBar.svelte (new): holder + elapsed time + an always-live Stop,
  sticky to the viewport bottom on phones. Stop is enabled regardless of who
  holds the arm — a stop that respects the lock is a stop that fails when you
  need it most — with a confirm when overriding someone else's run.
- Responsive pass on the control page: 44x44px touch targets, stacked layout
  under sm. The arm pages had 1 responsive utility class between all four.

Safety gate: the build is accepted against DRY_RUN only. startTask re-reads the
Pi's dry_run at submit time rather than trusting what the page rendered, since
the service could have been restarted since load, and refuses to start if it is
not true. The UI disables Start for the same reason.

npm run check: 10 errors / 434 warnings, zero in any ARM-01 file. (The 46-error
baseline recorded at ba2c37f8 has since drifted down; the remaining errors are
in r2.ts, AskBimsWidget and assembly/[sessionId], none touched here.)

2026-07-30 — master — Operator was right: Studio tip-cal probed at the WRONG HEIGHT (38.5 park vs the fill's 40.8) — fixed fleet-wide
================================================================================
Operator kept insisting the calibrator z difference between the Studio (38.5) and the reagent fill
(40.8) "has to cause some kind of offset". Initially dismissed (the endpoint sends zCal=40.8 in the
bridge payload) — WRONG: the daemon's ATTACHED-mode calibrate_tip ignored the payload z and probed
at the tip's CURRENT height ("z_probe = cur z", ot2-bridge.py ~1415). The Studio flow parks at the
fixture position z (38.5) before calibrating, so every Studio tip-cal pressed the switch levers
2.3mm below where every reagent fill presses them (wax teaching: ~4mm above the wax fill's 34.49).
Different lever contact height -> systematically different measured adjust -> a persistent
teach-frame vs fill-frame bias no deck re-teaching could remove (very likely the B14 ~0.8mm
bridge-vs-run residual empirically compensated on 07-29).
FIX: attached mode now keeps the current X/Y as reference but ALWAYS moves to and probes at the
payload z_cal for the mount (reagent 40.8 / wax 34.491) — Studio probe height == fill probe height
by construction. Deployed to all three robots (daemons restarted clean 15:34, all idle at deploy).
NOTE: existing taught decks keep their empirical compensations (captured against fill behavior, so
they stay valid); FUTURE Studio teaching now measures adjusts in the fill frame directly.
zCalReagent/zCalWax values themselves unchanged (40.8/34.491 remain the fill-proven heights); the
38.5 fixture position.z remains a park/jog height only.

2026-07-30 — fix/mag-test-run-time — magnetometer shows when the test RAN, not
when the data was pulled
================================================================================
"Fetch Results" on /validation/magnetometer reads the magnet_validation Particle
variable, which holds the output of a PREVIOUS run_test on the device. Both read
paths (the readFromDevice action and the auto-poll endpoint) stamped the session
with new Date() — the moment of the read — so a result measured weeks earlier was
recorded and displayed as if it had just been taken.

Not hypothetical. Across 431 magnetometer sessions in production, 153 are
mislabelled by more than an hour. The worst shows 2026-06-11 for a test that
actually ran 2025-10-06 — 248 days off. This is also the visible symptom of the
stale-variable failure mode behind the SPU-203 investigation: a frozen variable
looks like a fresh test precisely because BIMS dates it by the read.

Root cause and the data we already had: the device reports its own run time. The
payload's first line is the results filename the firmware wrote, e.g.
  /validation/magnet-1784661523.txt
and an older shape carries one too:
  #003<TAB>1710268200
The poll endpoint already regex'd that second form for its change-detection
counter and threw the timestamp away. Nothing ever read the filename form, which
is what 403 of 426 sessions actually use.

New shared parser src/lib/server/magnetometer-time.ts handles both shapes,
rejects an unset device clock (anything before 2020, which covers the 1970 and
2001 epoch defaults) and anything meaningfully in the future, only scans the
header so reading values can never be mistaken for an epoch, and returns null
rather than guessing.

Wiring:
  * readFromDevice + the poll endpoint store testRanAt on the session (additive
    field on ValidationSession) and return it. startedAt/completedAt keep their
    existing meaning — when BIMS recorded this — so no existing field is silently
    redefined. The SPU DHR validation.magnetometer block gets testRanAt alongside
    its untouched completedAt for the same reason.
  * The fetch results panel now leads with "Test run <time>", shows the read time
    beneath it, and warns when the results were already stale on arrival.
  * History and the [sessionId] detail page DERIVE the time from rawData whenever
    testRanAt is absent. rawData is already stored on every document, so all 413
    resolvable historical records display correctly with NO migration and without
    mutating a single stored record. The detail page previously showed two
    identical pull-time fields ("Started" and "Completed"); those become "Test
    run" and "Recorded in BIMS".
  * History CSV gains SPU and Test run columns.

18 legacy sessions carry no timestamp at all (their payload starts straight in at
"Channel A ... Channel B ... Channel C"). Those render as "unknown" with a tooltip
explaining why. Deliberately NOT backfilled with the read time — substituting the
read time is the exact bug being fixed, and a confidently wrong date is worse than
an honest gap.

Also fixed while in the file: the history page queried type 'magnetometer', but
every magnetometer session is written with type 'mag'. It was matching 5 stray
records out of 431, so that page has been showing almost nothing. Now matches
both values.

Verified against production with the real module via tsx (script deleted after):
431 sessions examined, 413 resolved a run time (403 via the filename form, 10 via
the counter form), 18 honestly unknown, 153 stale by more than an hour, and 0
implausible timestamps accepted.

Tests: 14 new unit tests — both formats, precedence when a payload has both, the
legacy no-timestamp payload, unset and future clocks, second vs millisecond
epochs, and a guard that epoch-looking numbers inside the readings table are never
mistaken for a timestamp. This branch is off master, which does not have the
test:unit script or config from the optical branch, so both were added here too.

npm run check: 76 errors / 434 warnings — identical to the clean master baseline
measured at this branch point (master has drifted up from the 46 recorded on
older branches), zero in touched files.

Files: src/lib/server/magnetometer-time.ts (new), magnetometer-time.test.ts (new),
src/lib/server/db/models/validation-session.ts,
src/routes/validation/magnetometer/+page.server.ts and +page.svelte,
.../history/+page.server.ts and +page.svelte,
.../[sessionId]/+page.server.ts and +page.svelte,
src/routes/api/validation/magnetometer/poll/+server.ts, package.json,
tests/vitest.unit.config.ts (new)

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-fix-mag-7db9fe-brevitest.vercel.app
  (immutable alias: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-lgwniznmg-brevitest.vercel.app)
Source: branch fix/mag-test-run-time @ commit 35d1d792
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout fix/mag-test-run-time at commit 35d1d792
  (or: git log fix/mag-test-run-time --oneline to find it if the branch has moved on)
What this deployment is for: confirming Fetch Results reports the device's own
  test time rather than the poll time, that stale reads are called out, and that
  magnetometer history is populated again.
2026-07-30 — feat/arm-pi-connection — Pi-side arm bring-up completed; preview rebuilt to pick up corrected ROBOT_ARM_BASE_URL
================================================================================
This deploy carries NO new BIMS code. Its only purpose was to produce a fresh preview
build that reads the corrected `ROBOT_ARM_BASE_URL` Vercel env var (env changes are not
applied to an existing build). Merged origin/master into the branch — 8f7a0171 (MCP
path-embedded key route) and bc3734d1 (ot2-bridge attached-mode tip-cal z fix). The only
conflict was progress.txt, where both sides had appended entries; resolved by keeping
both, verbatim.

Story S0 from the previous entry — the three Pi-side misconfigurations that were
deliberately held for explicit approval — is now DONE, with the operator at the bench.
Root causes, in the order they had to be peeled back:

1. STALE systemd ENVIRONMENT. The robot-arm service on arm-pi had been running since
   2026-07-22; .env had been corrected on 07-28 and 07-29. systemd reads
   `EnvironmentFile=` only at process start, so both fixes were silently discarded and
   the server kept insisting on ports .env no longer named. The three .env.bak-* files
   on the Pi are the fossil record of the same fix being applied twice and lost twice.
   Lesson: ALWAYS `systemctl restart robot-arm` after touching .env.

2. FOLLOWER BUSLINKER HAD BEEN SWAPPED. The board physically on arm-pi reports USB
   serial 5C4C126808, not the 5C4C128050 recorded in the PRD inventory, so udev never
   created /dev/buslinker-follower and the server reported "follower not present" even
   though it was plugged in. Updated `_KNOWN_ARM_SERIALS` (src/server/app.py) and
   deploy/pi/udev/99-buslinker.rules together, per the standing rule in the robot-arm
   CLAUDE.md. Stale 5C4C128050 references remain in that repo's
   docs/PRD_BIMS_robot_arm.md and docs/pi-deployment.md.

3. BUG IN THE PREFLIGHT CHECK ITSELF. `_diagnose_ports`, `port_status` and `preflight`
   all tested `cfg in present_devices`, but serial.tools.list_ports.comports()
   enumerates real device nodes only. The udev symlinks we deliberately configure
   (/dev/buslinker-leader, /dev/buslinker-follower) are perfectly openable but never
   appear in that enumeration — so a CORRECTLY configured Pi reported both ports
   missing. Worse, the generated diagnosis then advised reverting to
   order-of-enumeration ttyACM* names, contradicting docs/pi-deployment.md, which is
   the likely cause of the repeated .env flip-flopping. Fixed with `_port_present()`,
   which realpath-resolves before comparing. Committed to the robot-arm repo (NOT this
   one) as d418071 on branch robot-arm-pi-integration; arm-pi pulled to the same commit.

arm-pi now reports /health/preflight ok:true with both ports green and an empty
diagnosis. DRY_RUN was flipped to false with the operator at the bench, so the arms are
LIVE — the next /teleop/start, /record/start or /replay/start moves real hardware.

Access note for future sessions: `brevitest3` sudo on arm-pi requires a password that
we do not have, but `ssh root@arm-pi` authenticates by key straight to uid 0. Use that
for privileged ops. No reflash is ever needed for this.

Caveat NOT closed by this work: every reachability check so far was over the tailnet
(source 100.104.29.116). Tailscale Funnel is enabled and correctly proxies
/ -> 127.0.0.1:8000, but no request has yet reached the Pi from the public internet.
Funnel traffic arrives at the app as 127.0.0.1, so the first 127.0.0.1 line in
`journalctl -u robot-arm` will be the first hard proof the Vercel->Pi path works.

`npm run check`: 40 errors / 434 warnings (baseline ~46, so at or below it). All 40 are
in master-owned files — 28 in src/lib/server/mcp/bims-mcp.ts, 8 in
src/routes/assembly/[sessionId]/+page.svelte, and the rest in api/mcp and r2.ts. Zero
in robot-arm code; this branch's only edit was progress.txt.
