2026-07-30 — feat/arm-pi-connection — arm control page: controls-first layout, merged session bar, persisted connection log
================================================================================
Follow-up to the arm bring-up earlier today. With the arm actually usable, the control
page's layout became the bottleneck — it had grown by accretion during ARM-01 and buried
the three controls an operator actually uses.

LAYOUT. Teleop / Record / Replay moved to the top; RUN TASK sits below them. Arm connection
became a sticky right-hand side panel at lg+ so health stays visible while working, with a
one-line compact variant under the title on narrow screens. Both variants are driven by the
same derived state inside RobotArmConnectionPanel (a new `variant` prop) rather than
duplicated markup, so they cannot drift apart. Root width went max-w-5xl -> max-w-7xl to fit
the second column, and the page's own p-4 was dropped since cart-mfg/+layout.svelte already
applies p-4 lg:p-6 (it was doubled).

MERGED SESSION UI. The page carried TWO active-session displays that could contradict each
other: RobotArmControlBar, and an inline banner at +page.svelte:140-172 whose stop form had
no confirm and no use:enhance. The banner is deleted. RobotArmControlBar absorbed its states
and is now the single indicator, sitting directly above the controls it governs.

The subtle part: `active` (live from the Pi) and `holder` (derived from Mongo
robot_arm_runs via deriveArmHolder) are different sources that CAN disagree — a run whose
terminal webhook never landed leaves a holder row set forever while the Pi reports nothing
running. That is exactly the 70-day STALE row seen this morning. Precedence is now explicit:
the Pi is authoritative for WHETHER something is running, Mongo for WHO and SINCE WHEN. The
orphaned case renders STALE and states out loud that it blocks Run Task, so a ghost row
explains itself rather than being a mystery.

Two long-standing bugs in that component, both fixed:
  - elapsed() had no hour/day rollover, so a 70-day run rendered "101939m 3s". Now "70d 8h".
  - currentUserId was accepted as a prop but never used — because the component's LOCAL
    Holder interface omitted userId (ArmHolder in robot-arm-lock.ts:33 has it), which made
    the comparison un-writable against the declared type. The prop was not vestigial, the
    type was incomplete. Wired up per ARM-01 §8.4: Stop no longer prompts you to confirm
    stopping your own run, only someone else's.

CONNECTION LOG. Reuses the existing immutable DeviceEvent collection (30-day TTL) — no new
model, no schema change. Recorded from the BIMS side rather than pushed by the Pi, because
the event that matters most — "BIMS cannot reach the arm" — is only observable from here.

Writes on state change, plus an hourly floor while steady. The heartbeat is deliberately
coarse: /devices/+page.server.ts:19 renders DeviceEvent.find() UNFILTERED with limit(100),
so a 60s arm heartbeat (~1440 rows/day) would have buried an unrelated page. A 30s dedupe on
changes absorbs Refresh-button mashing and instantaneous flapping.

device_events carries applyImmutableMiddleware, which blocks updateOne/findOneAndUpdate — so
no atomic upsert throttle is available and the read-then-write is unavoidably racy across
concurrent tabs. Accepted deliberately: worst case is two adjacent same-state rows a few ms
apart in an append-only TTL'd telemetry log that nothing reads for correctness.

"Last connected" folds in the observation from the current request rather than reading the
newest success row. Reading the row directly would report an hour-old timestamp next to a
green ONLINE dot, since a steady arm only writes hourly. When down, it falls back to a wider
query rather than claiming the arm has never connected.

RUN TASK GATE. Kept exactly as-is — it is the UI half of a server-side re-check in startTask
(+page.server.ts:174-184) that is the real enforcement, and DRY_RUN=false currently blocks
it by design (ARM-01 §7.4). Only the message improved: it now names the reason and the exact
fix (set DRY_RUN=true in ~/robot-arm/.env, restart robot-arm), and the already-running case
points at the session bar.

HOUSEKEEPING. safeActive() normalized to {active, activeError} matching safePreflight(),
which deleted the hand-written isActive/isError union guards the page carried.

KNOWN TRADEOFF: RobotArmControlBar keeps sticky bottom-0 for phone thumb-reach, but sticky
only pins while the element's own flow position is on screen — moving it down to sit with
the controls means it pins across less of the scroll range than before. Judged worth it
since it now sits directly above the Start buttons, but it is a partial regression against
ARM-01 §8.4's "always one thumb-reach away".

NOT DONE, flagged for a separate commit: all four robot-arm AuditLog writes
(control/+page.server.ts:208,242 and calibrate/+page.server.ts:57,83) use field names that
do not exist in the AuditLog schema — resourceType, resourceId, userId, username, timestamp,
details. Mongoose strict mode silently drops them, so those audit rows land with NO
attribution at all, just _id/action/changedAt. Real bug, ~4-line fix, but not a UI change.

Also noted but not acted on: device_events has exactly one index (the TTL on createdAt), so
the connection-log read filters on an unindexed deviceId and reverse-scans the TTL index.
Bounded by the 30-day TTL and fine at current volume; if device_events grows past ~100k docs
add { deviceId: 1, createdAt: -1 }. That is a shared-model change affecting every
device_events writer, so it wants its own review.

`npm run check`: 40 errors / 434 warnings — IDENTICAL to the pre-change baseline (40), and
zero errors and zero warnings in the four touched files.

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-feat-ar-2c5b1f-brevitest.vercel.app
  (immutable: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-duzxteid7-brevitest.vercel.app)
Source: branch feat/arm-pi-connection @ commit 33a0603e
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout feat/arm-pi-connection at commit 33a0603e
  (or: git log feat/arm-pi-connection --oneline to find it if the branch has moved on)
What this deployment is for: the reorganized arm control page — controls first, connection

2026-07-30 — feat/box-inventory-disconnect — disconnect Box as inventory source
================================================================================
Box no longer supplies parts inventory. PartDefinition becomes BIMS-owned master
data, edited directly rather than wiped and re-imported on every sync.

Root cause this addresses: syncPartsFromBox() ran deleteMany({}) on
part_definitions and then re-upserted every row from the Box spreadsheet "Live
Equipment Overview.xlsx", so any manual correction was destroyed on the next
sync. The sync had also been failing since 2026-03-04 (lastSyncStatus "partial",
E11000 dup key on partNumber: null) and its Box access token expired 2026-03-20,
so the part rows were already effectively BIMS-owned in practice.

2026-07-30 — master — Claude-driven SPU reassembly inventory adjustment (MCP tools + agent API)
================================================================================
New capability: an operator can tell Claude (via the BIMS MCP connector) "Reassembly completed
on SPU 203 with all magnets in heating block used" and Claude resolves the parts, presents a
per-SPU confirmation list (SPU barcode + last-5 header, one row per part with fill-in-the-blank
quantities — per Alejandro's Figure 1 mock), and only after explicit user approval deducts
inventory. Same part on multiple SPUs = separate quantity per SPU. Vague prompts with no
identifiable parts → Claude asks the user to scan the SPU + part barcodes.

Built:
- src/routes/api/agent/inventory/parts/+server.ts (NEW): GET part resolver — scanned barcode
  (exact, falls back to partNumber), exact partNumber, or free-text q where every token must
  match name/description/partNumber/category ("screw upper metal bracket" → the bracket screw).
  Optional bomType=spu|cartridge filter. Returns candidates w/ live inventoryCount.
- src/routes/api/agent/inventory/reassembly/+server.ts (NEW): POST atomic per-SPU deduction.
  Resolves SPUs by _id/udi/barcode with unique-suffix fallback ("203", "71dbc"); parts by
  barcode/partNumber/exact name. Validates EVERYTHING first — any unresolved ref or non-positive
  quantity rejects the whole request with per-entry errors, nothing deducted. Requires
  confirmed:true (agent's assertion the human approved the list). Applies via the existing
  recordTransaction service (type 'consumption' → decrements PartDefinition.inventoryCount,
  fires low-stock notification) + one AuditLog per SPU. Response returns previous→new counts.
- InventoryTransaction model + recordTransaction service: added optional spuId field + index so
  reassembly deductions are queryable per SPU (additive schema change).
- src/routes/api/agent/inventory/spu-assembly-map/+server.ts (NEW): GET the active SPU work
  instruction as a step→parts map (title/text/partNumber/qty per step). This is how Claude
  resolves assembly-CONTEXT language part names can't ("the screw for the upper metal bracket"
  matches no part name — it's WI knowledge). Steps live in versions[].steps.partRequirements
  (partName is stashed in .notes), current version = wi.currentVersion. NOTE: today's active WI
  is sparse (v1, 1 step, 10 parts) — the map gets useful once Alejandro uploads the detailed
  SPU build instruction set he offered.
- src/lib/server/mcp/bims-mcp.ts: three new tools — parts_lookup, spu_assembly_parts_map, and
  record_reassembly_parts_usage. The record tool's description encodes the mandatory workflow
  (resolve vague parts → clarify ambiguity → no parts named = ask for barcode scans → show the
  Figure-1-style per-SPU quantity list → only call with confirmed:true after user approval).

TESTED live against Atlas on a local dev server (port 5199, env from the second working copy's
.env): parts_lookup "magnet" → the 3 magnet parts + upper bracket (PT-SPU-008/009/010/012 with
live counts); partNumber + barcode paths OK. Reassembly gates: confirmed:false → 400; unknown
part/SPU → per-entry errors, nothing applied; qty 0 → rejected; suffix "0202" resolved
BT-M01-0000-0202. Full end-to-end: deducted 1x PT-SPU-009 on SPU ...0202 (875→874, tx
PxiLXoMfIPSzQ-aGN5cBp, spuId-linked, AuditLog written) then REVERSED with a documented
adjustment tx (874→875) via raw driver. MCP layer verified: tools/list = 34 (31+3), tools/call
parts_lookup("main magnet spherical") → PT-SPU-008 over the wire.

Also: this working copy was missing @modelcontextprotocol/server + zod in node_modules (deps
were in package.json; nobody had npm-installed since the MCP work landed) — `npm install` fixed
it, which is also why /api/mcp 500'd locally before. npm run check DROPPED 41 → 10 errors (all
10 pre-existing, none in the new files; the old 31+ were all cannot-find-module fallout).
package-lock.json diff is just npm normalizing stale "peer" flags. Not committed/deployed yet.

Component map added same session (WIMF-SPU-01 v18 docx from Alejandro): curated component→parts
map in src/lib/server/services/spu-component-parts.ts (12 components: base structure, drive,
LRA, heater block, timing belt/stage, stage board, structure top, upper magnet bracket,
scanner/main board, antennas, enclosure, labels) served by spu-assembly-map with live
inventory cross-check. Cross-check found: PT-SPU-104 (40 Tooth GT-2 Pulley — the WI v18 part;
old PT-SPU-017 is 20-tooth) NOT in part_definitions; label parts PT-SPU-056/057/058/059/070/072
not in inventory; antennas PT-SPU-101/102/103 exist but inactive at 0; PT-SPU-099 Aluminum Tape
count 0. Alejandro says two new parts are coming — tracked in the map, NOT added to inventory.

2026-07-31 — master — Atlas query-targeting alerts: root causes + index fixes
================================================================================
Atlas fired "Query Targeting: Scanned/Returned > 1000" on all 3 nodes starting 17:08Z.
Full repo audit + live Atlas index/size snapshot + Query Insights (Jacob) findings:

CONFIRMED OFFENDERS (fixed by adding indexes / narrowing filters):
1. ot2_bridge_commands sweep-status lookup (kind + payload.sweepRunId) — polled in a loop
   by DeckLoadingGrid during every deck scan; NO index → full 50K-doc scan per tick.
   Query Insights #1: 14.5K exec/day, 19.2 min total, 35,997 examined:returned.
   → index {kind, payload.sweepRunId}.
2. kanban replenish page AuditLog.find({recordId: /^replenishment:/}) without tableName —
   full 35K-doc audit_log scan per view (52,116 ratio in QI) → filter now includes
   tableName so the {tableName,recordId} compound serves it.
3. /api/kanban/wip-mtime watermark poll: findOne().sort({updatedAt:-1}) on kanban_tasks,
   polled every few seconds per open board, no updatedAt index → index {updatedAt:-1}
   (+ {status,archived,statusChangedAt} for the archive cron).
4. Robot-arm control page DeviceEvent reads (deviceId+eventType) — only a createdAt TTL
   index existed → index {deviceId,eventType,createdAt:-1} (also covers equipment detail
   + ask-bims device reads).
5. opentrons health-poller singleton: OpentronsRunRecord.find({status:$in}) every 15s
   forever with no status-prefix index → index {status}.
6. validation_sessions had ZERO indexes (magnetometer poll + history) → {spuId,startedAt},
   {type,startedAt}.
7. agent_messages had ZERO indexes (3 endpoints filter by user/status) → {toUserId,createdAt},
   {fromUserId,createdAt}, {status,createdAt}.
8. cartridge_records (14.5K docs @ 35KB!): dashboard sort({updatedAt:-1}), createdAt-window
   counts, and $exists aggregates (QI ratio 164,818 on one shape) → {updatedAt},{createdAt},
   {reagentInspection.status},{waxQc.status}.
Indexes build automatically on deploy (Mongoose autoIndex).

ARCHITECTURE FLAGS (decisions needed, not yet changed):
- scanner_events: 1,575,522 docs, no TTL — heartbeat telemetry accumulating forever.
  Recommend TTL (e.g. 14d heartbeats / 90d scans) or move heartbeats out of Mongo.
- ot2_bridge_commands: 50K docs @ 9.1KB avg (~460MB) — completedAt TTL exists (7d) but
  pending/failed/claimed docs never expire; payloads are fat. Recommend TTL on createdAt
  or slimming payloads.
- cartridge_records avg 35.4KB with embedded photos[] + 30+ indexes — doc-size and
  index-count pressure on the biggest collection; longer-term: move photos to cv_images
  refs only.
- traceability/bom search: unanchored case-insensitive regexes (user-triggered, lower heat).
- Secondaries also alerted → some reads use secondaryPreferred (likely the research app
  sharing the cluster) — same fixes apply cluster-wide once indexes build.
npm run check: 11 errors — unchanged baseline.

Second follow-up ("someone needs to add an inventory mutation to the MCP server" on claude.ai):
diagnosed via this session's own BIMS connector — the connector CONNECTS fine to live prod
(inventory_overview returns current Atlas counts) but claude.ai still serves the pre-feature
31-tool list: record_reassembly_parts_usage / parts_lookup / spu_assembly_parts_map are absent
from its cached tools/list even though the server (verified sha 8d86d539) registers 34. Ruled
out wrong-host: the alejandro-valdezs-projects Vercel twin is behind SSO (a connector there
could not connect at all), so the connector must point at the live main domain. Root cause is
claude.ai's per-connector tool-list cache. Server-side mitigation: bumped McpServer version
1.0.0 -> 2.0.0 (bump on every tool add/remove from now on). User-side fix required once:
remove + re-add (or edit/save) the BIMS connector in claude.ai Settings -> Connectors to force
a tools/list re-fetch, then start a new conversation.

================================================================================
2026-07-31 — feat/robot-arm-pose-calibration — multi-pose joint-map calibration UI
================================================================================
Built the BIMS-side UI for the multi-pose robot-arm calibration whose API landed on the
Pi earlier today (separate repo C:\Users\aleja\robot-arm, branch feat/multi-pose-calibration
@ 17144e2). Sync zero captures ONE matched pose and can therefore only ever produce an
offset; the leader and follower are geared differently (1/345 vs 1/191 vs 1/147), so a 1:1
teleop mirror over-travels on any joint whose ratios disagree — the gripper is the visible
casualty. Capturing several poses lets the Pi least-squares fit a per-joint scale as well.

Files: src/lib/server/robot-arm-client.ts (getJointMap/capturePose/deletePose/
clearJointMap against /calibrate/map*, plus JointMapRecord + JointFitStatus types),
src/routes/manufacturing/cart-mfg/robot-arm/calibrate/+page.server.ts (joint-map load +
three actions), and .../calibrate/+page.svelte (pose capture, per-pose delete, per-joint
fit table with status and live tracking error).

Two real defects were caught by a pre-push review before the push, both verified by hand
against source rather than taken on the reviewer's word:

1. Serverless timeout overrun. svelte.config.js sets maxDuration: 30. The calibrate load
   awaits getActive -> getCalibration -> getJointMap strictly sequentially (the serial bus
   is single-owner, so Promise.all is not available the way control/+page.server.ts uses
   it). Adding the third call took the worst case from 5+15=20s to 5+15+15=35s. Against an
   unreachable Pi — the normal case from Vercel, since the host lives on the tailnet —
   every timer runs to completion and Vercel 504s the invocation, which defeats the whole
   point of the safeCalibration/safeJointMap wrappers. Cut the live timeouts 15s -> 10s
   (5+10+10=25s) and made safeActive report reachability, so a host that never answered
   skips the live reads entirely instead of taking the most expensive path.

2. AuditLog writes were being silently discarded. auditLogSchema declares tableName/
   recordId/action/oldData/newData/changedAt/changedBy/... — not the resourceType/
   resourceId/userId/username/timestamp/details shape documented in CLAUDE.md. Mongoose
   strict mode (no global override in connection.ts) drops unknown paths on create, so
   every calibration mutation would have persisted as {_id, action, changedAt}: an audit
   row that looks written but records no operator and no fit data. Rewrote the three new
   actions to the real schema (rich payload into newData, which is Schema.Types.Mixed).
   NOTE: the two PRE-EXISTING actions in the same file (capture, clear, for sync zero)
   still use the non-conforming shape and are still being silently dropped — left alone as
   out of scope, but they are a live QMS gap. CLAUDE.md's "Form Actions" example documents
   the wrong field names and is what propagated this; worth correcting at the source.

Also fixed: a whitespace hole in the deletePose index guard (Number('   ') === 0 would have
silently deleted pose 0), an unguarded fit.status.filter that would 500 the entire route
during SSR on a malformed joint_map.json, and copy that asserted "No joint map saved.
Teleop mirrors the leader 1:1." when the host was merely unreachable — on a page whose job
is telling the operator whether the arm is calibrated, that is the one thing it must not
state backwards.

npm run check: 57 errors / 426 warnings on this branch; zero diagnostics reference any of
the three touched files.

NOT YET TESTABLE END-TO-END: the Pi's running process still serves pre-calibration code.
Its working tree is at 17144e2 with the endpoints present, but robot-arm.service has been
up since 2026-07-30 15:31:03 CDT — before the checkout — so GET /calibrate/map returns 404.
The unit is system-scoped, brevitest3 has no passwordless sudo and root SSH is key-denied,
so the restart needs a human: ssh brevitest3@172.16.28.191 'sudo systemctl restart robot-arm'

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-feat-ro-e765c4-brevitest.vercel.app

Retrieve this exact code: git checkout feat/robot-arm-pose-calibration at commit 76eb1841
  (or: git log feat/robot-arm-pose-calibration --oneline to find it if the branch has since moved on)

2026-07-31 — master — multi-pose calibration UI promoted to production
================================================================================
Promoted feat/robot-arm-pose-calibration to master. Rebased onto 28c5ef35b (master had
moved 14 commits); only progress.txt conflicted — both sides had appended entries, both
kept in chronological order. The code commit applied cleanly with no conflicts.

npm run check: 61 errors / 426 warnings, zero of them in the three touched files
(robot-arm-client.ts, calibrate/+page.server.ts, calibrate/+page.svelte). The count rose
from 57 purely because master's 14 commits added 18 files and 4 errors of their own.

FIELD RESULT — the feature was used against real hardware the same day and found the
gripper (servo 6) bug it was built for. Diagnosis, in order:
  - Ruled out clamping: follower gripper Min/Max_Angle_Limit are 0/4095, i.e. unset.
  - The leader gripper travels 1973..3335 (1362 ticks); the follower claw only ~354.
    The follower runs OUT OF TRAVEL at roughly 35% of the leader's squeeze, so the
    relationship SATURATES — it is not one straight line. Active-region slope ~0.72,
    flat thereafter.
  - The original fitted scale of 0.8231 therefore kept climbing past the follower's
    mechanical stop (+707 ticks at full squeeze). On an STS3215 that stalls the motor,
    trips overload protection, and CUTS TORQUE — the claw goes slack. The reported
    symptom "won't close all the way" was over-commanding, not under-travel.
  - Early pose sets made this invisible: captures clustered in the open third of the
    range (5%..36%), with duplicate poses and one mid-range outlier giving segment
    slopes of 0.335 and 1.297 either side of it.
  - After cleanup the fit is honest: gripper scale 0.7187 residual 3.8 (was 170), and
    shoulder_pan residual fell 112 -> 2.9.

OUTSTANDING, in priority order:
1. Follower servo 6 needs Max_Angle_Limit set (~2530) so the existing clamp in
   _mirror_goal stops the goal at the closed position. Until then ~796 ticks of squeeze
   past the stop remain unprotected and the stall returns on a hard grip. EEPROM write,
   reversible by writing 4095 back. Not done — needs running on the Pi directly.
2. STALE-INDEX DELETE BUG (shipped, known). Pose delete posts a positional index, but
   the Pi renumbers every pose on each mutation (_build_map enumerates and reassigns).
   Deleting one row shifts everything below it, so a second delete against the page's
   original numbering removes the WRONG pose. This bit the operator today: it destroyed
   a good open/close pose pair that had to be physically recaptured. Fix is to post
   captured_at (already rendered on every row) and resolve it server-side. Was flagged
   as a warn in the pre-push review and shipped anyway at the operator's direction.
3. wrist_roll still reads insufficient_range (span 7 ticks across all poses) with a live
   tracking error near 886. It is the joint with the cord-tangle history; deserves its
   own capture pass.
4. The two PRE-EXISTING sync-zero actions in calibrate/+page.server.ts (capture, clear)
   still write the non-conforming AuditLog shape and are still being silently dropped by
   Mongoose strict mode. The three new joint-map actions were fixed to the real schema.
   CLAUDE.md's Form Actions example documents the wrong field names and is the source.

Deployment: production — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb.vercel.app
Source: branch master @ commit c93d3d17

  (or: git log master --oneline to find it if master has since moved on)

================================================================================
2026-08-05 - feat/particle-auto-create-spu - Particle sync auto-creates draft SPUs for new BT-M01 devices
================================================================================
Registering a device in the Particle console used to be only half the job: the device
synced into ParticleDevice, but until somebody manually registered a matching SPU in
BIMS it stayed in the "unmatched" list and never appeared anywhere the SPU record is
the entry point (magnetometer/thermocouple validation, the SPU detail page). Particle
sync now closes that gap - a device whose name is a real SPU name and has no matching
SPU gets a draft SPU minted for it in the same pass that does the linking.
- src/lib/server/particle.ts: new exported SPU_DEVICE_NAME_PATTERN = /^BT-M01-\d{4}-\d{4}$/.
  Inside linkDevicesToSpus(), when the suffix lookup misses, a device matching that
  pattern is created via Spu.create() with status 'draft', assemblyStatus 'created',
  qcStatus 'pending', udi AND barcode set to the Particle device name, a statusTransitions
  entry (null -> draft, reason 'auto_created_particle_sync'), and createdBy pinned to the
  literal 'system:particle-sync'. An AuditLog INSERT row is written
  alongside it (tableName 'spus', recordId = the new nanoid, newData carrying udi/status/
  autoCreatedFrom:'particle'/particleDeviceId, reason "Auto-created from Particle device
  during link", changedBy = actor username). The new record is then put into the in-run
  spuByNumber map keyed by its numeric suffix, so a second device with the same suffix
  in the same sync does not double-create; and it falls straight through to the existing
  link code, so particleLink.particleDeviceId / particleSerial / linkedAt are set in the
  same iteration - one pass, no second sweep needed. A create failure is caught and
  pushed onto errors[] rather than aborting the whole run.
- The pattern guard is deliberately strict and is the whole safety story here. The
  Particle account currently holds 106 device rows; 68 map to SPUs and 38 come back
  unmatched, but only 4 of those 38 are genuine SPUs - BT-M01-0000-0255, -0253, -0241
  and -0242. The remaining 34 are cartridge decks (Cartridge_Deck_GEN4_001), tip
  calibrators, bench tools (TOOL-CT-047) and scratch/dev names (new4, Argon, 71E50,
  owl_narwhal). Every one of those carries trailing digits, and the existing
  extractNumericId() matches on trailing digits (/(\d+)$/), so an unguarded auto-create
  would happily mint 34 junk records into `spus` - a SACRED collection under
  applySacredMiddleware, where cleanup is not a delete but a paperwork exercise. Anything
  that fails the pattern keeps the old behaviour exactly: it is reported in unmatched[]
  and nothing is written.
- Plumbing: linkDevicesToSpus(actor?) and syncDevices(actor?) both take an optional
  actor { _id, username } and now return created: string[] (the device names/UDIs of the
  SPUs minted this run) alongside the existing counters. syncDevices threads the actor
  into its post-sync link call and surfaces created[] from it. Link errors are folded into
  the sync's own errors[] rather than swallowed - a create failure on a sacred collection
  must never render to the operator as "Successfully synced N device(s)."
- Hardening applied after an adversarial review pass over the first cut, all real defects
  rather than theory:
  * createdBy is pinned to 'system:particle-sync' and NOT the acting user. /assembly's
    "Start new build" (src/routes/assembly/+page.server.ts:125-130) reuses any recent draft
    matching { createdBy: <that user>, status:'draft', assemblyStatus:'created', createdAt
    within 24h }. With the actor's id an auto-created SPU matched all four clauses, so an
    operator who synced and then started a build would have assembled a physical unit under
    a UDI already bound to a different Particle device. Actor attribution lives on the
    AuditLog changedBy instead, where it costs nothing.
  * Suffix keys are normalised through parseInt, and an exact-UDI map is consulted first.
    Previously an existing "SPU-255" produced key "255" while device BT-M01-0000-0255
    produced "0255" - no hit, and the new code would have minted a permanent duplicate into
    a collection whose delete hooks throw.
  * Two SPU-named devices sharing a trailing suffix (BT-M01-0000-0255 vs BT-M01-0001-0255)
    now push an error instead of silently renaming the SPU and stealing the first device's
    link.
  * The created SPU is registered in created[]/the lookup maps immediately after
    Spu.create, and AuditLog.create got its own try/catch. Previously an audit failure
    left a minted, undeletable SPU absent from every report of the run.
  * The settings page now renders the error list as well as the counts.
- src/routes/particle/settings/+page.server.ts passes { _id, username } from locals.user
  into both the sync and link actions, appends "Created N new SPU(s)." to the result
  message, and returns created[] to the page. +page.svelte renders it as an open
  <details> list under the success banner, mirroring the existing unmatched-devices
  disclosure so both lists read the same way.
- Known consequence worth stating rather than discovering later: linkDevicesToSpus also
  OVERWRITES spu.udi with the Particle device name on every link (unchanged behaviour),
  and the magnetometer page (src/routes/validation/magnetometer/+page.server.ts:14)
  selects SPUs purely on { 'particleLink.particleDeviceId': { $exists: true, $ne: null } }
  with no status filter - so an auto-created draft SPU shows up in that device picker as
  soon as it is linked. That is the intended outcome (the device is testable the moment
  it exists), but it does mean drafts are now visible there.
npm run check: 101 errors / 426 warnings across 2000 files - identical before and after
this change, and ZERO of them are in the three touched files (src/lib/server/particle.ts,
src/routes/particle/settings/+page.server.ts, +page.svelte). 101 is this branch's real
baseline off origin/master; an earlier draft of this entry quoted 11, which was wrong.
Local `npm run build` is not a usable gate on this machine (OOMs at ~30GB) - the Vercel
branch build is the real one.

Deployment: preview - https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-feat-pa-1e83a6-brevitest.vercel.app
  (immutable: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-5zpa6f409-brevitest.vercel.app)
Source: branch feat/particle-auto-create-spu @ commit 3401f0c9
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout feat/particle-auto-create-spu at commit 3401f0c9
  (or: git log feat/particle-auto-create-spu --oneline to find it if the branch has moved on)
What this deployment is for: verifying Particle sync/link auto-creates draft SPUs for
  unmatched BT-M01 devices (0241/0242/0253/0255) and surfaces created[] + errors[] on
  the Particle settings page.

2026-08-05 - PRODUCTION DATA CORRECTION - SPU BT-M01-0000-0255
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
0255 was created manually in prod ahead of this feature deploying, by an agent script
that built the document by CLONING an existing draft SPU as a field template so it would
pick up the schema defaults. That was the wrong approach: it also copied
BT-M01-0000-0246's entire `validation` block, so a never-assembled unit reported
magnetometer/thermocouple/spectrophotometer all "passed", carrying 0246's sessionIds,
rawData and cartridge reference.

This became an active misread rather than cosmetic noise: a real magnetometer run on
0255 FAILED (11 of 15 channels below the 3900-4500 Z range, session 9RLuZTzJ5mv_OKgNNcFJC,
test ran 15:40:38, recorded 17:41:31). validation/magnetometer/+page.server.ts:111 writes
the SPU rollup ONLY when a run passes, so the failed run left the inherited "passed" block
untouched and BIMS displayed a failed unit as passed.

Corrected: the rollup was rebuilt strictly from 0255's own validation_sessions (note
`type` in that collection carries BOTH short and long forms - 'mag'/'magnetometer',
'thermo'/'thermocouple' - so any consumer must alias them). Result: magnetometer failed
with its own failureReasons, thermocouple/lux/spectrophotometer pending, overall failed,
qcStatus failed. Also added the `barcode` and creation `statusTransitions` entry the
shipped auto-create path writes. AuditLog UPDATE row written with the bad block preserved
in oldData and the reason recorded.

The shipped feature does NOT have this defect - linkDevicesToSpus() calls Spu.create()
with explicit fields only, so Mongoose applies clean schema defaults and validation comes
out pending. The cloning existed solely in the one-off manual script, which was only
needed because the feature had not deployed yet. No other SPU was created this way.

Post-review self-check caught a REGRESSION introduced by the hardening pass itself, before
merge. The padding-normalisation fix (parseInt on the suffix key) was applied to the ONE
shared lookup map, so it changed matching for every device, not just SPU-named ones. Against
live data that meant 8 junk devices would have matched and RENAMED 4 real SPUs:
Cartridge_Deck_GEN4_001 / Cartridge_Deck_GEN3_001 -> BT-M01-0000-0001,
Cartridge-Deck-GEN3-002 / Cartridge_Deck_GEN4_002 -> BT-M01-0000-0002,
Cartridge_Deck_GEN3_003 / Cartridge_Deck_GEN4_003 -> BT-M01-0000-0003, and
new4 / Cartridge_Deck_GEN4_004 -> BT-M01-0000-0004. linkDevicesToSpus overwrites spu.udi on
every link, so those four SPUs would have been renamed to deck/scratch names and had their
particleLink repointed - strictly worse than the duplicate-SPU case the normalisation was
meant to prevent, and on the sacred collection.
Fixed by splitting the map in two: spuByNumber keeps the original raw-suffix keys (exact
legacy behaviour for every device), and spuByNumberNorm is a last-resort lookup consulted
ONLY when the device name matches SPU_DEVICE_NAME_PATTERN. Non-SPU-named devices can no
longer reach the normalised path at all. Also moved the same-suffix collision guard BELOW
the already-linked check so an established link is not re-reported as an error every run,
and registered newly created SPUs into all three maps rather than just the normalised one.
Verified by replaying the final matching logic against live prod data read-only:
linked=3 alreadyLinked=69 created=3 (BT-M01-0000-0241/-0242/-0253) unmatched=34 errors=0,
with zero renames of existing SPUs and no non-SPU-named device matching any real SPU.
npm run check after the fix: 101 errors / 426 warnings - unchanged baseline, zero in the
touched files.

Deployment: production - https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb.vercel.app
  (immutable: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-mst9jz08l-brevitest.vercel.app)
Source: branch master @ commit 932e42a9 (merge of PR #41, feat/particle-auto-create-spu @ 522cca70)
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout master at commit 932e42a9
  (or: git log master --oneline to find it if master has since moved on)
What this deployment is for: Particle sync/link now auto-creates draft SPUs for unmatched

  BIMS (magnetometer picker, SPU detail, servicing scan) without a manual SPU registration.
  Next Link run is expected to create BT-M01-0000-0241, -0242 and -0253.

2026-08-03 — feat/perm-03-shadow — PERM-03: deny-by-default evaluator in shadow (log-only) mode
================================================================================
Stacked on feat/perm-02-registry. Implements docs/prds/PERM-03.

- NEW src/lib/server/route-policy.ts — evaluateRoutePolicy(): public prefixes (/login,
  /logout, /invite) -> allow; no session -> deny(unauthenticated); no `bims` membership ->
  deny(no-bims-membership); admin-gate map (/admin -> admin:full, /documents/*/approve ->
  document:approve) -> deny(gate:X); else allow. Classification by CREDENTIAL not path:
  requests bearing key material (x-api-key/x-agent-api-key/x-station-agent-key/authorization/
  ?key=//api/mcp/k/) are exempt — machine surface is scoped in PERM-05. Shadow log model
  (permission_shadow_log, 30-day TTL) registered locally — models/index.ts deliberately NOT
  touched (Jacob's uncommitted KB2 work lives there).
- hooks.server.ts: shadowEvaluate() runs on every request after session resolution.
  LOG-ONLY: writes a row per would-be denial; hard requirement is it can never throw or
  block (outer try/catch swallow + console.error). Anonymous non-API paths aren't logged
  (current hooks already redirect those); anonymous /api/ hits ARE logged.
- NEW scripts/report-shadow-denials.ts — aggregates by caller x path x reason (--days N).
  PERM-04 flip criterion: >=7 consecutive days where every logged denial is intended.
Expected legitimate denials to watch for: the stale `admin` (Research Admin) user if anyone
still uses it (no bims — intended), research users browsing BIMS, any forgotten automation
hitting session endpoints without keys.

2026-08-05 — feat/perm-04-prep — PERM-04 correction: kanban tier gate is kanban:replenish (not a new string)
================================================================================
Merged current origin/master into the stack (only conflict was progress.txt; both sides kept).
Stack re-verified against master: npm run check 11 errors (baseline), test:unit 57/57.

Design correction found while installing the last gate. PERM-00 specced a NEW `kanban:promote`
for the tier-1 -> tier-2 gate. Reading the KB2 code shows that gate already exists and is
architecturally airtight:
  - transitionTask() throws TIER_CROSSING_FORBIDDEN on every tier crossing unless the caller
    passes allowTierCrossing.
  - Only 4 call sites pass it. The two human ones are replenish.ts promote + demote, both
    behind requireReplenisher() -> kanban:replenish || isAdmin.
  - Operators do not hold kanban:replenish; Admins do. So "tier promotion is admin-only" is
    already true.
Adding kanban:promote would have been a second name for one chokepoint — exactly the drift
this project exists to kill. So: GATE_PERMISSIONS now contains kanban:replenish (moved out of
the legacy kanban group), kanban:promote is retired via a new DEPRECATED_PERMISSIONS list, and
migrate-perm-02-additive.ts now strips deprecated strings as well as adding missing ones.

Re-ran the migration (APPLIED): Admin +[kanban:replenish] -[kanban:promote], propagated to 8
users. Note what that reveals — the LIVE Admin role never had kanban:replenish at all (the
seed-drift from the audit; seed.ts was fixed in PERM-01 but the live role predates it), so
admins have been passing the replenish gate purely via the isAdmin() fallback in replenish.ts.
Now they hold the permission itself.

Two non-human tier-crossing paths logged as PERM-05 follow-ups (in PERM-04 PRD §B):
  - standing.ts supply autopilot promotes with a system actor — by design (KB2-13).
  - api/agent/operations/kanban/tasks/merge forces a merged Tier 1 option to done on agent-key
    auth alone. Under "bots are not admins" that is a bot crossing the commitment point;
    needs a decision in PERM-05.

2026-08-05 — feat/perm-05-machine — PERM-05 machine surface + PERM-06 cleanup script
================================================================================
Stacked on feat/perm-04-prep. Implements docs/prds/PERM-05, plus the PERM-06 migration
(written, deliberately NOT applied — see interlock below).

NEW src/lib/server/machine-actor.ts — the whole machine-surface policy in one module:
- assertHumanOnly(): admin-gated actions can never be performed by a key-authenticated
  caller, whatever actor is named. HUMAN_ONLY_ACTIONS maps each to WHERE a human does it,
  so the refusal tells the model what to say. Covers both directions of the commitment
  point (replenish + demote + ready-queue reorder), policy/template/standing-target edits,
  proposal + approval decisions, document approval, lot release, assay lock.
- resolveActor(): every machine WRITE must name the human it acts for; the name must match
  an ACTIVE user who holds `bims` (so research-only accounts are rejected too). Attribution
  only — the resolved user's permissions are never consulted for authorization, because
  bots can't reach a gate regardless of whom they name. Reserved actor `system` for cron.
- The refusal IS the mechanism: MISSING_ACTOR_MESSAGE tells the model to ask the human and
  reuse the name for the conversation. Elicitation deliberately unused (CLI-only support).
- logMachineActivity(): dual-identity audit row -> AuditLog tableName 'machine_activity',
  changedBy = the KEY identity (cryptographically true), newData.reportedActor = the claimed
  human, explicitly labelled attribution:'self-reported'. Shows up in Admin → Agent Activity.

bims-mcp.ts (server version 2.13.0 -> 3.0.0 — clients cache tool lists; major bump because
tool contracts changed):
- machineWrite() wraps every mutating tool: human-only check -> actor resolution -> run ->
  dual-identity log. 23 write tools wrapped; `actor` is now a REQUIRED field on all of them
  (was optional/absent on 10, and defaulted to the literal string "agent" on 2 — that default
  is exactly the attribution hole this closes).
- Reads (31 tools) unchanged — no actor, no friction.
- Behaviour change for Jacob's workflow: kanban_replenish / kanban_demote / reorder(scope
  "ready") now REFUSE and return instructions. Committing work is an admin action and bots are
  non-admins. Project-scope reorder still works. kanban_replenishment_status still shows what
  is eligible, so the useful half of the flow is intact.

Agent API: the two body-`actor` -> kanban:admin lookups are DELETED, not patched —
policy PATCH and templates POST now refuse with the human-only message and log the attempt.
Both have full web-UI equivalents (kanban/policy has 7 actions behind requirePolicyAdmin),
verified before closing, so no capability is lost.

NEW src/lib/server/fleet-keys.ts — per-fleet device keys (scanner/ot2/mocreo/particle/station),
timing-safe, ADDITIVE: verifyFleetKey() accepts the fleet key OR the shared AGENT_API_KEY until
<FLEET>_STRICT=true. Nothing to do on any device the day this ships; rollout is per fleet
(set env -> update devices -> confirm audit rows -> flip strict). No endpoints switched over
yet — that's the physical-device step and wants a bench day.

NEW scripts/migrate-perm-06-cleanup.ts — final vocabulary strip (Admin 53 -> 7, Operator
10 -> 1, delete Viewer, deactivate 4 stale test accounts; research roles untouched).
DRY RUN VERIFIED, NOT APPLIED. Guarded by an interlock: --apply refuses unless
PERM06_SWEEP_DONE=true, because stripping legacy strings before the PERM-04 call-site sweep
would lock Operators (not Admins — they have the wildcard) out of real work.

Tests: new machine-actor.test.ts (6 cases: every gated action refused, ordinary actions
allowed, both commitment directions, message content). npm run test:unit 63/63 pass.
npm run check: 11 errors — unchanged baseline.

================================================================================
2026-08-06 — master — reagent run timer: elapsed stopwatch + work-based estimate (was ~2x too long)
================================================================================
Alejandro: filling 24 cartridges across all four wells shows "47 minutes" but the run
actually takes ~25. Asked for either a stopwatch or a corrected timer. Did both, in the
roles that make each honest.

Root cause. The countdown was one multiplication done once in the startRun action
(reagent-filling/+page.server.ts): cartridgeCount x reagentFilling.fillTimePerCartridgeMin,
stored as runEndTime. The setting in Mongo is 2.0 min/cartridge, so 24 carts predicted
48 min. The formula never looked at the well_* run-time parameters, so a one-row run and a
four-row run of the same deck predicted identically — the cartridge count is not what the
protocol spends its time on.

What actually drives the run. Each selected reagent row is one dispense_reagent() call in
Reagent_Filling_GEN7.py: pick up + calibrate a tip, aspirate in batches of 10 wells, dispense
into every well, drop the tip. A full row (well_2..well_5) fills 3 wells per cartridge; a
single-column selection (well_2a..well_5c) fills 1. So the cost is
startup + rows x per-row + wellsFilled x per-well.

Fitted against reagent_batch_records. Took the fastest run of each distinct
(dispenses, rows) shape — fastest = least operator dwell = closest to robot-only time.
Fit: 60s startup + 60s/row + 3.7s/well. Predicted vs measured: 288 wells 22.8 vs 22.0 min,
264 -> 21.3 vs 21.0, 207 -> 16.8 vs 17.3, 192 -> 16.8 vs 16.5, 180 -> 16.1 vs 15.4.

Files:
- NEW src/lib/manufacturing/reagent-run-estimate.ts — countReagentWork() +
  estimateReagentRunSeconds(); tuning constants overridable from settings; falls back to
  assuming a full four-row deck when a run has no well_* params recorded; flags
  run_calibration_check (it ends in an unbounded operator pause the estimate can't cover).
- NEW src/lib/manufacturing/reagent-run-estimate.test.ts — 17 tests, incl. a regression
  guard asserting the model stays within 2 min of each measured run shape.
- reagent-filling/+page.server.ts — startRun now estimates from protocolParameters.
- reagent-filling/RunExecution.svelte — the big readout is now ELAPSED (counts up, always
  true); the estimate is a secondary "~N min remaining (estimated)" line that switches to
  an amber "past the ~N min estimate — waiting on the robot" instead of lying. Added a
  breakdown line ("4 reagent rows x 24 cartridges = 288 wells, ~23 min") so an operator can
  sanity-check the number. The progress bar is position-against-estimate, not a deadline.
- Behaviour fix in the same file: the countdown hitting zero used to declare "Filling
  Complete" and fire onTimerComplete. With a correct (shorter) estimate that would now fire
  ~2 min BEFORE a typical run ends. Completion is now driven only by the OT-2 terminal
  status the EmbeddedRunController already polls (new robotFinished prop), with
  autoCompleteOnExpiry as a fallback for runs with no opentronsRunId, and FinishTimerButton
  still available as the operator override. The 3-beep alarm moved to actual completion
  (suppressed on a page load of an already-finished run).
- manufacturing-settings.ts — reagentFilling gains startupOverheadSec,
  secondsPerReagentGroup, secondsPerDispense; fillTimePerCartridgeMin left in place and
  commented as legacy (no migration needed — absent fields fall through to defaults).
- reagent-filling/settings — the three knobs replace the flat fill-time input, with a live

2026-08-06 — master — stop the spurious "Robot status timed out (bridge slow)" banner
================================================================================
Alejandro: every reagent run pops a red "Robot status timed out (bridge slow) — retrying"
banner right after the cartridge scan. The run always starts anyway; he just wants it gone.

Root cause: a client timeout shorter than a normal round trip, not an actual fault. The
bridge is a relay — BIMS writes an Ot2BridgeCommand to Mongo, the on-robot daemon
long-polls for it (POLL_WAIT_MS 18s), runs it against the OT-2 HTTP API one command at a
time, writes the result back. Every hop budgets 30s for that: the daemon's RELAY_TIMEOUT_S
= 30, proxy.ts BRIDGE_TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000, and the runs/[rid] endpoint's maxDuration = 45.
EmbeddedRunController's browser-side AbortSignal was 6s.

Why right after the scan specifically: the queue is serialized per robot. Entering Running
mounts the controller, which polls immediately — behind whatever startRun just enqueued
(freshness re-sync, run create, play, auto_resume_run) and behind the scan itself, which
per the scanner's command mode can't be shorter than ~5s. So the first poll routinely sat
in the queue past 6s, aborted, painted the banner, and the next poll 2s later cleared it.
Transient and cosmetic — the run was never affected, which is why it always started fine.

Fix (EmbeddedRunController.svelte, shared by wax + reagent so both get it):
- POLL_TIMEOUT_MS 6s -> 20s and ACTION_TIMEOUT_MS 12s -> 30s, so the browser stops giving
  up before the chain it is waiting on has even reached its own deadline.
- Split error state: operator-pressed control actions still surface immediately (they are
  waiting on it), but background poll failures now need POLL_FAILURES_BEFORE_WARNING = 3
  consecutive misses before the banner appears, and the copy says how many checks have
  gone unanswered instead of implying a one-off blip is a fault. A successful poll resets
  the counter. Net effect: a lone slow poll is silent, a genuinely dead bridge still warns
  (fast failures like a 502 trip all three in ~6s; slow timeouts take longer by design,
  since a slow response may still be on its way).

Note this is observational only — BIMS polling the run status has no bearing on whether the
protocol executes, so tolerating a slow poll costs nothing operationally.

npm run check: 11 errors (baseline, unchanged). npm run test:unit: 80 passed.
npm run build: clean.

Deployment: production — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb.vercel.app
Source: branch master @ commit fe8d60aa
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout master at commit fe8d60aa
  (or: git log master --oneline to find it if the branch has since moved on)
What this deployment is for: suppressing the spurious "Robot status timed out (bridge
slow)" banner that appeared on every wax/reagent run right after the cartridge scan.

2026-08-06 — master — Pause actually pauses: stop auto-resume from cancelling operator pauses
================================================================================
Alejandro: "the pause button doesn't work well — I click it during a reagent or wax fill and
it doesn't pause, the robot keeps going, and eventually I have to cancel the run." Follow-up
detail that pinned it exactly: "it says pause requested and then I have to press again for
it to actually pause."

Root cause: the client's auto-resume was cancelling the operator's pause. EmbeddedRunController
auto-resumes the engine's initial off-deck "confirm deck loaded" pause so runs start hands-off,
guarded by a one-shot autoResumedInitial. But the daemon's own execute_auto_resume_run polls
the robot directly at 1s from ON the robot, while the browser goes through the serialized
bridge queue at 2s+ — so the daemon almost always wins that race and the browser NEVER observes
the initial pause. autoResumedInitial therefore stayed false for the whole run, and the first
pause the browser ever saw was the operator's, which it dutifully resumed.

That is exactly why pressing twice worked: press #1 paused the robot, the client resumed it and
in doing so finally consumed the one-shot; press #2 then had nothing left to override it. The
operator was manually burning off a stale flag every single run.

Fix (EmbeddedRunController.svelte — shared, so wax + reagent both get it):
- operatorPaused: set the instant Pause is pressed, BEFORE the request goes out, so a poll
  landing mid-flight cannot resume a pause that was just asked for. Pressing Pause also marks
  autoResumedInitial done — after a manual pause there is no initial pause left to chase.
- Auto-resume is now bounded by the RUN's age (startedAt, AUTO_RESUME_WINDOW_MS 90s — the
  daemon's AUTO_RESUME_TIMEOUT_S of 75s plus headroom) instead of by component lifetime.
  Reloading the page mid-run can no longer re-arm auto-resume against an earlier pause.
- pausePending + displayStatus: the OT-2 pauses at the next command boundary, so it keeps
  reporting `running` for a few seconds. The reconcile poll used to flip the chip straight
  back to RUNNING, which read as "the click did nothing". The chip now holds PAUSE-REQUESTED
  until the robot really pauses, with a note that it finishes its current step first.
- pausePending is bounded by PAUSE_LAND_TIMEOUT_MS (60s): past that, say the pause was not
  acknowledged and re-enable the button rather than leaving a permanently disabled control.
  Held in its own pauseWarning state because a successful poll clears lastError every 2s.
- A hard (non-conflict) refusal of a pause clears the pending state so it can be retried;
  a TIMEOUT deliberately does not, since the request may still have landed.

STILL OPEN — both need a robot-side deploy of scripts/ot2-bridge.py, not a Vercel push:
1. execute_auto_resume_run watches for up to AUTO_RESUME_TIMEOUT_S (75s) and resumes the first
   pause it sees. If a run's initial pause hasn't happened yet and the operator pauses inside
   that window, the DAEMON will resume it — same bug, server side, where the browser can't
   defend. Narrow (the initial pause normally lands within ~1-2s of play, and the daemon breaks
   out immediately after) but real, especially if a protocol ever stops producing an initial pause.
2. clear_stale_protocol_run() treats `paused` as stale and STOPs it to free the run engine. It
   only fires when opening a maintenance run hits a conflict (deck scan / jog), but a
   deliberately-paused fill run is indistinguishable from a leftover off-deck pause to it — so
   starting a scan while a fill is paused will kill the fill. Likely related to "eventually
   leads me to having to cancel it".

npm run check: 11 errors (baseline, unchanged). npm run test:unit: 80 passed. npm run build: clean.

Deployment: production — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb.vercel.app
Source: branch master @ commit c07d8959
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout master at commit c07d8959
  (or: git log master --oneline to find it if the branch has since moved on)
What this deployment is for: Pause on a wax/reagent fill run now holds on the first press
instead of being auto-resumed away, and the chip reports PAUSE-REQUESTED until it lands.

2026-08-06 — master — hold the reagent run clock while the robot is paused
================================================================================
Alejandro: "when it gets paused, the timer is still running — make sure the timer stops
when it's paused as well."

The stopwatch (RunExecution.svelte) only ever knew runStartTime and wall-clock now; pause
state lives in EmbeddedRunController, and nothing connected the two. So a paused robot kept
accruing "elapsed" and kept burning down the remaining estimate for work that wasn't
happening — which also meant the finish time it implied was wrong by however long the
operator paused.

- EmbeddedRunController: new optional onStatusChange(status) prop, fired on real status
  transitions only. Deliberately the ROBOT's status, not the optimistic one — a pause the
  robot hasn't acted on yet still reports `running`, and the clock should keep going until
  it actually stops, not from the moment the button was clicked.
- reagent-filling/+page.svelte: tracks robotStatus, passes paused={robotStatus === 'paused'}.
- RunExecution: accumulates paused time across however many pauses (pausedAccumMs plus the
  currently-open stretch) and subtracts it from elapsed. Remaining is now derived from the
  BUDGET (runEndTime - runStartTime) minus adjusted elapsed, rather than from the wall-clock
  end stamp — so a pause pushes the projected finish out instead of eating the estimate.
  While paused the readout goes yellow and says "paused — clock held", and once any pause
  has happened it shows "Not counting N min paused" so the held time is visible rather than
  looking like a frozen/broken timer.

Wax has no run timer (its RunExecution shows only the start clock), so this is reagent-only.

npm run check: 11 errors (baseline, unchanged). npm run test:unit: 80 passed. npm run build: clean.

Deployment: production — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb.vercel.app
Source: branch master @ commit 739324ee
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout master at commit 739324ee
  (or: git log master --oneline to find it if the branch has since moved on)
What this deployment is for: the reagent run stopwatch holds while the robot is paused, and
paused time no longer counts against the remaining-time estimate.

================================================================================
2026-08-06 — master — daemon: never auto-resume or auto-kill a deliberately-paused run
================================================================================
The two robot-side hazards flagged in the Pause writeup above, now fixed in
scripts/ot2-bridge.py and deployed to all three robots.

Both had the same root problem: by STATUS ALONE, the engine's start-of-run off-deck
"confirm deck loaded" pause and an operator pressing Pause are both just `paused`. The
daemon couldn't tell them apart, so it treated a deliberate pause as a leftover.

The discriminator: run.actions. The OT-2 API documents it as "client-initiated run control
actions, ordered oldest to newest" — so a commanded pause (POST /runs/<id>/actions with
actionType 'pause') lands there, while the engine's own protocol-level wait does NOT. New
pause_was_commanded() reads the LATEST play/pause/stop in that log rather than "has ever
been paused", because the log is append-only: checking for any pause would keep a run
flagged for life and make a genuinely stale off-deck pause later in that run unclearable.
Latest action 'pause' = the operator's most recent instruction was stop. Verified against
the robot's own OpenAPI schema (RunActionType is play|pause|stop|resume-from-recovery|...)
and unit-checked offline over 14 cases incl. null/malformed/empty action logs.

1. execute_auto_resume_run: now refuses to resume when the pause was commanded, and reports
   {"resumed": ..., "declined": ...}. Previously it would undo a Pause pressed inside its
   75s watch window — the same bug the browser had, but server-side where the browser
   can't defend.
2. clear_stale_protocol_run: a commanded pause now raises RobotCommandError like the ACTIVE
   case instead of being silently stopped. Starting a deck scan while a fill was
   deliberately paused used to kill the fill; it now fails loudly telling the operator to
   resume or stop it explicitly. Refactored _current_protocol_run to carry pauseCommanded,
   plus a shared _fetch_run helper.

Evidence this was real, straight off B07's run d2d46890a action log:
  play, play, pause, play, pause, play, pause, stop
i.e. start, auto-resume of the initial pause, then THREE operator pauses each undone by a
play, and finally the operator giving up and stopping the run. Exactly the reported
"I press pause, it keeps going, eventually I have to cancel it".

Deployment (robot-side — NOT a Vercel deploy):
  Deployed: scripts/ot2-bridge.py md5 c7b0e96d... to B07 172.16.28.101, R04 172.16.28.144,
  B14 172.16.28.71 at /data/ot2-bridge/ot2-bridge.py; prior version (md5 b63bad15..., which
  matched git HEAD exactly on all three — no robot-only drift) saved alongside as
  ot2-bridge.py.bak-20260806.
  All three: py_compile OK on device, systemctl restart, active + enabled, scanner port
  reopened, zero errors in the journal since restart. Verified end-to-end by pushing a
  read-only GET /health through the Mongo command queue to each: R04 2.6s, B07 0.9s,
  B14 0.6s round trip. All three robots were idle (stopped/succeeded) before the restart.
  Disk before deploy: B07 18%, R04 6%, B14 18% — no cleanup needed.
  Rollback: cp /data/ot2-bridge/ot2-bridge.py.bak-20260806 /data/ot2-bridge/ot2-bridge.py
           && systemctl restart ot2-bridge

2026-08-06 — master — reagent: pre-select USE TIP CALIBRATION so it isn't ticked every run
================================================================================
Alejandro: "make it so that it automatically uses the tip calibration and I don't have to

Reagent_Filling_GEN7.py declares use_tip_calibration with default=False, and

unchecked every run and the operator ticked it every run.

Checked before changing it, because [[project-b07-calibrator-and-build-state]] records
tip-cal being turned OFF fleet-wide at one point over calibrator flakiness. That is no
longer how the line runs: of the last 60 reagent runs that recorded the flag,
B07 ON=28/OFF=0, B14 ON=23/OFF=1, R04 ON=8/OFF=0 — 59 of 60 ON, and the single OFF
(2026-08-03T14:48, B14) was a Cancelled run. Pre-selecting it matches actual practice.

BOTH ProtocolStartPanel instances — the pre-scan "Set protocol parameters" panel (which had
no contextValues at all, and is the one operators actually use) and the post-scan direct-
start panel. contextValues wins over the .py default when the form seeds.

switching this off is the documented workaround (protocol falls back to nominal well
positions, see the use_tip_calibration branches in the .py), so the operator keeps the
escape hatch. The panel already renders bool params with a hidden input that always posts
true/false, so unticking a pre-selected param correctly posts false instead of being dropped.

Verified the value survives the whole path: the pre-scan panel's FormData is captured and
replayed verbatim into ?/startRun (startRunWithCapturedParams only injects param_cartridges),
and startRun parses param_<name> with `value = s === 'true' || s === 'on'` for bool RTPs.
Frozen constant so the pre-scan panel's contextValues identity is stable — ProtocolStartPanel
re-seeds the entire form when contextValues changes, which would wipe operator edits.

Wax untouched (explicitly scoped to reagent). The .py default stays False, so nothing changes
for anyone driving these protocols from the Opentrons app rather than BIMS.

2026-08-07 — master — wax: pre-select the gate volumes (1.6 / 2.2 / 2.2 / 2.2)
================================================================================
Alejandro: "when you start a wax fill, make it automatically go to wells 2-4 have 2.2 and
well 1 has 1.6 so I don't have to adjust it every time."

Wax_Filling_GEN7_Cartridge.py declares vol_gate1..vol_gate4 all at default=1.60, and
ProtocolStartPanel seeds from the .py's RTP defaults, so the operator retyped three fields
on every wax run.

Confirmed against wax_filling_runs before changing it — every run since 2026-07-28 has been
gate1=1.6, gate2/3/4=2.2 (16 consecutive runs across R04 and B07); everything before that
was the 1.6/1.6/1.6/1.6 protocol default. So these are already the operating values; this
only removes the typing.

Checked the row-alignment hazard from [[project-wax-batch-row-alignment]], since gate
volumes are exactly what un-aligns aspiration batches. It holds at these values, per the
.py's own documented arithmetic (comment above the aspirate_remainder RTP): a cartridge row
is 4 wax wells (gates 4,3,2,1) = 8.2uL at 2.2/2.2/2.2/1.6, and usable-per-trip is
20 - aspirate_remainder, so a whole row fits per trip as long as the remainder is <= 11.8.
Its default is 11.5 and every recent run recorded 11.5. A batch ending mid-row would force
the next to open by crossing a cartridge wall and bend the tip; the protocol also guards
this structurally with the row_key check in the batching loop.

Fix: new WAX_PARAM_DEFAULTS constant on the wax page, spread into contextValues on ALL
THREE ProtocolStartPanel instances (the deck-loaded panel, the pre-scan "Set protocol
parameters" panel, and the orchestrated scan-and-start panel) so the defaults hold whichever
path the run starts from. contextValues wins over the .py default when the form seeds.

NOT added to contextReadonly — gate volumes are the main fill-quality tuning knob and stay
editable per run. dispense_depth deliberately left alone: it is per-robot (B07 needed its
own value to stop tips breaking on dispense, see [[project-b07-wax-dispense-depth]]) and has
no single correct default; recent runs show -1, 0 and 1 across robots.

Value path verified end-to-end as with reagent: the pre-scan panel's FormData is replayed
verbatim into ?/startRun, which parses param_<name> with parseFloat for float RTPs. The .py
defaults stay 1.60, so driving the protocol from the Opentrons app is unaffected.

2026-08-07 — master — reagent run timer: back to a countdown as the headline number
================================================================================
Alejandro, on the run-timer work from 2026-08-06: "can you actually change it to the
countdown instead of stopwatch?"

Swapped the visual hierarchy back — the big number is MM:SS remaining again, with elapsed
demoted to a small line underneath. Everything that made the earlier change necessary is
kept, because the original countdown's problems were never about it being a countdown:

- It still does NOT decide the run is over. Completion comes from the OT-2 terminal status
  (robotFinished), with autoCompleteOnExpiry only for runs that have no opentronsRunId.
  Hitting zero used to fire onTimerComplete and show "Filling Complete" mid-fill.
- Past zero it flips to "+MM:SS over the ~N min estimate — waiting on the robot" in amber
  and keeps counting UP, so an under-estimate reads as an under-estimate rather than as a
  finished run. This is the specific reason a bare countdown was wrong before: the old
  estimate ran ~2x long so it never expired early, but the corrected estimate does.
- It still holds while the robot is paused ("paused — countdown held", yellow), and paused
  time still doesn't burn the estimate down.
- Elapsed is still shown, just secondary: "12:34 elapsed, not counting 3 min paused".

No logic changed — remainingMs/elapsedMs/pastEstimate already existed with these semantics;
this is presentation only. Added countdownMin/Sec and overMin/Sec derivations plus a pad()
helper, and refreshed the two comments that described the stopwatch layout.

npm run check: 11 errors (baseline, unchanged). npm run test:unit: 80 passed. npm run build: clean.

2026-08-13 — feat/magnetometer-stage-calibration — SPU magnetometer stage
calibration tool + live stage position during moves
================================================================================
Alejandro: "try to recover work on the magnetometer calibration feature for the
validation tab. it was a tool to set the location of each read position and create
[the] code that will replace the current code. but its a tool first, that then makes
the fix" — then: "commit both to a working branch off of master. It should also
directly control the spu from the bims so we can see the motion in real time".

Recovered the calibration tool onto a clean branch off origin/master (the work had
been sitting in the validation-runs worktree on an unrelated branch). Two commits:

  8b2b00cf  SPU magnetometer stage calibration tool
  31466a6d  live stage position during calibration moves

The tool is the deliverable, and the constants are its output. The operator homes the
stage, jogs to each of the 8 well read positions, captures each one, and the page
emits the MAGNETOMETER_FIRST_POSITION / STAGE_MICRONS_TO_MAGNETOMETER_START_POSITION
constants to paste into firmware — measured rather than derived, which is the actual
fix for the read positions drifting off the wells.

Real-time motion: stage_control blocks for the whole duration of a move, so its return
value only ever reported the FINAL position. Firmware v90 (separate repo, below) adds
stage_pos + stage_moving cloud variables; because the sketch runs SYSTEM_THREAD(ENABLED)
the Particle system thread keeps answering variable reads while the motion loop is
blocked. BIMS polls them at 400ms through /api/validation/magnetometer/calibrate/live
alongside the move, and the readout switches to a pulsing "moving" live value during
travel. Polling is best-effort by design: stage_control's return value stays
authoritative and a dropped sample never surfaces as a move error.

VALIDATION NOTE — npm run build was NOT run locally (it OOMs at ~30GB on this machine;
the Vercel branch build is the gate and it passed, see below). svelte-check in this
worktree reports 100 pre-existing errors across 115 files, almost all missing-dep noise
(jsqr, bwip-js/browser) because the worktree has no full node_modules. Zero errors in
the magnetometer files — grepped specifically to confirm.

  firmware constants (MAGNETOMETER_FIRST_POSITION / MAGNETOMETER_LAST_POSITION /
  STAGE_MICRONS_TO_MAGNETOMETER_START_POSITION plus the well_move table) for pasting
  into brevitest-firmware. The tool comes first; the firmware fix is its output.
- Direct USB control: src/lib/services/spu-serial.ts drives the stage over Web Serial
  from the browser, so the motion is visible in real time instead of round-tripping
  through the Particle cloud. Cloud transport remains the fallback with no cable.
- Live stage position readout while a jog/goto is in flight.
- "Stage Calibration Tool" link added to the existing /validation/magnetometer page.

svelte-check: 98 errors / 429 warnings — baseline (master's own), 0 in the new files.

2026-08-16 — feat/kanban-tweaks — task detail: back link returns to origin page + autosave on navigate away
================================================================================
Two small kanban task-detail fixes (src/routes/kanban/task/[taskId]/+page.svelte only;
no server changes):

1. "Back to Board" was hardcoded to /kanban, so arriving from Inventory (or Flow,
   Archived, …) always dumped you back on the Queue board. The page now captures the
   in-app origin via afterNavigate — any /kanban/* page except another task detail,
   query string preserved — and the back link (plus the post-archive redirect) returns
   there. Direct/external entry still falls back to /kanban.

2. Edit-form changes now auto-save on navigation away. The form tracks dirty state on
   input; beforeNavigate cancels the pending navigation, POSTs the form to the existing
   ?/update action (x-sveltekit-action + deserialize, same wire format as enhance), and
   resumes the navigation on success. On action failure (e.g. cleared title) the page
   stays put and shows the error banner instead of silently dropping the edit. Tab
   close / hard navigation gets a best-effort navigator.sendBeacon flush. The Save
   Changes button still works and clears the dirty flag.

npm run check: 11 errors = baseline, 0 new (none in the task page).

camera_capture_NO_POST_PROCESSING.py edits — deliberately left untouched, not part of

2026-08-17 — jq/barcode-scanning — Zebra ZT230 cartridge barcode printing via Browser Print
================================================================================
New branch + worktree (..\Bioscale_Operations_System_V2-jq-barcode-scanning) for a run of
barcode-scanning work. First deliverable: print the same cartridge UUID barcodes the Avery
94102 page mints onto a Zebra ZT230 loaded with 2-across ¾" x ¾" roll labels.

Delivery choice: Zebra Browser Print (local agent on the operator PC, printer on USB or
LAN/WiFi) rather than a server push — Vercel can't reach the lab LAN (same constraint the
OT-2 bridge exists for), and Browser Print needs no daemon deployed. Positional
repeatability comes from the ZPL, not the transport: every element is at absolute dot
coordinates from one geometry function, with a per-printer x/y offset and a mint-nothing
alignment label. Built so a queue + lab-side bridge can be added later without rework.

- src/lib/server/services/barcode-print-batch.ts (NEW) — reserve → atomic-claim → confirm
  lifecycle extracted from print-barcodes/+page.server.ts. `medium: 'avery-sheet' |
  'zebra-roll'`: Avery keeps sheet consumption + avery94102SheetsOnHand mirror; Zebra
  records only the `creation` of N labels on PT-CT-106 (labels come off a roll). Same
  5-min PREVIEW_TTL_MS, same distinguishable 409s, same AuditLog row.
- src/routes/manufacturing/print-barcodes/+page.server.ts — rewritten on the service.
  Response shapes unchanged; +page.svelte untouched except copy (it said the page mints
  CART-NNNNNN — it mints UUIDs) and a link to the Zebra page.
- src/lib/zebra/cartridge-label-zpl.ts (NEW, isomorphic, 11 unit tests) — computeGeometry(),
  buildCartridgeLabelsZpl() (one ^XA..^XZ per 2-label row; ^BQN QR of the raw UUID, A/B/C
  marks, UUID text split over two centred lines; text auto-drops if the QR leaves no room),
  buildAlignmentZpl(). Refuses any payload outside [0-9a-f-] so ^/~ can't reach ^FD.
- src/lib/zebra/browser-print.ts (NEW) — talks to the agent's REST API directly
  (https:9101 → http:9100 fallback; /available, /default, /write, /read + ~HS parse).
  No Zebra JS bundle.
- src/routes/manufacturing/print-barcodes/zebra/ (NEW) — ?/mint (1–400 labels) and
  ?/confirm; page has printer picker (remembered), calibration panel (dpi, gap, x/y
  offset, darkness, speed, QR module size), alignment-row button, canvas preview drawn
  from the same geometry, countdown, "Send to printer" → confirm modal, .zpl download
  fallback. Nav: Manufacturing → "Print Barcodes (Zebra)".
- docs/ZEBRA-ZT230-BARCODE-PRINTING.md — setup runbook (agent install, USB vs IP, accept
  origin, media calibration, offset dial-in, operator flow).

Not yet verified against the physical ZT230 — needs Browser Print installed on a lab PC
and the printer on USB or a lab-LAN IP. Assumptions to confirm on first print: 203 dpi
model, 0.125" column gap, agent accepts the text/plain JSON body on /write.

npm run check: 11 errors (baseline, unchanged). npm run test:unit: 91 passed (80 + 11 new).

2026-08-18 — jq/barcode-scanning → master — Zebra ZT230 bench-verified: calibrated defaults, ^LT fix, Content-Type fix
================================================================================
Live session with Jacob at the printer. Ethernet on the ZT230 showed 000.000.000.000 (no
DHCP on that wall port; the printer is not on the 172.16.28.0/24 WiFi subnet either), so we
went USB → Browser Print 1.3.2.489 on Jacob's PC lists it as 52j150301773 (usb). Windows
reports "ZTC ZT230-200dpi ZPL" → 203 dpi; thermal transfer with ribbon.

Findings and fixes, all now on master:
- Browser Print /write rejects a POST with no Content-Type ("Failed to write to device:
  null"); the client now sends text/plain (still a CORS simple request). This would have
  broken the page's Send button on first use.
- Hot-replugging USB crashed the agent (javaw exited, :9100 closed) — relaunch from the
  desktop shortcut; documented (there is no window, only a right-click tray icon).
- The roll is 20 mm × 20 mm labels (sold as ¾"), ~0.13" web; the printer's sensor reports
  161 dots. First alignment row (¾" geometry) fell ~1 mm short on the right/bottom; 20 mm
  geometry lands on the die-cut in both columns.
- Top-of-form sat ~1 mm below the die-cut. A negative Y offset did nothing because ^FO
  clamps at 0 — switched the vertical offset to ^LT (label top, −120..120), ^LT-8 nailed it.
- Layout tuned with Jacob in hand: UUID text 14 → 10 dots (line 2 was on the bottom edge;
  "the udi can be small"), A B C 12 → 15 dots. Approved: "you nailed it" / "looks pretty
  good from an alignment repeatability perspective".
- New: buildFeedZpl (empty ^XA^XZ is ignored by the printer; a 1-dot mark feeds), Feed 2
  rows button on the page, Label size field in the calibration panel, calib localStorage
  key bumped to v2 so cached ¾" values don't override the new baseline.
- New: scripts/zebra-send.ts (align | labels | raw | status | list; --w --h --gap --x --y
  --mag --dark --dry) — the bench tool used for the whole session.
- docs/ZEBRA-ZT230-BARCODE-PRINTING.md: "Bench-verified 2026-08-18" section + gotchas.
- 10 test labels (random UUIDs, NOT minted — told Jacob to discard) printed and scanned OK.

Not yet done: the end-to-end run through the BIMS page itself (mint → Send via agent →
confirm → PT-CT-106 + audit) — waiting on the production build of 3a24b047.

2026-08-18 — master — B07 wax "went to the reagent hole" investigation + tip-calibration guards (opt-in, adjust cap, wax calibration check)
================================================================================
INVESTIGATION (from B07's own run logs, runs 096d4346 / 3831da1d on protocol ec109ceb, plus Mongo
+ deck_calibration_edits): operator saw the wax tip land on the REAGENT hole on the middle carrier
of deck-004 while the Studio positions looked right, and suspected the .py mixes up locations.
- The .py well mapping is correct: destinations [X2,X4,X6,X8]...[X10,X12,X14,X16]... (even = wax);
  carrier 2 used the same tip and the same tip-cal adjust as carrier 1; run labwareOffsets all 0;
  bundled deck-004 == Mongo (freshness gate working). Nothing carrier-specific in the code.
- The tip-cal adjust IS the error: B07 wax runs applied x=-1.5..-3.1mm (y +0.4..+1.1) — the wax
  and reagent holes are only ~3.5mm apart, so the "bend correction" walks the tip onto the reagent
  hole. Cause: wax (p20) and reagent (p300) protocols probe from DIFFERENT start points
  (cal_x-0.6/cal_y-6.5 vs cal_x-1.4/cal_y-7.0) against ONE 'C' baseline that was zeroed for the
  reagent recipe. Missed-probe landmine seen live: 3831da1d's 2nd tip walked 5mm on both axes;
  6edf15cd (08-13, deck-002 baseline -4.5/1.2) dispensed with adjust (-6.8,-1.3).
- Also found: bridge daemon Calibrate-tip uses the WAX recipe for all mounts (0.8mm x bias for
  reagent Studio teaching — explains the B14 07-29 residual); deck-004 carrier 2/3 wax wells were
  group-shifted 08-03 and never per-hole taught (only carrier 1 was, 08-05); deck-003's wax sits on
  the wrong side (-3.9 x) of its reagent partners. The 'C' baseline is per DECK (calibrator = the
  carriage's particle device).

BUILT (protocols/Wax_Filling_GEN7_Cartridge.py, now 22 RTPs):
- use_tip_calibration (default OFF, mirrors reagent): OFF skips the 'C' read + X/Y probe and
  dispenses at the taught wax hole — WYSIWYG with the Deck Calibration Studio.
- max_tip_adjust (default 1.5mm): missed limit switch or |adjust| > cap = REJECTED probe -> retry
  once with a fresh tip -> else pause + continue at nominal (0,0). Never dispenses a rejected adjust.
- run_calibration_check (default OFF): tours 9 wax holes (X2,L6,A8,A10,L14,X16,X18,L22,A24) with
  the exact fill motion incl. adjust, pausing at each, then asks before dispensing.
- dispense_depth comments corrected (default -1.0 = hover 1mm above the rim).
- wax-filling/+page.svelte WAX_PARAM_DEFAULTS pre-selects both bools OFF (server posts only
  schema-declared params, so R04/B14's older uploads are unaffected until they resync).
- scripts/deploy-wax-tipcal-guards.cjs: gated deploy (analysis clean, required RTPs, geometry
  identical to the robot's current wax protocol) + audit log; ROBOT=b07|b14|r04.
- Stored wax OpentronProtocol .py synced to this build (scripts/set-stored-wax-py.cjs, pre-image in
  calibration-backups/) so the run-start auto-resync cannot downgrade the code.
NOTE: R04/B14 will inherit tip-cal OFF the next time the freshness gate re-bundles their wax
protocol — run RUN CALIBRATION CHECK there first. Not yet built: per-mount probe recipes in the
bridge daemon; per-run adjust logging into BIMS.
npm run check: 11 errors — unchanged baseline.

2026-08-18 — master — wax: mid-run tip swap (swap + recalibrate + resume at the same well)
================================================================================
Operator: probes miss the calibrator on some runs / tips fail; wanted to change the tip during a paused
run, calibrate it, and continue exactly where the run was. OT-2 protocols can't take commands mid-run,
so: wax run page "Tip problem?" card (running or paused) -> action requestTipSwap -> Ot2BridgeCommand
kind tip_swap_request (new enum) -> daemon execute_tip_swap_request writes
/data/ot2-bridge/tip-swap-request.json -> the wax protocol polls it before every dispense + at batch
start. On request: blow-out un-dispensed wax to source; 'rack' = drop + next tracked tip; 'hand' =
raise 60mm over calibrator + pause "pull old tip off, push new one on, Resume" (pick_up=False path
keeps the API's tip model); re-probe (no-tip/cap guards apply); re-aspirate; continue at the
interrupted well. Stale request cleared at run start; Cancel button removes an un-acted request.
npm run check: 11 errors — baseline.
