2026-08-19 — feat/scan-then-check-v2 — wax deck: scan-then-check deferred validation (ported onto current master)
================================================================================
Validating every cartridge scan round-tripped to the server and made wax deck loading crawl. The deck
grid now accepts scans locally without judgement and validates the whole set at the deferred boundary
(last slot filled, or the next action taken), so the operator scans at wedge-scanner speed and sees
failures once, at the end.

checkCartridgeScans() in src/lib/server/manufacturing/cartridge-scan-check.ts does the local checks
(duplicate, over-capacity) in memory plus one CartridgeRecord.find({_id:{$in}}) for the entire set and
returns per-barcode flags; it is read-only. POST /api/manufacturing/cartridge-scan-check exposes it as
a batch endpoint. wax-filling/DeckLoadingGrid.svelte drops the per-scan blocking rejects, keeps the
scan input open past capacity so an extra scan lands and gets flagged instead of being silently
dropped, and renders per-slot failures with mismatch reconciliation.

Lineage note: this replaces the earlier feat/scan-then-check branch, which was cut 2026-03-16 and is
now 1650 commits behind master. That branch edited src/routes/spu/manufacturing/{wax,reagent}-filling/
— paths master has since moved to src/routes/manufacturing/cart-mfg/ — and its versions of both
DeckLoadingGrid components predate Jacob's May–June robot deck-scan / sweep-progress / QR-alias
rewrites (wax grid 490 -> 1273 lines). Merging it would have reverted that work; it should not be
merged and is superseded by this branch.

Still NOT ported from the old branch, deliberately, and open as follow-up work:
  - reagent-filling DeckLoadingGrid deferred validation
  - TopSealing deferred validation + batched completeSealBatch (master still has scanCartridgeForSeal)
  - wax QC completeQCBatch — NOT needed: master's cart-mfg/opentron-control/wax/[runId] already
    batches QC, already reads r.reasonCode, and already applies rejections before the phase loops,
    so both wax-QC bugs the old commit fixed are independently fixed on master.

Merged current master (through the mid-run tip swap work) into the branch first; no conflicts, and
master's 6 new commits do not touch the wax deck grid.
npm run check: 11 errors / 434 warnings — exact baseline, zero errors in any touched file.

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-fyqgpd52k-brevitest.vercel.app
  (also aliased: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-opejkb67i.vercel.app)
  Branch git-alias not resolvable from CLI (branch name too long — Vercel truncates + hashes it);
  read it off the inspector if needed: https://vercel.com/brevitest/bioscale-operations-system-mongodb/GZEgUne4upLiTA1WNbeUzqqix9SC

2026-08-19 — feat/deck-calibration-hardening — deck calibration audit: version control, deck identity binding, staged per-robot rollout
================================================================================
Full audit of the cartridge-deck calibration path (BIMS -> Mongo -> deck JSON -> OT-2)
after repeated "calibrated deck fills in the wrong place" reports. Branch cut off
origin/master @ 916126b91.

AUDIT FINDINGS (read on master, not inferred). Deck geometry never travels at run
time — it rides only inside the protocol upload bundle, as ABSOLUTE well coordinates
in labware_definitions (BIMS deliberately does not use Opentrons labwareOffsets; see
CALIB-3). Root causes found:
  A. Deck identity was never bound. Operator picks DECK-004; the robot picks its
     definition from a Particle id read over USB serial. Nothing compared them.
     deck_calibration_edits.deckEquipmentId was null on all 47,340 rows.
  B. Maintenance-run labware reuse matched on loadName only (maintenance.ts), and
     edits never bumped version — so after a deck edit the robot kept serving
     pre-edit geometry while the Studio showed the new numbers, and the operator's
     second jog silently doubled the correction. (Was already known: progress.txt
     "close and reopen the maintenance run after every deck edit".)
  C. Editing never bumped version. Opentrons keys definition identity to
     namespace/loadName/version; changing geometry at a fixed version is how stale
     calibration gets applied to new coordinates.
  D. DECK-001's own definition is wrong (unchanged here — needs one jog measurement;
     288 wax wells deliberately NOT touched, per docs/DECK001-WAX-ALIGNMENT-TEST.md).
  E. Every upload bundled all 19 labware defs, each named <loadName>.json — two defs
     sharing a loadName would collide on multipart filename with an undefined winner.
     Prod has no duplicates today, so this never fired.
  F. opentrons_run_records was EMPTY — no run ever recorded which geometry it used.

NOT root causes (corrected mid-audit): run-start staleness is already solved on master
by ensureFreshRunProtocol (protocol-freshness.ts), wired into both fill routes; and
calibration-baseline.cjs is NOT dangerous — its writes are gated behind RESTORE_APPLY=1.

BUILT
  + src/lib/server/db/models/deck-version.ts — immutable deck_versions collection
  + src/lib/server/services/deck-calibration/deck-versions.ts — publish / rollback /
    list. Publish bumps version and freezes the blob + sha256; rollback republishes an
    old snapshot FORWARD as a new higher version (never rewrites history).
  + .../deck-calibration/resolve.ts — one deterministic loadName -> definition
    resolver, replacing 6 ambiguous findOne({loadName}) sites.
  + .../deck-calibration/definition-hash.ts — dependency-free canonical sha256 shared
    by app and migration scripts.
  + .../deck-calibration/run-guard.ts — resolves the deck a run will use; refuses an
    unbound/dangling/archived deck when enforcement is armed.
  + .../deck-calibration/rollout.ts — DECK_HARDENING_ROBOT_IDS per-robot opt-in.
  + src/lib/server/opentrons/labware-refs.ts — parses load_labware() calls and the
    Particle-id -> deck map straight out of the .py (comment-stripped, so retired
    decks 005/006 are excluded).
  ~ proxy.ts robotUploadProtocol — bundles only what the protocol loads (6 defs, not
    19) and hard-fails on ambiguity or filename collision.
  ~ maintenance.ts loadLabwareInRun — reuse compares the full ns/loadName/version URI.
  ~ apply-edit.ts — resolver-based lookups, writes target the resolved _id, each edit
    marks the deck unpublished.
  ~ equipment.ts — deckLoadName, particleDeviceId, archivedAt/By/Reason.
  ~ opentrons-run-record.ts — deckGeometry provenance subdoc.
  ~ wax-filling + reagent-filling startRun — deck guard + run-record provenance.
  ~ deck-calibration/+page.server.ts — publishDeck / rollbackDeck actions; Sync now
    freezes a version before uploading; load exposes version history.
  ~ calibration-baseline.cjs — restore now sets hasUnpublishedEdits so restored
    geometry gets its own version on the next Sync.
  + scripts/backfill-deck-versions.ts, scripts/deck-registry-bind.ts (--plan/--apply)

STAGED ROLLOUT — nothing is on by default. DECK_HARDENING_ROBOT_IDS names the robots
the new behaviour applies to; unset means every robot keeps today's exact code path,
which is what makes this branch safe to merge before it is safe to enable. Run-record
provenance is deliberately NOT gated (it changes no behaviour and is how you see
whether the guard would have fired before arming it).

PRODUCTION SAFETY — verified, not assumed. Every field the migrations write was
grepped against origin/master: equipment.deckLoadName (on master that name belongs to
deck_calibration_edits), equipment.particleDeviceId (belongs to the SPU/Particle
models), equipment.archivedAt (never used with equipment; deck pickers don't filter on
it), lastPublishedVersion / hasUnpublishedEdits / deck_versions (all new). Production
reads none of them. A version bump also cannot disturb other robots: the production
freshness gate compares well coordinates, and publishing changes version, not coords.

MIGRATIONS — DRY-RUN ONLY, nothing applied to production yet.
  backfill-deck-versions --plan: would freeze 5 baselines at v1 —
    gen4deck_gen7cartridge_001/002/003/004 (576 wells each), robotarm_cartridge_deck_001 (24)
  deck-registry-bind --plan: bind DECK-001..004 to their loadName + Particle id
    (parsed from the .py), archive DECK-005/006 (no definition, 0 run references,
    lastUsed never — matches the protocols' own "decks 005/006 retired" comment).
    Archive, never delete.

npm run check: 73 errors / 434 warnings — identical to the master baseline, zero
errors in any changed file.

Diagram of the full datapath (artifact):
https://claude.ai/code/artifact/90794613-a1e4-4edf-9e19-661ea3b1ddb2

NOT DONE / NEXT
  - No physical robot exercised. Version round-trip should be proven on
    robotarm_cartridge_deck_001 first (24 wells, not a production fill deck).
  - Deck->Particle bindings follow the existing numbering convention; the convention
    is now written down and checkable but has NOT been measured. equipment.currentRobotId
    is null on all 6 deck rows, so the DB does not know which deck sits on which robot.
    Confirm the deck physically on R04 before trusting its binding.
  - A Vercel preview shares the production database and drives real robots — preview
    isolates code, not data. R04 is itself production equipment.
  - DECK-001 wax geometry still unresolved (Candidate A vs B unsettled).
  - No UI yet for the version history / rollback actions; server actions only.

================================================================================
2026-08-19 — feat/deck-calibration-hardening — global robot offsets RETIRED; deck JSON is the single source of truth
================================================================================
Follow-on from the deck-calibration audit, after tracing the live "wax lands further
right than it should" reports to their cause.

WHAT WAS ACTUALLY WRONG (evidence, not inference)
  17:23 today  +1.0mm X applied to 12 REAGENT wells (row X) of gen4deck_gen7cartridge_001
  17:24 today  R04 global robot offset set to {x:1, y:0, z:0}
The same 1mm correction was entered TWICE, in two different layers, one minute apart.
The deck edit moved 12 reagent wells; the global offset moves ALL 576 wells plus the
tube rack and tip rack. Verified against docs/deck001-live-labware-def-2026-08-19.json
(this morning's snapshot): reagent X1 59.655 -> 60.655 and X3 53.655 -> 54.655, while
wax X2/X4 did not move at all. So the wax holes shifted +1mm right purely as collateral
from the global offset — exactly the reported symptom, and unintended.

KEY MECHANISM: the Deck Calibration Studio jogs through the maintenance API
(maintenance.ts), which reads the labware definition and applies NO global offset.
A production run applies one via set_offset. So the position an operator physically
verifies in the Studio was NOT the position the run used. Confirmed by grep: nothing
in maintenance.ts reads RobotDeckOffset.

PRECEDENT: same mistake, third occurrence. B14 was set to {-0.1,-2.4,0} on 07-01,
caused the 07-08 deck-004 fill misses, zeroed 07-09 with the note "decks used on B14
are Studio-tuned on B14, so the global offset double-counts".

CHANGED
  ~ calibration-rtps.ts — bims_native forced TRUE, offset_x/y/z forced 0. Forcing
    bims_native true is load-bearing: with it false the .py falls back to its hardcoded
    ROBOT_OFFSETS table, which is NOT all zeros (hidden-leaf/B07 = 0.15,-0.25,-1.3), so
    deleting the rows would REINTRODUCE offsets. cal_x/cal_y/z_cal are kept — those are
    where the tip-calibrator fixture physically sits, not a hole correction.
  ~ deck-calibration/+page.server.ts — saveRobotOffset now REFUSES any non-zero offset.
  + scripts/zero-robot-offsets.ts (--plan/--apply)

APPLIED TO PRODUCTION
  npx tsx scripts/zero-robot-offsets.ts --apply
    Robot 2 R04  {x:1,y:0,z:0} -> {x:0,y:0,z:0}   (audit action=retire_global_offset)
    Robot 1 B14  already 0,0,0
    Robot 3 B07  already 0,0,0 (reference)
  Live immediately — production reads robot_deck_offsets at run start, so the next fill
  gets set_offset(0,0,0) with no deploy required. Reversible: the prior value is in the
  audit row.

DECK GEOMETRY — EARLIER CLAIM WITHDRAWN
  An earlier note in this session said decks 001/004 had wax "~3.3mm too far right".
  That was WRONG — an artifact of computing a deck-wide wax->reagent statistic while
  ignoring cartridge orientation. Rows band in threes (A-C, D-F, ... V-X) = one cartridge
  each, and orientation ALTERNATES: bands ABC/GHI/MNO/STU run +x, DEF/JKL/PQR/VWX run -x.
  Within each band the wax->reagent offset is a consistent ~3.0mm with the sign flipping,
  i.e. wax midway between reagent columns, which is correct.
    deck 001  +2.4..+3.5 / -2.8..-3.5   correct (band spreads up to 2.0mm — loosest)
    deck 004  +3.1..+3.2 / -2.5..-3.2   correct, tightest (spreads <= 0.8mm)
    deck 003  -4.4 / -9.9   wax wells uniformly ~7.4mm displaced vs reagent
    deck 002  +27.5 / +21.4 wax wells uniformly ~24.5mm displaced vs reagent
  Decks 002/003 look bulk-shifted by a single constant (a bad global apply), not drifted.
  NOT corrected — needs one jog measurement per deck to confirm before touching 288 wells.

STILL OPEN — per-tip calibration cannot be enabled yet
  The target model is: deck JSON = only source of hole positions; per-tip probe = only
  correction. The protocol already has the switch (use_tip_calibration RTP, added
  2026-08-18) but the probe math lost two frame terms in the PRD-6 refactor:
    legacy   xOffset = round(x_pos - shift - 150.536 + offset['x'], 1)
    current  xOffset = round(offset['x'] - shift, 1)
  Independently confirmed these are directly comparable: both probe in the carriage
  frame (types.Location(..., carriage)), both apply the result the same way
  (well.top(h).move(Point(adjust.x, adjust.y, 0))), and the probe point is identical —
  legacy hardcodes x_pos=124.581, current computes cal_x-0.6 = 125.181-0.6 = 124.581.
  Missing terms: X 124.581-150.536 = -25.955 ; Y 165.747-177.0 = -11.253.
  Enabling use_tip_calibration today would nudge every dispense ~26mm X / ~11mm Y.
  Restore those terms (expressed via cal_x/cal_y, not re-hardcoded) and bench-validate
  BEFORE turning the per-tip probe on.

================================================================================
2026-08-19 — feat/deck-calibration-hardening — CORRECTION: per-tip probe math is fine; tip calibration forced ON
================================================================================
RETRACTION of the previous entry's "STILL OPEN — per-tip calibration cannot be enabled"
section. That conclusion was WRONG and is withdrawn.

The earlier entry (and docs/DECK001-WAX-ALIGNMENT-TEST.md:167-188) claimed the PRD-6
refactor dropped two carriage-frame constants (X -25.955, Y -11.253) from
pick_up_and_calibrate_tip, and that re-enabling the probe would throw dispenses ~26mm.
The reasoning looked sound — same carriage frame, same application via
well.top(h).move(Point(adjust.x, adjust.y, 0)), and the probe start point is identical
(legacy hardcodes x_pos=124.581; current computes cal_x-0.6 = 125.181-0.6 = 124.581).

It was still wrong. scripts/ot2-bridge.py:1493-1495 documents the removal:
  "adjust = (calibrator C-string baseline) - travel-to-switch. The old hardcoded
   position constant (150.536/177.0) that produced a ~32mm jump is GONE — the C
   string is now the baseline the operator dials in."
So the constants WERE the bug and their removal was deliberate. The current formula
(offset['x'] - shift, where offset is the calibrator's serial 'C' bend reading) is
correct. A sanity check during the analysis had already shown the legacy formula
yielding a ~26-31mm adjust — implausible for a tip-bend correction — and that signal
should have been followed instead of the handoff doc.

ALSO WRONG in the previous framing: per-tip calibration was never disabled by default.
  protocols/Wax_Filling_GEN7_Cartridge.py:109  use_tip_calibration default=True
  protocols/Reagent_Filling_GEN7.py:41         default=False (BIMS overrides to true)
  wax-filling/+page.svelte:197                 use_tip_calibration: true
  reagent-filling/+page.svelte:128             REAGENT_PARAM_DEFAULTS { use_tip_calibration: true }
The "Per-tip calibration DISABLED" line in the DECK001 failing run was that run's own
setting, not a system default.

WHY THIS MATTERS OPERATIONALLY (from the .py's own note at :101-107)
  Deck holes are taught in the Studio WITH "Calibrate tip" active, so the stored
  coordinates are tip-NEUTRAL. Running with the probe OFF does not degrade to
  "uncorrected" — it parks the tip on the REAGENT hole instead of the wax one
  (confirmed B07/deck-004 2026-08-18). Wrong hole, not a small error.

CHANGED
  ~ calibration-rtps.ts — use_tip_calibration forced TRUE alongside the forced-zero
    offsets. It is now a system invariant rather than a form default that can drift.
    A dead calibrator is a STOP condition, not a reason to switch the probe off;
    max_tip_adjust (4.0mm) still rejects an implausible probe per-tip.

RESULTING POSITIONAL MODEL — now exactly as specified
  deck labware definition  = the only source of hole positions
  per-tip calibrator probe = the only correction applied on top, re-measured per tip
  global robot offsets     = forced 0 (rows kept at zero so bims_native stays true)
  protocol ROBOT_OFFSETS   = bypassed

2026-08-19 — feat/deck-calibration-hardening — ROOT CAUSE: Studio taught every deck with the WAX probe recipe
================================================================================
Operator ran reagent filling on R04/DECK-001 after the offset retirement and the tip
still landed right of the hole. Traced it to the real cause, which is NOT in the
BIMS -> Mongo -> upload path audited earlier.

EVIDENCE (run 6d5f0828-11f4-49f9-8b50-ea2da1179f4f, read off R04 directly)
  RTPs were exactly as intended: bims_native true, offset_x/y/z 0,
  use_tip_calibration true, cal_x 125.181, cal_y 173.247, z_cal 40.8.
  Robot log: "Offset calibration: x=-5.0, y=1.8" (C baseline read fine),
             "BIMS-native offsets (applied to ALL labware): x=0.0, y=0.0, z=0.0".
  Applied well offsets on every dispense: {x:-6.5, y:-0.1}.
  Probe travel: X start 123.781 -> 122.381 (1.400mm, 15 steps) => adjust -5.0-1.5 = -6.5
                Y start 165.747 -> 163.947 (1.800mm, 19 steps) => adjust  1.8-1.9 = -0.1
  So the probe ran, the math is correct, and the adjust WAS applied.

  But the Studio's own calibrate_tip on the same robot repeatedly measured
  adjust x = -7.4 / -7.5 / -7.6 (bend x = -5.0, shift 2.4-2.6). Tight cluster,
  not noise — a systematic ~1.0mm disagreement with the fill.

THE CAUSE — three probe recipes, one hardcoded
  Wax_Filling_GEN7_Cartridge.py:697   x_pos = cal_x - 0.6  (124.581), y = cal_y - 6.5
  Reagent_Filling_GEN7.py:617         x_pos = cal_x - 1.4  (123.781), y = cal_y - 7.0
  scripts/ot2-bridge.py:1492          HARDCODED 124.581 / 166.747 for BOTH
  (The Y probe start is identical in both protocols: cal_x + 8.829, cal_y - 7.5.)

  The daemon that backs the Studio's "Calibrate tip" always used the WAX start.
  The probe walks in -X and the adjust is a function of travel-to-switch, so
  starting 0.8mm further left yields 0.8mm less travel and an adjust ~0.8mm less
  negative. Every deck — wax holes AND reagent holes — was therefore taught
  tip-neutral against the WAX probe, while a reagent fill measures against the
  REAGENT probe. Net: reagent holes land ~0.8-1.0mm to the RIGHT. Hole radius is
  0.9mm, so it misses. Wax was self-consistent only by accident: the hardcoded
  numbers ARE the wax recipe, which is why B07/deck-004 wax was confirmed good on
  08-18 and why the wax complaint was the separate +1mm global offset (now zero).

FIXED
  ~ scripts/ot2-bridge.py — CAL_PROBE_RECIPES{wax,reagent} expressed as deltas from
    CAL_NOMINAL_X/Y so they translate onto whatever absolute calibrator point BIMS
    supplies, exactly like the .py does. X probe start now comes from the resolved
    profile; Y probe expressed through the same constants (values unchanged).
    _resolve_cal_profile() prefers an explicit payload profile, falls back to the
    tiprack loadName (20ul = wax, 200ul/biotix = reagent). Deliberately does NOT
    infer from z_cal — B07's zCalWax is 40.15, too close to reagent's 40.8.
  ~ api/scanner/calibrate-tip/+server.ts — sends profile: 'wax' | 'reagent' from
    TIP_FOR_MOUNT (right/p20 = wax, left/p300 = reagent).

  Verified arithmetically: wax -> 124.581/166.747 (unchanged, no regression),
  reagent -> 123.781/166.247 (matches the failing run's observed probe start
  exactly), Y -> 134.010/165.747. Profile resolution unit-checked for explicit,
  tiprack-inferred, and empty cases.

STILL TO DO
  - ot2-bridge.py runs ON the robot at /data/ot2-bridge/ and is deployed over SSH.
    The key (docs/brevitest-opentrons-files-4-21/ot2_ssh_key) is NOT on this machine
    and is not tracked in git, so the daemon fix is NOT yet on R04.
  - R04 still has run 6d5f0828 current and PAUSED — stop it before re-testing.
  - Existing reagent hole coordinates were taught against the wax probe and are
    therefore ~0.8mm too far right. Fixing the daemon corrects future teaching only.
    After deploying, re-run Calibrate tip and jog ONE reagent hole on deck 001 to
    measure the true residual before deciding re-teach vs a one-time X shift on the
    odd-column wells. Do not bulk-apply on the strength of the 0.8mm estimate —
    that is how decks 002/003 ended up 24.5mm and 7.4mm out.

================================================================================
2026-08-19 — feat/deck-calibration-hardening — bridge fix deployed to R04; deck-001 reagent holes corrected -1.0mm

2026-08-04 - feat/robot-arm-page-consolidation - ARM-02 arm page consolidation + live arm camera
================================================================================
PRD docs/prds/ARM-02-arm-page-consolidation-and-camera.md. Two asks: (1) Jog / Runs /
Calibrate stop being siblings on the Cart Mfg nav and become tabs inside the Robot Arm
page; (2) the camera newly mounted on the arm Pi becomes usable in that same window, so
the arm can be driven and watched at once (incl. during leader-follower).
- New src/routes/manufacturing/cart-mfg/robot-arm/+layout.svelte hosts the tab strip
  (Control | Jog | Calibrate | Runs) + +layout.server.ts loads camera status. Because the
  panel is mounted in the LAYOUT, one component instance survives tab navigation - the
  feed does not restart when the operator switches tabs. Cart Mfg +layout.svelte drops the
  three now-nested nav entries.
- New src/lib/components/RobotArmCameraPanel.svelte: camera picker, fps 1/5/10, size
  S/M/L, pause, collapse, fullscreen, snapshot download; prefs persisted to localStorage
  under bims.armCamera.v1.
- Transport is snapshot POLLING through a same-origin proxy
  (src/routes/api/robot-arm/cameras/[name]/snapshot/+server.ts), NOT the Pi's MJPEG
  stream. Two reasons: the browser's per-host connection cap means a hung MJPEG socket
  starves the rest of the page, and the proxy keeps ROBOT_ARM_API_KEY server-side instead
  of shipping it to the client. New robotArmFetchBytes() in robot-arm-client.ts is kept
  separate from robotArmFetch() because the latter JSON.parses every body and would
  corrupt a JPEG.
- Frames preload into a detached Image before swap-in, so a slow/failed frame leaves the
  previous frame up rather than flashing an empty box.

Three silent-failure bugs found and fixed during review, all of the same shape - the UI
would have looked fine while lying:
- localStorage restore ran in an $effect tracking `cameras`, which changes identity on
  every layout invalidation. A background data refresh would have yanked the operator's
  camera selection back to the saved one mid-use. Now guarded to restore once.
- `selected` was seeded once from cameras[0]. If the list was empty at first paint (Pi
  unreachable, or populated by a later invalidate) the panel stayed blank FOREVER with
  cameras sitting in the dropdown. Replaced with a reactive reconcile effect that also
  covers a camera disappearing out from under the operator.
- A deliberately paused feed was about to show the amber "stale" dot, training the
  operator to ignore the one indicator that means something. Paused vs stale are now
  visually distinct and a frozen frame is always labelled.

npm run check: 11 errors - identical to the documented baseline (r2.ts, AskBimsWidget,
assembly, parts); zero in touched files. Warnings 427->426 and files-with-problems 92->91,
i.e. the only delta is the one warning this branch removed. NOT verified against a live
Pi: the CameraStatus interface mirrors robot-arm/src/server/cameras.py CameraWorker
.status() as read, but the field mapping and the rendered tab strip are unproven until the
page is opened with the arm up. On mismatch the panel renders its error path rather than
breaking.

2026-08-04 - feat/robot-arm-page-consolidation - fix 500 on all four Robot Arm tabs
================================================================================
Every Robot Arm tab returned 500 on a full page load. Diagnosis came from the Vercel
runtime log, not from inference: "TypeError: cameras.find is not a function" thrown from
RobotArmCameraPanel during SSR of the arm +layout.svelte.

Root cause: robot-arm/src/server/app.py:857-860 returns an envelope, {"cameras": [...]},
but robot-arm-client.ts:483 declared it as robotArmFetch<CameraStatus[]>. The generic on
robotArmFetch is an unchecked cast with no runtime validation, so the wrong shape was
invisible to `npm run check` - the type said array, the wire said object. The panel then
called cameras.find(...) on an object.

Why it took down the whole page rather than one panel: the camera panel is rendered in the
+layout, so an SSR throw inside it fails every child route. The prior entry's claim that a
field mismatch would "render its error path rather than breaking" was wrong - that guard
only covered the fetch-rejected path (cameras -> []), never a fetch that succeeded with an
unexpected shape. The /__data.json requests returned 200 throughout; only the HTML render
500'd, which is the signature of a component-SSR fault rather than a load fault.

Fix, two layers:
  - robot-arm-client.ts listCameras() now unwraps the envelope and returns
    Array.isArray(body?.cameras) ? body.cameras : [] - correct shape at the boundary.
  - +layout.server.ts additionally requires Array.isArray() before passing cameras
    through, so future drift in the Pi's response degrades to the empty/error state
    instead of 500ing all four tabs.

`npm run check`: 11 errors / 426 warnings / 91 files-with-problems - byte-identical to the
baseline recorded in the entry above; zero errors in touched files.

================================================================================
2026-08-04 - feat/robot-arm-page-consolidation - ARM-02 mode B: optional direct
             MJPEG from the Pi, with permanent snapshot-proxy fallback
================================================================================
Built the second camera transport. The proxy path (every frame round-trips through
a BIMS serverless function) is why the feed reads as stills rather than video; this
adds an opt-in path where the browser opens ONE MJPEG connection to the Pi instead.

Pi side (robot-arm repo, commit 9c2c039, deployed + hash-verified on the Pi at
065efb40708d96b5a876eb00d289c301df00fad3):
  - POST /cameras/auth now RETURNS the stream token in the JSON body, not just as
    a Set-Cookie. A cookie is useless here: the browser is on the BIMS origin and
    the stream is on the Pi's, so a cross-site cookie is exactly what SameSite
    blocks. Token is camera-scoped, cannot move the arm, and expires in minutes.
  - NOT YET LIVE: the service needs `sudo systemctl restart robot-arm.service`,
    which requires interactive auth this session cannot provide.

BIMS side:
  - robot-arm-client.ts: mintStreamToken() wraps POST /cameras/auth.
  - NEW /api/robot-arm/cameras/stream-url: mints a token and returns it with an
    origin. Publishes ROBOT_ARM_PUBLIC_URL, never ROBOT_ARM_BASE_URL - separate
    vars on purpose, so the private/tailnet address stays server-side as before.
    Unset => reports unavailable and the panel keeps polling. Failures are soft
    (json available:false), never 5xx, because the caller has a working fallback.
  - RobotArmCameraPanel.svelte: negotiates once per mount. Probes with a
    TERMINATING snapshot.jpg rather than the stream itself - an <img> pointed at
    multipart/x-mixed-replace has no dependable load event and cannot be timed
    out, so the stream cannot serve as its own reachability test. On success,
    src becomes the Pi's stream.mjpg; any error at any later point falls back to
    proxy permanently. No crossorigin attribute (would demand CORS the Pi does
    not send). Pause/collapse clears src, which is what actually closes the
    socket - otherwise "pause" would stop the picture while the Pi kept encoding.
  - Honesty fixes that fall out of it: the fps selector is DISABLED in direct mode
    (it sets the polling rate, which no longer exists) rather than silently doing
    nothing; the frame counter is omitted in direct mode because the browser owns
    that socket and we genuinely cannot count its frames; staleness switches to
    the Pi's own server-computed flag, since a client-side clock cannot see a
    frozen direct stream.

INERT BY DEFAULT: with ROBOT_ARM_PUBLIC_URL unset, behaviour is byte-identical to
before - stream-url reports unavailable and every viewer polls the proxy.

OPEN DECISION, flagged not resolved: ARM-01 PRD s7.6 consequence 1 states "Nothing
client-side may reference the Pi ... no Pi hostname rendered into an <img src>".
Mode B breaks the letter of that rule. It preserves the REASON (G0 "any device":
proxy stays the default and the fallback, so a phone on cellular is unaffected),
but the origin an operator would set is the Funnel FQDN, which discloses the
tailnet name to anyone who can load the arm page. That is the user's invariant to
waive or keep, so the env var is left unset pending their call. The s11 grep check
the PRD promised was never implemented, so nothing enforces this today either way.

`npm run check`: 11 errors / 426 warnings / 91 files-with-problems - identical to
the baseline above; zero errors in touched files.

2026-08-05 - feat/robot-arm-page-consolidation - ARM-02 follow-ups: invariant
             guard, mode-B route tests, scoped jog poll
================================================================================
- Jog tab polled every 2s with invalidateAll(), which re-ran the arm layout load
  too - so the tab where the operator is most likely to be moving the arm was
  also issuing two extra Pi round trips (camera list + preflight) every two
  seconds. Load now declares a dependency; the poll invalidates only that.
  NOT fixed: use:enhance still defaults to invalidateAll() on submit, so each
  jog click re-runs the layout load. Per-click rather than continuous, and
  narrowing it means hand-written submit handlers across seven forms on a page
  this branch cannot exercise yet.
- NEW src/lib/server/robot-arm-url-invariant.test.ts (27 tests): the check
  ARM-01 s7.6 promised and s11 left unwritten. Enforces value ACCESS rather than
  name MENTION - a literal grep for ROBOT_ARM_BASE_URL hits 3 files today (all
  legitimate error-message prose) and would have been unlandable, and an
  unlandable check gets deleted. Masks comments/markup/style, keeps string and
  template-literal bodies as code so bracket access and indirection still bite,
  and uses a non-alphanumeric boundary rather than \b so PUBLIC_ROBOT_ARM_BASE_URL
  cannot sneak through on the underscore. Explicitly asserts ROBOT_ARM_PUBLIC_URL
  stays ALLOWED, including testing the compiled regex against it, so a later
  broadening to /ROBOT_ARM_\w*URL/ fails loudly instead of silently killing mode B.
- NEW src/routes/api/robot-arm/cameras/stream-url/server.test.ts (15 tests):
  pins the soft-failure contract - env unset, Pi throws, Pi returns no
  stream_token (older build) all yield {available:false} and never a 5xx, and the
  happy path sets a no-store Cache-Control because the body carries a credential.
- tests/vitest.unit.config.ts gains a stub for $env/dynamic/private so route
  handlers can be imported without SvelteKit's vite plugin. package.json gains
  test:invariants.

`npm run test:unit`: 90 passed (4 files) - run directly, not taken on report.
`npm run check`: 11 errors / 426 warnings / 91 files - baseline unchanged, zero
in touched files.

FOUND, NOT FIXED - pre-existing leak of the PRIVATE origin to the browser:
  control/+page.server.ts:206  armBaseUrl: env.ROBOT_ARM_BASE_URL ?? null
consumed at control/+page.svelte:61 and :422 as baseUrl={data.armBaseUrl}. This
is returned from a load function, so it is serialized into page data and readable
in the browser. It predates ARM-02 (it came in with ARM-01's connection panel,
whose inline comment defends it as "not a secret" - true of the KEY, but s7.6.1
is about the ADDRESS). Consequence worth stating: the tailnet FQDN was ALREADY
reaching browsers on the Control tab before mode B, so mode B's disclosure is
much less incremental than assumed when the exception was waived. Left alone
because the fix is a UX decision (drop the diagnostic, or switch it to the
sanctioned ROBOT_ARM_PUBLIC_URL), not a mechanical one. The new invariant test
does NOT catch it - +page.server.ts is outside the client scan surface.

2026-08-05 - feat/robot-arm-page-consolidation - fix spurious first-load fallback
             to proxy in the camera transport negotiation
================================================================================
First real end-to-end run of ARM-02 mode B. It worked, but the Vercel access log
showed it worked by accident:
  12:52:10.41  GET /api/robot-arm/cameras/stream-url   200   <- negotiated
  12:52:10.41  GET .../cameras/external/snapshot       200   <- proxy starts (by design)
  12:52:16/17/18  .../snapshot 200 x3                        <- STILL polling 6-8s later
  12:52:40.81  stream-url 200 + snapshot 200                 <- remount, 2nd negotiation
  (nothing after; page active until 12:52:49)                <- direct engaged
Snapshot traffic stopping dead is the signature of direct mode, since those
frames then go browser->Pi and never touch BIMS. So attempt 1 failed and fell
back; only a remount (triggered by the operator interacting with a form) reached
direct. On a plain page load the feed would have stayed on proxy indefinitely.

Not a server problem: timed the exact probe URL through the public Funnel three
times - connect 0.001s, TLS 0.02s, total 0.022-0.030s, HTTP 200. The 3s probe
budget was never about the origin being slow, it was about first-request TLS/DNS
setup from a cold browser.

Fix: probe timeout 3s -> 8s, and up to 3 attempts with backoff (1.5s, 3s),
minting a fresh token each pass rather than reusing one that may itself have been
the failure. Retrying is safe because it is not load-bearing - proxy is streaming
the whole time, so a slow probe costs stills, not blackness. Still bounded, and
an available:false answer (no public origin configured, or the Pi cannot mint)
returns immediately instead of burning attempts, since retrying cannot change it.

`npm run check`: 11 errors / 426 warnings / 91 files - baseline unchanged, zero in
touched files. `npm run test:unit`: 90 passed.

2026-08-20 — feat/deck-calibration-hardening — WAX root cause: every probe rejected by max_tip_adjust
================================================================================
Reagent was fixed 08-19 (probe-recipe mismatch). Wax was still landing right of the
hole. DIFFERENT bug — not the probe recipe, which is self-consistent for wax.

EVIDENCE — R04 wax run c8cd6298, read off the robot
  Dispenses into X2/X4/X6/X8 carried wellLocation offset {x:0, y:0} — NO tip adjust
  at all, where the reagent run had carried {x:-6.5, y:-0.1}. The robot's own log:
    "Offset calibration: x=-5.0, y=1.8"
    "Tip calibration REJECTED (adjust (-6.0, -0.0) exceeds max_tip_adjust 4.0mm)
     — retrying with a fresh tip."
    "TIP TRACKER: consumed tip G12 (retry) — index 95"
    "Continuing with adjust x=0.0, y=0.0 (nominal wax hole positions)."
    "Dispensed 2.2uL into well X2 (tip #1)"
  X probe start 124.581 = cal_x - 0.6 = the wax recipe, so the recipe was correct.

WHY THE ADJUST IS "TOO BIG"
  The cap is compared against the RAW adjust, which is
  `calibrator C-string baseline - travel-to-switch`, so it carries the fixture's
  dialled baseline. R04's baseline is -5.0, making a NORMAL wax adjust about -6.0;
  the 4.0 default rejected all of them. B07's baseline is -1.0 and its normal wax
  adjust ~-2.2, which is why wax looked fine there and why one global cap cannot be
  tight enough for B07 and loose enough for R04 at once.
  Falling back to nominal is not "uncorrected" — the holes were taught WITH the
  probe adjust applied, so nominal is ~5.7mm off, to the right. Exactly the symptom.
  NOTE: this is the cap hypothesis raised on 08-19 and then wrongly dropped when the
  reagent protocol turned out to declare no cap. Both were true; two separate bugs.

FIXED — per-robot cap, not a global loosening
  ~ Wax_Filling_GEN7_Cartridge.py — max_tip_adjust ceiling 5.0 -> 12.0. Default stays
    4.0, so a robot BIMS has no value for is unchanged. (5.0 was itself below R04's
    normal 6.0, so even the old maximum could not have unblocked it.)
  ~ tip-calibrator-fixture.ts — maxTipAdjust (Number, default null), per robot.
  ~ calibration-rtps.ts — injects max_tip_adjust from the fixture, CLAMPED to the
    ceiling the uploaded protocol actually declares (parametersSchema carries min/max).
    A robot still on the old .py therefore gets 5.0 rather than a failed POST /runs.
  + scripts/set-tip-adjust-cap.ts — sets the cap AND refreshes the stored wax
    protocol source, refusing to run unless the local .py carries the raised ceiling.

APPLIED TO PRODUCTION
  npx tsx scripts/set-tip-adjust-cap.ts --robot CCyX8FjTRGvYOd9vISGvi --cap 8 --apply
    R04 maxTipAdjust: unset -> 8   (normal adjust ~6.0; a missed probe is ~-10.0,
    since a miss walks the full 5mm, so 8 still rejects a genuine bad probe)
    stored wax protocol source refreshed c6868c3de06d -> 9aded7ff07ac
  B07 and B14 left unset — they keep the 4.0 default and their tight guard.

SEQUENCE THAT MATTERS
  The robot must receive the raised-ceiling .py BEFORE BIMS sends 8.0, or POST /runs
  would reject an out-of-range value. The clamp makes that ordering forgiving, but
  the wax protocol must still be SYNCED to R04 for the fix to take effect — the
  run-start freshness gate compares WELL COORDINATES only and will not notice that
  the .py changed.

NOT a coordinate problem: the wax hole geometry was never touched and must not be.

--- addendum, same day ---
Two further changes under the "deck is the only source of position" rule:

1. NO NOMINAL FALLBACK (wax). pick_up_and_calibrate_tip used to pause and offer
   "Resume to CONTINUE AT NOMINAL", then return adjust {0,0}. That reads like a safe
   degraded mode and is the opposite: the holes are taught tip-neutral, so nominal is
   a KNOWN-WRONG position ~5.7mm off, not an uncorrected one. It is what silently put
   288 holes in the wrong place on 2026-08-20. Now raises instead — if the probe
   cannot produce a correction there is no legitimate position, so the run stops.
   Reagent needed no equivalent: its only zero-adjust return is the
   use_tip_calibration=False path, and BIMS forces that true.
   The remaining zero-returns in wax are that same disabled path (unreachable) and a
   serial-write failure that returns a FAILED probe tuple into the retry/raise logic.

2. FILENAME REGRESSION FIXED. scripts/ot2-bridge.py had been renamed on disk to
   scripts/ot2-bridge-new.py.py while preparing the Jupyter upload, and a `git add -A`
   in 472540abe committed the rename — so master briefly carried the daemon under the
   wrong name while OT2-BRIDGE-DEPLOYMENT.md still referenced scripts/ot2-bridge.py.
   Content was always correct (81114 bytes, CAL_PROBE_RECIPES present); only the path
   was wrong. Renamed back.

auto_resume_run was investigated and CLEARED: it resumes only the engine's own
start-of-run pause and declines operator/protocol-commanded pauses
(pause_was_commanded), so it was not dismissing the calibration-reject prompt.

--- addendum 2, same day: last position influence removed ---
Audited EVERY X/Y influence in both fill protocols (grep for set_offset and
.move(types.Point(...))). Result: every well move is well.top(...) — the JSON
position — plus the per-tip `adjust`, and nothing else. The complete list:
  .move(Point(adjust.x, adjust.y, 0))   the tip calibrator — the one allowed corrector
  set_offset(robot_offsets)             carriage + tuberack + tiprack; BIMS forces 0,0,0
  ROBOT_OFFSETS hardcoded table         per hostname, ALL labware — NOW ZEROED
  cornerOffsetFromSlot                  0,0,0 on all four decks
  Opentrons LPC labwareOffsets          never sent; BIMS does not use them

ZEROED the hardcoded ROBOT_OFFSETS fallback in both protocols. B07 carried
0.15/-0.25/-1.3 (wax) and 0.2/-0.35/-0.35 (reagent). Dormant, because BIMS forces
bims_native=True and sends zeros — but if that injection ever failed to reach a
protocol the table would move all of B07's labware, including -1.3mm in Z on wax.
Both tables are now all-zero with a comment telling the next person not to
"calibrate a new robot" by adding numbers there; the Studio is what the fill uses.

+ scripts/refresh-protocol-source.ts — syncs a repo .py into the stored
  OpentronProtocol.fileContent. Needed because BIMS uploads from the STORED source
  and the run-start freshness gate compares WELL COORDINATES only, so a .py edit is
  invisible to it: refresh the source, then Sync to push it.
  Applied: wax 61a0322f -> d898d238, reagent 1d4d629d -> be37b43e.

Z-axis dispense mechanics (jump_height, well_z_depth, dispense_depth, touch_tip
v_offset) deliberately left alone — they set how deep the tip goes into a hole, not
which hole it goes to.

Jacob reported the BIMS connector showing "This connector has no tools available" — and
recalled someone mentioning it BEFORE today's roadmap deploy. Root cause confirmed by
bisection (local dev server + pre-KB2-27 file + zod construct isolation): NOT the key

zero tools — broken for two days, masked as a caching quirk.

the handler; MCP version 3.2.1. Verified locally: tools/list returns 59 tools incl.
kanban_roadmap + the plan tools. npm run check: 11/434 baseline.

2026-08-21 — feat/spu-mfg-tabs — SPU Mfg gets a section tab strip (Assembly / Work
Instructions / Validation), consolidated out of the left nav
================================================================================
SPU Mfg was a bare page while Cart Mfg (/manufacturing/cart-mfg) had a proper tab
strip across its process steps. SPU Assembly, Work Instructions and Validation were
loose siblings in the left "Manufacturing" nav group with nothing tying them to SPU
Mfg. This gives the SPU side the same shape.

New src/lib/components/spu/SpuMfgTabs.svelte — breadcrumb (SPU / SPU Manufacturing /
<section>) plus the tab strip: Overview (/spu/mfg), SPU Assembly (/assembly), Work
Instructions (/documents/instructions), Validation (/validation). Styled to match
cart-mfg's underline tabs. Takes showBreadcrumb so it can sit under a layout that
already renders one.

Deliberately NOT a route move: those four trees keep their own URLs, and the strip is
mounted by each one's layout instead. That keeps every deep link intact
(/assembly/[sessionId], /validation/magnetometer/[sessionId], /documents/instructions/
[id]/run/[runId]) while the tabs stay visible as you move between sections — a move
under /spu/mfg/* would have rewritten every href and redirect in those trees.

Files: NEW src/routes/spu/mfg/+layout.svelte, src/routes/assembly/+layout.svelte,
src/routes/documents/instructions/+layout.svelte (breadcrumb suppressed — the
Documents layout above already renders one, so Work Instructions shows the Documents
tabs and the SPU Mfg strip both; it genuinely belongs to both sections).
MODIFIED src/routes/validation/+layout.svelte — strip replaces its local breadcrumb,
its own Runs/Magnetometer/Thermocouple/Optical sub-tabs stay below (section tabs →
modality tabs). Dropped the now-dead getSectionName/currentSection and fixed a
pre-existing type error there (item.exact was passed to isActive but navItems carry
no exact field).
MODIFIED src/routes/+layout.svelte — SPU Assembly / Work Instructions / Validation
removed from the Manufacturing nav group. NavItem gains an optional sectionPaths[];
SPU Mfg claims ['/assembly','/documents/instructions','/validation'] so the group
still auto-expands and SPU Mfg still highlights anywhere inside the section. The
three ad-hoc prefix matches (group auto-expand, activeLabel, isActive) collapse onto
one matchesItem() helper that honours sectionPaths.

NOTE ON THE BRANCH POINT: this was first built in the home-root clone, which sits on
feat/scan-then-check — 1702 commits behind master, from before the routes moved out
of /spu/ (there: /spu/assembly, /spu/validation, /spu/documents/instructions,
/spu/manufacturing, and the nav in /spu/+layout.svelte). Every href was wrong for
master. Rebuilt from scratch here off origin/master; nothing was cherry-picked.

are artifacts of borrowing pm-test's node_modules via junction (missing @xyflow/svelte

2026-08-19 — feat/tip-calibrator-teach — robot arm: re-register leader BusLinker
================================================================================
The leader BusLinker serial recorded on 2026-08-18 (5C4C128110) matches no board
physically attached to arm-pi. Re-read the board directly over SSH with
`udevadm info -q property -n /dev/ttyACM1`: the attached leader reports
5C4C125867 (VID/PID 1a86:55d3). Confirmed it is a live arm bus, not a stray
CH343 — all 6 STS3215 servos answer at ~12.4 V. The follower (5C4C126808 on
ttyACM0) was already correct and its udev symlink was present.

Because the udev rule keyed on the wrong serial, /dev/buslinker-leader was never
created, and LEADER_PORT in the Pi's .env points at that symlink — so the leader
arm could not be opened at all and teleop could not start.

Files touched (BIMS): scripts/seed-robot-arm.ts — leader serialNumber
5C4C128110 -> 5C4C125867, with the board history in a comment.

DB (production, cluster0): ran `npx tsx scripts/seed-robot-arm.ts`. It upserts by
role, so the existing leader document was updated in place rather than inserted:
robot_arms leader _id -RnCMND5dwA_Zze3XfBoj now serialNumber 5C4C125867.
Still 2 arms / 12 servos — no duplicates. Verified with a read-only probe after
the write.

Companion changes in the robot-arm repo (branch fix/leader-buslinker-swap,
uncommitted pending review): deploy/pi/udev/99-buslinker.rules, src/server/app.py
(_KNOWN_ARM_SERIALS), docs/pi-deployment.md, docs/PRD_BIMS_robot_arm.md.
app.py + the rule file were copied to arm-pi and the service restarted;
/ports/status now reports the new board with expected_role "leader".

STILL OPEN: installing the udev rule into /etc/udev/rules.d/ needs a sudo
password on arm-pi (only `systemctl {start,stop,restart} robot-arm` and
`daemon-reload` are NOPASSWD there). Until that is run by hand,
/dev/buslinker-leader does not exist and the leader arm stays unusable.
No deployment created for this entry.

2026-08-19 — Deck floor from labware dimensions + guarded production calibrator
================================================================================

Branch: feat/tip-calibrator-teach
Commit: 7253d1ab5155ddba132922347f4277dcbd4d1a96
Preview: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-feat-ti-528704-brevitest.vercel.app
Deployment: dpl_34muEmrsxsumhKfDh7hJCXUmHZfX (Preview, Ready, 2m build)

Goal: get the new cartridge deck editable for a bench test with the tip
calibrator, where holes must move UP so the pipette dispenses into a raised
location.

Branch recovery + assembly. feat/tip-calibrator-teach was local-only and
unpushed (it plus ~280 lines of uncommitted work and an untracked PRD were the
only copies). Pushed as-is first, then:
  - 1b55eaf7b  committed the uncommitted two-field-Z work (approach Z vs probe Z)
  - 69e0c7868  cherry-picked 32554cf69 "allow fill holes anywhere on the deck"
    from feat/deck-unrestricted-hole-positions. Cherry-pick, NOT merge: that
    branch sits on a far newer master, so merging would have dragged in 261
    commits / 417 files of unrelated work (zebra, kanban, wax tip swap) the day
    before a bench test. The commit itself is 2 files, +23/-50. Applied clean.
  - 7253d1ab5  this entry.
bims-tipcal-recovery-backup/ was a redundant Aug-18 snapshot; every blob in it
verified present in the repo, nothing lost.

1. Floor restored, ceiling deliberately not. Raising a hole and moving it in XY
stay unrestricted — that is the point of the new deck. Below the deck's bottom
face there is no hole to dispense into, only the slot, so a negative Z is now
refused (reject, never clamp). Floor is derived from the deck's own `dimensions`
in the labware JSON, never from its holes: dimensions describes the physical
block, hole x/y/z are locations inside that frame, so a crept hole must not be
able to drag the floor down with it (same reasoning as safeArcZ). Enforced on the
SAVED coord in apply-edit.ts at all three call sites, and on the COMMANDED coord
in moveToWellSafe since a move reads live coords.

2. Production stopped being laxer than the wizard that teaches it.
calibrationRtpValues read the calibrator with a bare finite(), so cal_x/cal_y
accepted the fixture schema's DEFAULT position of 0 — a never-taught row would
send a real fill run to the deck's front-left corner at full confidence — and
z_cal accepted 0/negative/500mm, with finite(null) coercing to a probe depth of
0, straight through the deck. Now uses the same taughtXY() zero-guard and
plausibleZ() envelope as resolveCalibratorPoint, falling back to the .py
defaults (34.491 wax / 40.8 reagent). taughtXY exported for this.

Verification: svelte-check clean on all touched files (the 11 repo-wide errors
are pre-existing, in AskBimsWidget.svelte and assembly/[sessionId]). 14 guard
assertions run against the built module, all passing. resolveCalibratorPoint
itself was already correct — the bug was only that production bypassed it.

KNOWN ISSUES, not fixed here (found by a 4-agent review of the merged tree):
  - Stale labware cache is the one most likely to cost a bench session:
    maintenance.ts:375 reuses labware at a slot by loadName ONLY, and
    apply-edit.ts never bumps `version`. Inside a reused maintenance run an edit
    is silently ignored — the gantry goes to the OLD hole while the UI shows the
    NEW coords, so the operator jogs again and the additive delta DOUBLES, with
    no error anywhere. WORKAROUND until fixed: close and reopen the maintenance
    run after every deck edit, before moving.
  - revertCalibrator restores history without re-running plausibleZ.
  - applyBatch/applyPerWell are the only mutating actions with no AuditLog.
  - startRun on both production fill pages has no requirePermission.
  - CAL_Z_LIMITS.max is still 200mm on the calibrator teach path — unchanged by
    the unrestricted-deck work. If the new deck needs a calibrator taught above
    200mm the form will refuse it.

Left uncommitted on purpose: protocols/Wax_Filling_GEN7_Cartridge.py has a
separate legacy-path X/Y zero restoration (X_ZERO -25.955 / Y_ZERO -11.253,
fixing a +25.955mm bias on the non-bims_native path). Unrelated to this work,
still undefended in the working tree.

2026-08-20 — One-click "Calibrate tip" in the Deck Calibration Studio
================================================================================

Branch: feat/tip-calibrator-teach
Commit: eecaba83a773fc86bef6ad43f1468de22be65c6b
Preview: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-hlbxmo3r4.vercel.app
Deployment: 6010775501 (Preview, success)
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout feat/tip-calibrator-teach at commit eecaba83a773fc86bef6ad43f1468de22be65c6b
What this deployment is for: bench-test the one-click tip calibration flow on a
robot with the calibrator fixture wired.

Goal: quality-of-life. Running a tip calibration was a strict four-click ritual
— open a maintenance run, pick the tip type, "Pick up tip", "Go to calibrator",
then "Calibrate tip". Steps 3 and 4 are mandatory and never vary, but the
Calibrate button was hard-disabled until they were done (!hasTip) and its label
only nagged: "Calibrate tip — pick up a tip first". Worse, forgetting the travel
failed deep in the robot rather than in the UI: ot2-bridge.py
execute_calibrate_tip in ATTACHED mode probes from the pipette's CURRENT
position and does not travel to the fixture itself, so a skipped "Go to
calibrator" ended as "limit switch not reached within 5mm".

Now "Calibrate tip" runs the whole sequence in one click: pick up a tip from the
tiprack that matches WHICH CALIBRATION IS BEING DONE (tipProfile — wax → p20
cosmasanddamian_96_tiprack_20ul, reagent → p300 Biotix 200uL, slot 11 / A1),
travel to the calibrator approach point, then probe.

Files: src/routes/manufacturing/cart-mfg/deck-calibration/+page.svelte only.
No server, endpoint, or ot2-bridge.py changes — every step reuses an endpoint
the page already called.

  - pickUpTipAction / goToCalibrator each owned their own busy/msg handling, so
    they could not be called from inside another sequence. Split the action out
    of the handler into throwing doPickUpTip() / doGoToCalibrator(z) helpers.
    The two standalone buttons are unchanged behaviourally and stay in the UI as
    manual escape hatches.
  - atCalibratorNow(): within CAL_AT_TOLERANCE_MM (3mm) of calX/calY the travel
    is SKIPPED. A tip the operator jogged onto the fixture to TEACH a point is
    the point being taught — re-driving to the stored field values would throw
    that jog away silently, and the daemon probes from current position anyway.
    Forced off after an auto pick-up (pickedUpNow): refreshPosition swallows its
    own errors, so a stale liveX/liveY must never be allowed to skip a move.
  - busy is now held for the WHOLE sequence, not just the probe — this button
    commands gantry motion now, so the jog pad and the other motion buttons
    (all already gated on busy) lock out for its duration.
  - Button: dropped the !hasTip gate, KEPT the !tipProfile gate, label states
    what the click will do, and a hint line under it names the tiprack/point so
    none of the auto-motion is hidden.

Invariants deliberately preserved: tipProfile is still never inferred from the
mount (the pipettes are not fixed to mounts on this fleet — that is the historic
6.309mm probe-depth error), and Approach Z (calZ, travel) is still never the
Probe Z (touch-off depth, owned by the daemon's protected routine).

npm run check: 11 errors / 434 warnings — identical pre-existing baseline, zero
in the touched file.

NOT YET BENCH-VERIFIED. Needs a robot with the calibrator fixture wired:
  - p20/wax with no tip → expect p20 rack A1 slot 11, arc to (calX, calY, calZ),
    probe returns adjust{x,y}, tip stays on.
  - p300/reagent → expect the Biotix rack and the zCalReagent depth.
  - jog ~1mm off the fixture and click again → expect NO re-travel; the hint and
    the status line should both say it is probing from the jogged position.
  - no tip type selected → button disabled, amber hint still shown.

2026-08-21 — Tip calibration panel condensed to the one button
================================================================================

Branch: feat/tip-calibrator-teach
Commit: 7a0340fba7848023dfd44cdea4d9c42b66d8c802
Preview: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-nlupgcv0o-brevitest.vercel.app
Deployment: 6024173094 (Preview, success)
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout feat/tip-calibrator-teach at commit 7a0340fba7848023dfd44cdea4d9c42b66d8c802
What this deployment is for: bench-test the condensed tip panel together with
the one-click Calibrate tip flow from 2026-08-20 (commit eecaba83a).

Follow-on to yesterday's one-click Calibrate tip. The panel still showed the
manual steps that button now performs, so the everyday flow read as three
competing buttons instead of one.

  - "Pick up tip" DELETED, along with its pickUpTipAction wrapper. It did
    nothing calibrateTip doesn't do as step 1, and nothing else on the page
    wants a tip on its own. doPickUpTip stays as the shared step.
  - "Go to calibrator" MOVED into the teach disclosure, relabelled "Go to
    calibrator (park, no probe)". Deliberately NOT deleted: it is the first step
    of the teach loop (park → jog → From live ↧ → Save), and "Calibrate tip"
    would travel to the same point but then spend 1-2 min probing, which
    teaching does not want. Deleting it would have cost this branch the very
    workflow it is named for.
  - calX / calY / Approach Z, the Probe Z block, that park button and the
    From live ↧ / Save → robot row all moved into a native <details> titled
    "Fixture point (teach)", closed by default.

Main view is now: tip status → tip type → one button + its hint. Nothing the
click depends on is hidden silently — the hint under the button always names the
tiprack and the exact point it will drive to, and the inherited-from-global
provenance warning stays in the main view because it is conditional and rare.

Files: src/routes/manufacturing/cart-mfg/deck-calibration/+page.svelte only.
Markup move plus one deleted function; no behaviour change to the sequence
itself, and no server/endpoint/ot2-bridge.py changes.

npm run check: 11 errors / 434 warnings — identical pre-existing baseline. The
two warnings in this file are the deck SVG canvas (lines 1218/1251), untouched.

STILL NOT BENCH-VERIFIED — the 2026-08-20 entry's four test cases are unchanged
and still outstanding. Add one: open "Fixture point (teach)", confirm "Go to
calibrator (park, no probe)" parks WITHOUT probing, and that jog → From live ↧
→ Save still round-trips the calibrator point.

Asked to ship this branch to master. It could not go as-is: merge-base was
2026-07-10, master was +312 commits, and the merge hit 7 conflict hunks across
6 files, all in the calibration path. Not mechanical — master and the branch had
grown OVERLAPPING implementations and each fixed a bug the other still had.
Resolved by intent rather than by side:

  calibration-rtps.ts
    MASTER's offset policy verbatim (bims_native forced true, offsets zeroed,
    use_tip_calibration mandatory, RobotDeckOffset no longer read). The branch
    predated it and still read RobotDeckOffset, which would have REINTRODUCED
    the wrong-hole dispense confirmed on B07/deck-004 2026-08-18.
    BRANCH's guards kept on the calibrator point master retains: taughtXY() /
    plausibleZ() rather than bare finite() reads.

  api/scanner/calibrate-tip/+server.ts
    BRANCH's tipProfile wins; master's TIP_FOR_MOUNT dropped (mount-inference is
    the 6.309mm probe-depth error). MASTER's `profile` payload field (4b3209eb6)
    and strict resolveLabwareDefinition kept.

  deck-calibration/+page.server.ts
    Imports unioned; BRANCH's saveCalibrator wins (range-checks all three Zs).

  deck-calibration/+page.svelte
    MASTER's 409 SLOT_OCCUPIED recovery kept, retargeted tiprackForMount ->
    tiprackForProfile, and it now retakes the busy lock (stopMaintenance /
    startMaintenance clear it in their own finally blocks while the calibrate
    sequence still owns the gantry). BRANCH's doPickUpTip step wraps it.

  scripts/seed-robot-arm.ts — master (leader serial re-read 08-19).
  progress.txt — union.

MASTER BUG FOUND WHILE MERGING, fixed here:
  master carries the "global robot offsets are retired" guard
  (x!==0||y!==0||z!==0 -> fail) INSIDE saveCalibrator, where x/y/z are the
  tip-calibrator FIXTURE POSITION (~125.181, 173.247, 34.491). On master today
  that rejects every legitimate calibrator save. Meanwhile saveRobotOffset — the
  action that actually writes RobotDeckOffset.offset — has NO guard, so the
  thing it was written to block still gets through. Moved to saveRobotOffset,
  master's wording verbatim. WORTH A HUMAN CONFIRMING this reading: it changes
  behaviour on both actions.

npm run check: 84 errors / 434 warnings — identical to master's OWN baseline
(verified by checking out origin/master and running check there). Zero added,
none in any resolved file.

MASTER IS UNTOUCHED. Not merged to master pending a bench test — this tree has
never run on a robot, and it now carries two independent gantry-motion changes
(the one-click sequence and the reconciled offset/probe path) plus the
saveCalibrator guard move. Outstanding tests are the four in the 08-20 entry,
the teach-disclosure test in the 08-21 entry, plus: confirm a calibrator Save
now succeeds, and confirm saveRobotOffset refuses a non-zero offset.

2026-08-21 — feat/restore-tip-pickup — "Pick up tip" restored to the deck-calibration tip panel
================================================================================

Reverses one specific deletion from this morning's panel condense (7a0340fba),
and nothing else. That commit removed the standalone "Pick up tip" button and
its pickUpTipAction wrapper on the reasoning that calibrateTip already picks a
tip up as step 1. It does — but only as the opening move of the 1-2 minute
limit-switch probe, and two flows on this page want a tip with NO calibration
attached:

  - Fill motion. Its own hint still reads "Pick up a tip first to match the real
    fill height", and after the delete there was no button on the page that
    does that. The hint pointed at a control that no longer existed.
  - Move to hole -> Capture offset. nominal and capture have to be taken in the
    same tip frame (the page rejects a capture if nominal.hasTip != hasTip), so
    teaching a hole with a tip on meant sitting through a probe nobody asked
    for, on a fixture that has nothing to do with that hole.

RESTORED
  - pickUpTipAction, byte-for-byte the same guards as calibrateTip's step 1:
    maintenance run open, and a tip type explicitly chosen. Tip type is still
    never inferred from the mount — probe depth differs by 6.309 mm between the
    p20/wax and p300/reagent racks, and f8a07846 exists because of that. The
    success message now says outright that nothing was calibrated, so the two
    buttons cannot be confused after the fact.
  - The button, in the MAIN view directly under "Calibrate tip" — thin,
    outlined, 11px, deliberately subordinate to the bold purple primary. The
    condense was right that three competing buttons read badly; the fix is
    hierarchy, not deletion. Label switches to "Pick up tip (another one)" when
    the page believes a tip is already on.

DELIBERATELY NOT gated on hasTip. That flag is this page's *belief* about the
mount, not a sensor reading — it is set optimistically after a pickup and
cleared only when the run opens or closes. An operator recovering from a
dropped, broken or hand-removed tip (see d76a6165, 48fee8cd) has to be able to
pick another one up without reopening the run, and a hasTip gate would lock
exactly that person out.

UNTOUCHED: "Go to calibrator (park, no probe)" stays in the "Fixture point
(teach)" disclosure where the condense put it, and the disclosure itself is
unchanged. calibrateTip, doPickUpTip, doPickUp and the 409 SLOT_OCCUPIED
recovery are all untouched — this commit adds a caller, it does not change the
sequence.

Files: src/routes/manufacturing/cart-mfg/deck-calibration/+page.svelte only.
No server, endpoint or ot2-bridge.py changes.

  npm run check — 118 errors / 434 warnings, byte-identical to the SAME tree
  with this change stashed (measured both ways, not assumed). Zero errors and
  zero warnings in the touched file. The count reads 118 rather than the 84
  logged for master because this worktree junctions the home-clone's
  node_modules, which is missing bwip-js / qrcode / jsqr; those three missing
  modules and the implicit-any cascade behind them are the whole difference.
  An install artifact, not a regression — the delta from this change is 0.

  (immutable: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-g5eqf2u19-brevitest.vercel.app)
Source: branch feat/restore-tip-pickup @ commit af69cac3a
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout feat/restore-tip-pickup at commit af69cac3a
What this deployment is for: bench-check that "Pick up tip" picks up a tip and
stops there — no travel to the calibrator, no probe — and that Fill motion and
Move to hole -> Capture are reachable with a tip on without running a

NOT BENCH-VERIFIED. Needs a robot with a tiprack in slot 11:
  1. p20/wax selected, no tip -> "Pick up tip (no probe)" picks up from

FIELD NOTE (2026-08-21, immediately after this deploy): first report from the

button was greyed. It is gated on `!pipetteId || busy || !tipProfile`, and the

2026-08-21 — feat/kanban-tweaks → master — KB2-33: dependencies panel on the task page
================================================================================
Per Jacob: dependency data was write-only from the UI — links (KB2-20: blocks/blocked_by/
relates_to + note + author, cycle-guarded) existed and drove the scheduler/canvas, but no
page showed OR edited them; relates_to was visible nowhere. PRD

2026-08-24 — master — reagent partial fill: barcode sweep now respects the cartridge count
================================================================================
Operator: on partial reagent runs the auto barcode scan swept all 24 positions, waiting out the full
scanner read window on every empty slot. Cause: the params step captures the operator's cartridge
count into capturedParamsFd, but reagent DeckLoadingGrid (unlike the wax grid) had no
plannedCartridgeCount prop — sweepCount hardcoded to 24, and the only control was the small Count box
defaulting to 24. The /api/scanner/sweep endpoint already supported maxSlots; the value simply never
reached it. Fix: +page.svelte derives plannedScanCount from capturedParamsFd (param_cartridges,
clamped 1..24) and passes it to the grid; the grid seeds/follows it via $effect (never mid-sweep).
Count box override preserved; the run's real cartridge count is still stamped from the actual scan.
npm run check: 11 errors / 434 warnings — baseline.

Deployment: production — Vercel auto-deploy from master push
Source: branch master @ this commit
What this deployment is for: partial reagent runs scan only the loaded positions instead of timing out on all 24.

2026-08-25 — master — B07 "Engine hung" was a restart storm; daemon now refuses to kill a booting server
================================================================================
Operator couldn't wax fill: persistent Engine hung despite power cycles + server restarts. Diagnosis
from robot journal: nginx answered 502 (backend down) because the server was permanently 'activating'
— it takes ~2-3 min to boot, BIMS labels that window "Engine hung", and every Restart press queued a
bridge restart_robot_server command that killed the boot of the previous one (15 requests 15:26-16:01;
two boots reached "Application startup complete" and were killed 4-8s later). Recovery: expired the 5
queued/claimed restart commands in ot2_bridge_commands, hands off, server up at 16:05 (health/runs
200). Fix: execute_restart_robot_server checks systemctl is-active first — 'activating' => no kill,
returns alreadyStarting + wait message. Deployed to B07 daemon (restarted, active); B14/R04 daemons
get it on their next update. Possible follow-up (not built): health.ts could show "Starting…" instead
of "Engine hung" while the server is activating.

Deployment: robot daemon — B07 /data/ot2-bridge/ot2-bridge.py @ this commit (ot2-bridge restarted 16:08Z)
Source: branch master @ this commit
What this deployment is for: pressing Restart during the 2-3 min boot can no longer wedge the robot.

2026-08-25 — feat/kanban-tweaks — Task-detail back arrow survives refresh
================================================================================
Follow-up to the roadmap batch: Jacob asked for the task-page back arrow to return to wherever you
came from (board / Tier 1 / map). That already worked via the afterNavigate origin capture (all
kanban pages use plain client-side <a> links) — the always-"Back to Board" label was hiding it and

origin and fell back to /kanban. backUrl is now mirrored to sessionStorage (key kanban:taskBackUrl)

2026-08-26 — feat/kanban-milestone-ui — /kanban/roadmap can create milestones (UI)
================================================================================
Milestones were agent-only: the service layer has supported them since KB2-27
(createKanbanItem takes itemType 'milestone' + dueDate + blockedBy, and the KB2-28
scheduler anchors its backward pass to any dated milestone), but the only way to
make one was kanban_capture over MCP. The roadmap's empty state literally told you
to go do it in the Claude app. This adds the missing UI on the page that consumes
them.

New action createMilestone in roadmap/+page.server.ts rides createKanbanItem — the
same service the MCP path uses — so a UI milestone and an agent milestone are the
same shape (Tier 1 'captured', rank appended, tags normalized, links cycle-checked,
AuditLog INSERT written by the service). Due date is required and validated as
YYYY-MM-DD, then parsed as LOCAL midnight to match the scheduler's parseLocalDate
contract; an undated milestone anchors nothing, so it is rejected rather than
silently landing in the unscheduled strip. blockedBy ids are de-duped before the
call; resolveLinks still rejects dangling ids and blocking cycles.

The load function now also returns linkCandidates — non-archived tasks excluding
done/declined — to feed a chain picker.

UI (roadmap/+page.svelte): a dashed "＋ Milestone" chip at the end of the milestone
strip, and a primary "◆ New milestone" button in the empty state (whose copy now
leads with the button and keeps the kanban_file_plan path as the secondary route).
The modal takes title, due date, tags, an optional meaning line, and a blocked_by
chain picker: filter by title/tracking number/tag, checkbox list capped at 200
rows, selected ids held in state (not in the DOM) so filtering a row out of view
cannot silently drop it from the submission, with removable chips for what is
selected. Errors render inside the modal — a page-level banner would have been
behind the z-50 overlay.

Files: src/routes/kanban/roadmap/+page.server.ts, src/routes/kanban/roadmap/+page.svelte.
npm run check: 0 errors and 0 warnings in both touched files. Absolute count read
120 because this worktree borrows node_modules from .worktrees/pm-test, which
predates @xyflow/svelte, chart.js, bwip-js and friends — all real entries in
package.json, so that noise is install drift, not breakage. Vercel installs clean
and remains the real gate.

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-o9xf44kda-brevitest.vercel.app
  (also aliased: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-kp64wx0z5.vercel.app)

2026-08-26 - feat/kanban-milestone-ui - Tier 1 inventory: copy the visible task list
================================================================================
Added a "Copy list" button to the Tier 1 inventory filter bar, next to the
"N options" count. It copies the task list as plain text, one task per line:

  12  Fix wax probe bias  [processed/deliverable]  hardware

Fields are rank, title, [status/itemType], then tags. Empty tags are dropped
before the join rather than left as trailing whitespace.

It copies `filtered`, not `data.tasks` - i.e. exactly what the status
checkboxes, type/origin selects and tag filter are currently showing, in the
displayed order (rank asc, as the load function sorts it). Copying the full
set regardless of filters would hand back a list that doesn't match the screen,
which is the wrong list whenever a filter is on.

navigator.clipboard is the primary path with a hidden-textarea execCommand
fallback, since the Clipboard API needs a secure context and can be denied; if
both fail it sets errorMsg so it fails loudly instead of looking like a no-op.
The button flips to a green check for 2s and is disabled when the filters leave
zero rows.

Files: src/routes/kanban/inventory/+page.svelte (one file, +58 lines).
npm run check: 120 errors / 434 warnings absolute, with zero of either in the
touched file. 120 matches the baseline recorded in the entry above - same cause,
this worktree borrows node_modules from .worktrees/pm-test which predates
@xyflow/svelte, chart.js and bwip-js. Vercel installs clean and stays the gate.

  brevitest-scope one above)

================================================================================
Investigation of "carts intermittently not marked wax filled" found it systemic, four silent paths:
(1) every wax run since 08-26 had cartridgeIds:[] AND no deckId — startRun allowed starting without a
scan, so the robot filled carts no record pointed at; (2) cancel/abort reverted physically-filled
carts to backing (e.g. 08-18 runs: 17 filled carts -> backing); (3) advancement only ever happened in
storeDeckAndComplete ("Confirm Deck Removed" + fridge) — unclicked = 34 carts parked at wax_filling;
(4) the status-filtered bulk update silently skipped carts (gIUsDWCc: 16 of 24 stamped).
Operator decision: the ceremony is gone — a clean completion is the commit. Built: auto-advance in
recordRunFinished (succeeded -> waxFilling stamp + PT-CT-105 + run completed); load-time self-heal
for browser-closed completions; SMART ABORT (robot command log -> per-cart dispensed-well counts vs
row-pattern expectation; fully-filled carts advance, rest revert); startRun untracked-fill guard with
explicit audited "Test fill — no cartridges tracked" checkbox (calibration runs stay possible).
cartridgeIds[i] = deck position i+1 (slot-ordered scans) is the smart-abort mapping assumption.

stuck wax_filling carts from old runs remain — disposition list available on request.

2026-08-28 — master — tip-calibrator fixture re-keyed from ROBOT to DECK (B14/deck-003 restored)
================================================================================
Operator hit a "tip calibrator a foot away" on B14/deck-003 for reagent filling. Investigation:
deck-003's HOLE geometry was untouched (zero deck_calibration_edits since 07-31; reagent grid in
family with 001/004 — same 6mm pitch, z 6.15-8.76 vs 8.20-8.80 and 6.75-9.10). The damage was in
tip_calibrator_fixtures, which was keyed robotId:UNIQUE — one row per robot — while the calibrator
is physically bolted to the cartridge carriage and travels with the DECK. The 08-21 robot-arm
session walked B14's row from (125.181,173.247,38.5)/zReag 40.8 to the arm rig's
(294.88,79.24,23.85)/zReag 23.85 in five saves (all preserved in the doc's history), i.e. 169.7mm x
/ -94mm y = 194mm (~7.6"), probe Z 17mm low. Swapping back could not restore it: the row was gone.

REWORK: fixtures are now keyed by deckKey = the carriage's Particle device id (the same id the .py
reads at run start to pick the deck definition, so geometry and calibrator cannot disagree);
deckLoadName carried for humans; equipment rows already map barcode <-> loadName <-> particle id.
Chose the Particle serial over the deck barcode: the calibrator itself reports the id
(self-identifying), no scanner dependency/5.5s read window, and a barcode label can be swapped
independently of the hardware. Resolution order: deck row -> legacy robot row -> global -> .py
default. Both fill startRun paths pass the deckBinding they already resolve; calibrate-tip and the
Studio save/revert are deck-scoped (Studio now refuses to save a calibrator with no deck selected).
MIGRATION APPLIED (scripts/migrate-calibrator-to-deck.cjs, dry-run default): dropped the stale
robotId_1 unique index; B07->deck-004, R04->deck-001 stamped; B14's arm values moved to a
robotarm_cartridge_deck_001 row; deck-003 recreated from the doc's own 07-20 history entry
(125.181,173.247,38.5 / zWax 34.491 / zReag 40.8) — the point in force for every good B14 reagent
run through 08-14. Verified: any robot + any fill deck resolves the standard point; the arm deck
keeps its own. npm check 11 (baseline); build clean.
NEXT (operator): verify B14 with RUN CALIBRATION CHECK before a production reagent fill. Swapping
the arm rig on/off B14 is now non-destructive in both directions.

Deployment: production — Vercel auto-deploy from master push
Source: branch master @ this commit

2026-08-28 (cont.) — Calibrate tip on B14+deck-003 still drove to the robot-arm point AFTER the data
rekey: the bridge command carried (294.88, 79.24, 23.85) as a jogged override. Cause was entirely
client-side — currentCalibrator matched on robotId (so deck-003 on B14 resolved the arm row), and the
effect seeding the calX/calY/calZ boxes fired only on ROBOT change, so selecting a different deck
never re-seeded them; Calibrate tip then sent those stale boxes as an operator override, which wins
over the stored fixture by design. Fixed: deck-first resolution (legacy robot rows only when the deck
has none), seeding keyed on robot+deck with a .py-default fallback for never-taught decks, and
deckLoadName/deckKey carried on each CalEntry. Lesson: re-keying storage is not enough when the UI
can send an override — check every path that can win over the stored value.

2026-08-28 (cont.) — wax gate-1 default volume 1.6 -> 1.8uL on the run-start form (operator request).
Row-alignment check: row = 2.2+2.2+2.2+1.8 = 8.4uL vs 8.5uL usable per aspiration (20uL pipette -
11.5uL aspirate_remainder), so aspiration batches still end on cartridge-row boundaries. Headroom is
now 0.1uL — raising aspirate_remainder or any gate volume from here WILL break row alignment and
walk the tip over a cartridge wall between batches. .py defaults (all gates 1.60) unchanged; the form
supplies the line's values.

2026-08-31 — master — Deck Cal Studio: pipette-mount toggle moved to the top picker bar
================================================================================
Operator could not pick up a p20 tip on B07 for wax tip-calibration. Cause: mount and tip type are
deliberately independent (since f8a07846, 08-18 — pipettes are not fixed to mounts fleet-wide), so
BOTH must be set: on B07 the p20 is on the RIGHT mount (left = p300, confirmed live). The Studio
defaults to Left, and the mount toggle lived inside the JOG panel where it looks like a during-run
control, so a run got opened on the p300 and the 20uL pickup could not succeed. Toggle now sits in
the top picker bar beside Labware/Deck/Robot (still disabled while a run is open).
Checked at the same time — B07 wax tip-cal has NOT drifted and Rachel's p20/p300 work did not affect
it: deck-004 fixture unchanged since 08-04 (125.181/173.247/38.5, zCalWax 40.15 — deliberately
per-B07 via fix-b07-zcalwax.cjs; empty edit history), B07's daemon is byte-identical to the repo
(has CAL_PROBE_RECIPES), and the last two wax runs measured adjust x=-2.0 y=+1.0/1.1 with z_cal
40.15 — squarely in B07's historical band. Before 08-18 the code inferred right = p20/wax, which
matches B07's actual layout, so the decoupling was a no-op there.
NOT built (offered): auto-select the mount from the chosen tip type, or block when the selected
profile does not match the pipette on that mount.
