Source: branch master @ this commit
What this deployment is for: the mount is chosen with the other pre-run settings, so tip pickup stops failing silently.

2026-08-31 — DATA FIX — B14/deck-003 tip-adjust cap set to 8 (reagent runs died at tip calibration)
================================================================================
Symptom: B14 reagent on deck-003 scanned 20 carts, then picked up a tip, trashed it, picked up
another and stopped — "acts like there are no cartridges". Nothing to do with cartridges: the run
never reached the first aspirate. Run logs (70085c39, 6dd538e2):
  Offset calibration: x=-5.3, y=0.8
  Tip calibration REJECTED (adjust (-6.5,-1.9) exceeds max_tip_adjust 4.0mm) — retrying with a fresh tip
DECISIVE EVIDENCE that -6 is normal for this pair, not a fault: B14's last GOOD reagent run
(eb029606, 08-14) ran the SAME baseline (-5.3, 0.8) and dispensed with adjust (-6.0, -1.7) into 70
wells. So deck-003's holes are taught in the tip-cal-ON frame at ~-6mm x, and the probe is healthy
(travel ~1.2mm — the operator watched it and it looked normal). The only thing that changed since
those good runs is the max_tip_adjust guard that shipped with the guarded reagent build on 08-27
(before that reagent had NO cap and simply applied the -6.0).
Fleet comparison — this is what made B14 the odd one out:
  B07/deck-004 baseline (-1.0,+2.5) adjust ~(-2.0,+1.0) cap unset(4.0)  — device was re-zeroed 07-20
  R04/deck-001 baseline (-5.0,+1.8) adjust ~(-6.0..-6.7) cap 8 (08-20)  — device never zeroed
  B14/deck-003 baseline (-5.3,+0.8) adjust ~(-6.0..-6.5) cap NEVER SET  — device never zeroed
B14 simply never got either treatment (open item from the 08-20 log: "B14: … set maxTipAdjust from
the reading"). FIX: deck-003 fixture maxTipAdjust = 8, matching R04 and restoring exactly the
behaviour of the 08-14 runs that filled correctly. Verified the uploaded reagent protocol declares
max_tip_adjust max=8 so the value is accepted (calibration-rtps also clamps to the declared ceiling).
Audit row written. NOT done (needs a deck re-verify, do not do casually): re-zeroing the B14 and R04
calibrator devices (new string = old - mean adjust) to bring their adjusts into B07's ~1-2mm range —
both decks are taught in their current probe frames, so re-zeroing without re-teaching would shift
every fill by ~6mm.

2026-08-31 — master — Recovered exp-351 cart fb996782 (cancelled but actually completed)
================================================================================
Cart fb996782 (exp 351, S1, device BT-M01-0000-0202) showed cancelled though the operator
watched the test complete. Root cause is in the device-upload Lambda (see
docs/logging-middleware-update/index.mjs test_upload): at 19:52:23Z the device sent an EMPTY
upload (0 readings) 28s after test start, and the Lambda's rule "numberOfReadings===0 ->
cancelled" stamped the cart. The REAL 252-reading upload arrived at 20:19:13Z (duration 1619s,
in line with the batch's 9 other devices) but the handler's idempotency guard
(status==='cancelled' -> return cached) silently DISCARDED it and answered SUCCESS to the
device. Data was still in device_events (event YnMTT0JNQ7NGkbj4v9JM_).
RECOVERY (applied + verified, no code change): re-parsed the blob with the Lambda's exact
parser, wrote rawData + status completed + checkpoints.completed (when=20:19:13Z, who
'brevitest-cloud (recovered)') to cartridge_records, synced the experiment's embedded status,
audit entry changedBy claude-recover-fb996782, full-doc backup at
calibration-backups/fb996782-cancelled-backup-2026-08-31T20-30-17-654Z.json.
OPEN: (1) why the device emitted an empty upload 28s in; (2) Lambda fix — a cancelled cart
with 0 readings should accept a later data-bearing upload (the guard should only skip when
rawData already has readings). Lambda is deployed infra, not this repo.

2026-08-31 — master — wax aspiration floored like reagent (8-cart runs were riding 0.5mm off the tube bottom)
================================================================================
Operator: "with 8 carts we are hitting the bottom of the tube too early... can we make wax match
reagent?" Yes — and the mechanism was a branch, not a calibration issue:
    if aspirate_end < 30: height model   else: source_well.bottom(0.5)
aspirate_end grows as the tube empties, so the branch is chosen by BATCH SIZE. 24 carts (654.8uL)
-> 25.6 (model); 12 -> 29.7 (model); 8 carts (251.6uL) -> 32.4 -> bottom(0.5mm) for the WHOLE run;
4 carts likewise. Small batches ran with the tip 0.5mm off the floor. Reagent never had this branch —
it has min_tip_clearance 1.5mm + tip_floor_z.
Second defect found while checking: the wax tube constants had drifted from the labware — .py said
rim 107.55 / bottom 68.48, cosmas_and_damian_drybath_tuberack says 105.85 / 67.25. Depth was computed
against the hardcoded rim but commanded via source_well.top(-depth) (measured from the REAL rim), so
every aspiration ran 1.70mm deeper than intended.
FIX (wax now mirrors reagent): single aspiration path = height model clamped to
tube_bottom + min_tip_clearance (new RTP, default 1.5mm); rim/bottom read from the loaded labware so
they cannot drift and any rack offset is included. Modelled clearance 8 carts: 0.50mm flat ->
4.22mm tapering to 1.80mm; 24 carts: 10.65 -> 11.12 tapering to 1.80. Submersion stays at
aspirate_cover 2mm, so no new air-pull risk. Wax .py 27 RTPs; stored .py synced.
Deployment: robot protocol — B07 6fd42371 -> 5fa897fc, R04 91a4742a -> 68124b01, B14 471b4098 -> ee4d2dfc
Source: branch master @ this commit
What this deployment is for: small wax batches stop driving the tip into the tube floor.

2026-08-31 — master — deck-cal capture Z: 2mm approach-clearance fix finally merged
================================================================================
Operator asked the teaching convention for the tube rack ("align the tip with the rim?"). The
intended answer is yes — but master's capture math made it wrong: nominal is the critical point
parked at reference + APPROACH_Z_MM (2mm), and capture did dz = liveZ - nominal.z, i.e. measured
from the HOVER. Jogging onto a correct rim stored dz = -2, and each re-teach walked the well down
another 2mm (the "precise re-teaching makes it worse" effect); the fill's own hover then landed the
tip on the rim. Fix = dz + APPROACH_Z_MM, which is commit 4bff9f35 on fix/deckcal-capture-clearance
(2026-07-17) — it had NEVER been merged (verified with git merge-base). Branch now redundant.
Newly urgent because 7cf4896e made the wax protocol read the tube rim/bottom from the labware
definition: a 2mm teach error on the tube rack is now a 2mm aspiration-depth error.
CONVENTION (now true): Move to hole -> jog the tip ONTO the rim -> Capture. Do it with the tip type
you fill with; capture refuses if tip state changed since the move.
CAUTION: wells taught rim-style under the old math sit ~2mm low per teach event. Do NOT blanket-
correct — verify per deck with Tour / Fill motion.

Deployment: production — Vercel auto-deploy from master push
Source: branch master @ this commit
What this deployment is for: teaching a rim now stores the rim.

2026-08-31 — feat/spu-servicing-ui-master — servicing short ID fix + barcode assignment UI committed
================================================================================
Two fixes on the SPU servicing branch, both from a session that died before committing.

extractShortId() in src/routes/spu/mfg/servicing/+page.server.ts sliced the FIRST 8 characters of
the UDI to build the "SPU-0244" label in the Start Service Job picker. Every unit shares that
prefix - GS1 UDIs all start "(01)...(21)0a10aced2021949..." and plain ids all start "BT-M01-0000-" -
so every SPU in the dropdown rendered the same label and the picker could not be used to choose a
unit. Now takes the LAST 4 characters, which is the part that varies, stripping separators first so

2026-08-31 — DATA FIX — deck-001 wax band re-seated onto its reagent surface (matches deck-004)
================================================================================
Operator: deck-001 IS used a lot for wax filling; bring it in line with deck-004.
Evidence for the change — the physical invariant is that a cartridge's wax (even col) and reagent
(odd col) rims sit on the same top face, so their stored z should match:
   deck-002 +0.60 | deck-003 +0.62 | deck-004 +0.16 | deck-001 -4.65  <- lone outlier
deck-001's wax z history shows the cause is deliberate whole-deck operations, NOT the capture-
clearance bug: the 07-10 rebuild session, then +0.5mm (07-28) and -3.9mm (08-06, ialas) applied to
all 288 wax wells. Two decks agree with each other; 001 did not.
APPLIED: each deck-001 wax well re-seated onto ITS OWN reagent partner's z using deck-004's measured
relation (+0.158mm). Per-well, not a flat shift, so deck-001's reagent-taught surface variation is
preserved. Wax z 3.50..3.85 -> 8.36..8.96 (mean shift +4.81mm, range 4.51..5.41). X/Y untouched,
reagent untouched, 0 wells out of bounds (zDimension 12.7). 288 deck_calibration_edits rows written
so this is visible in history and undoable through the Studio; audit_logs row + backup
backups/gen4deck_001-backup-2026-08-31-pre-wax-z-align.json. Post-check: deck-001 wax-reagent now
+0.16mm, matching deck-004 exactly.
Direction of risk is deliberate: RAISING the band means a too-high tip (wax may sit on the face —
visible, harmless, revertible) rather than a too-low one (tip into the cartridge).
MUST DO BEFORE A PRODUCTION WAX RUN ON R04: Deck Cal Studio -> deck-001 -> R04 -> p20/wax tip ->
Fill motion on a few holes and confirm the tip hovers ~1mm above the rim (dispense_depth -1.0).
The run-start freshness gate pushes this geometry to R04 automatically on the next wax run.
Rollback: restore the backup json, or Undo in the Studio.

2026-09-01 — feat/spu-service-groups — group tasks on the servicing board (group notes + per-unit findings)
================================================================================
The floor regularly runs the SAME job across a handful of units (recalibrate a batch, swap a part
across a lot). Before this that meant N unrelated service records, with the shared context living in
somebody's head or copy-pasted into every record's notes, and no way to see which units had actually
been looked at.

NEW MODEL — ServiceGroup (src/lib/server/db/models/service-group.ts, collection service_groups).
It owns only the SHARED half: task name, description, serviceType, priority, and group-wide notes.
Membership is a `groupId` stamped on ServiceRecord; the group deliberately holds NO member array, so
a unit can never be in a group while missing its own service job. Every member keeps its own
location, locationHistory, parts/firmware/other change trail, and close — a grouped unit is
identical to an individually-serviced one apart from the stamp.

ServiceRecord also gains `findings[]` (text + outcome ok/issue/rework/blocked + author + timestamp)
and FINDING_OUTCOMES. Findings are deliberately separate from `notes`: notes are running commentary,
findings are results, and only findings carry an outcome that rolls up to the group.

BOARD — a "Group tasks" section above the individual jobs. Collapsed: task name, type/priority,
unit count, "n/m with findings", and a red "N need attention" badge when any unit's latest outcome
isn't ok. Expanded: group notes + add-note; per member the location, latest-outcome badge, full
findings list, a record-a-finding form, "Complete unit", and "Remove from task"; an "+ Add units"
panel; and "Close remaining N unit(s)" with a shared resolution. Bulk close defaults each unit to
ITS OWN previousStatus rather than forcing units pulled from different states into one bucket, and
refuses to mark the group done if a finalized SPU had to be left open.
New actions: createGroup, addToGroup, removeFromGroup, addGroupNote, addFinding, closeGroupRemaining.
enrollInGroup reuses master's existing createServiceJob helper, so a grouped unit takes the exact
same intake path as a scanned or hand-picked one (same status transition, same audit trail).
The existing closeService now auto-closes a group when its last member leaves.

LAYOUT — the board was stats-first. Reordered to put the functional surface on top: scan bar and
filters, then open jobs, then recently closed, with Summary + "Where they are" moved to the bottom
under a "Statistics" heading. The scan bar was already master's primary path and is untouched.

NOTE ON LINEAGE — this work was first built in the home-root clone on feat/cleaning-calendar, which
forks from master at 2026-03-16 and is 1828 commits behind. That branch carries a STALE parallel copy
of the servicing page with no scan action, no replacePart/recordFirmwareChange/recordOtherChange.
Shipping it would have regressed master by months, so the feature was re-ported onto current master
here instead. Not carried over: the client-side scan card (master already scans server-side) and the
"where it is" card on /spu/[spuId] — that page uses a DIFFERENT service model (embedded
spu.serviceRecords[] with Phase A send / Phase B return) rather than the ServiceRecord collection the
board uses, so which model the record page should read is an open design question, deferred.

Files: src/lib/server/db/models/service-group.ts (new), service-record.ts, models/index.ts,
src/routes/spu/mfg/servicing/+page.server.ts, +page.svelte.
npm run check: 120 errors / 433 warnings — master's pre-existing baseline, ZERO in the touched files.
Not exercised against real data; the Vercel preview build is the gate.

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-6p44wsg03-brevitest.vercel.app
  (immutable: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-6p44wsg03-brevitest.vercel.app)
  build: https://vercel.com/brevitest/bioscale-operations-system-mongodb/6isa3zaRy5S4HsuN4YvJr6F6ZbDF
Source: branch feat/spu-service-groups @ commit a7f92c10
Retrieve this exact code: git checkout feat/spu-service-groups at commit a7f92c10
What this deployment is for: review group tasks (group notes + per-unit findings) and the
  reordered servicing board before merging to master.

[processTask] → [replenish] → [reorder]. `landing` captured|processed|committed (default
captured), `position` 1-based slot in the landing list (Tier 1 order, or the ready queue
when committed; blank = bottom; clamped), sizeClass/classOfService required for the two
shaped landings (the capturer IS the processor — KB2-03 rule kept explicit). Gates are the
existing ones, FRONT-LOADED: committed runs requireReplenisher + dorMissingFields on the
would-be task + a ready-cap check BEFORE anything is written, so a refused commit never
leaves a half-created item; the crossing itself is still replenish() (one audited
replenishment event). Placement reuses reorder()'s bulkWrite path. The `created` activity
entry gains landing/position so the feed explains the jump.

QUICK BOX (/kanban/inventory): the deliverable input under the box is GONE; it is title +
tags + a small "#" position field (blank = bottom, tooltip shows the bottom slot) + a
"Detailed…" link. Server action now calls captureTask.

DETAILED CAPTURE: NEW route /kanban/capture (+page.server.ts, +page.svelte) shaped like the
task detail page — main column title/description/deliverable/handoff brief (+ investigation
question/timebox when itemType=spike); sidebar Landing radios (Commit disabled without
kanban:replenish), Position (placeholder = bottom slot of the chosen list), Commit note,
Shaping (item type, size class w/ policy definitions + sizing test, class of service, due
date — required for milestones and fixed_date, estimate/effort days, assignee, tags with a
datalist + click-chips from the board vocabulary). Required markers follow the landing
(deliverable * when committing; handoff brief * when committing a 'software'-tagged item).
Submit label = Capture / Capture & Process / Capture & Commit. Success redirects to Tier 1
(captured/processed) or the Board (committed) so the row is visible in its slot; errors
render the server reason verbatim. Board header gets "+ New task" → /kanban/capture?
landing=committed. Milestones without a due date are refused (roadmap anchor rule).

AGENT API + MCP: POST /api/agent/operations/kanban/tasks accepts landing/position/sizeClass/
classOfService/commitNote and routes through captureTask (echo + landing + position +
replenish event when committed; ReplenishError → 400/403). kanban_capture (MCP 3.3.0 →
3.4.0) exposes the same fields with the doctrine in the description; capture_bulk and
subtasks keep the plain always-captured shape (bulk commits stay a deliberate
kanban_replenish). Files: src/lib/server/kanban/capture.ts (new), src/routes/kanban/capture/*
(new), src/routes/kanban/inventory/+page.server.ts + .svelte, src/routes/kanban/+page.svelte,

2026-09-01 — feat/kanban-capture-modes — KB2-39: chains as the unit of processing
================================================================================
Jacob: "make chains a core concept… blockers and blocking are what make the chain… a chain
can have branches… only the immediate work belongs in chains because chains are part of
processing… go review the plans." Reviewed all three filed plans (Fall v4, M1 20-SPUs,
M2 robot-arm) + the live roadmap: processing as actually practiced is a WORKSHOP over a
whole milestone (split/merge/wire/scope/spikes/plan), and its unit is the chain; the
per-task Process modal is the last mile. Live: all 4 milestone chains wired but 56/71 chain
tasks still status captured; plans M1/M2 showed 0 spawned tasks (nothing linked plan ↔
milestone). PRD docs/prds/KB2-39-chains.md.

DEFINITION (derived, never stored): a chain = a milestone's dependency DAG (milestone +
everything transitively blocking it via blocks/blocked_by). Branches normal. Named by the
milestone, dated by its dueDate, ordered topologically (rank tiebreak). Primary chain for
shared prerequisites = earliest due date ("also in" the rest). Unanchored chains = wired
components with no milestone downstream, named "→ terminal task". Unwired = the inbox.
next up = not done + every blocker done; behind N = open transitive ancestors.

SERVICE: NEW src/lib/server/kanban/chains.ts — deriveChains() → { chains[], byTask{} }, one
source for every surface. Verified vs live board (scripts/diag-chains.ts, NEW, read-only):
M1 orders TASK-113/125 tracks first, Build-20 behind 9, Validate behind 10; M2 shows its
chute/deck/software tracks; 74 wired tasks; 6 small unanchored chains surfaced.

PLAN ↔ CHAIN: PlanningDocument gains `milestoneId` (indexed). Agent POST /plans validates it
is a milestone task; kanban_file_plan accepts milestoneId (MCP 3.4.0 → 3.5.0). NEW
scripts/backfill-plan-milestones.ts parses "**Milestone:** TASK-NNN" (dry-run default,
APPLY=1) — APPLIED to prod 2026-09-01: M1 → TASK-111, M2 → TASK-127; Fall v4 left unlinked
(names two milestones; its tasks carry sourceRef).

TIER 1 (/kanban/inventory): chain badge on every wired row from the moment it is wired
("◆ M1 · NEXT UP" green / "◆ M1 · behind 3" cyan / "+1" for also-in; click = filter). Chain
chips row (one chain at a time + "Unwired N"). View toggle "By rank | By chain" — chain view
groups rows under chain headers (◆ name, due, done/total, on-Board, here, next-up count,
plan ›, roadmap ›) in DEPENDENCY ORDER, then an Unwired group; rank is untouched (lens, not
re-rank). "Process chain (k)" on each header walks the chain's captured tasks through the
existing Process modal in order, advancing on each save (modal shows "Processing chain: i of
n" + stop-after-this; chain context line ◆ name · position · next up/behind). Estimate days
(working days) joins the Tier 1 Process/reshape modal (was task-page only) — inventory
process/reshape actions now pass estimateDays. URL ?chain=<id>&view=chain[&process=1] opens
the chain view filtered and can auto-start the walk (roadmap band labels + task page + plan
page link here).

ROADMAP: timeline bands = CHAINS (primary membership from deriveChains; replaces the KB2-36
union-find components), sorted by the chain service (dated milestones by due date), each
with a label rail: NAME · due · ±N wd buffer (from the scheduler, green/red) · done/total ·
next up · "Tier 1 ›" · "plan ›". Band height grew 26px for the rail. Unwired block unchanged.
RoadmapCanvas takes a `chains` prop; roadmap load runs deriveChains alongside computeRoadmap.

TASK PAGE: Task Info gains Chain (link to the Tier 1 chain view), "3 of 13", next up /
behind N, due, also-in, plan ›. PLANS: list shows "◆ chain · due · done/total" for linked
plans; detail page gets a live Chain section (dependency order, NEXT UP markers, size/
estimate, Tier 1 › / Roadmap › links). MCP: kanban_board_snapshot tasks carry
chain {id,name,kind,dueDate,position,total,nextUp,behind,also} | null.

scripts/backfill-plan-milestones.ts (new), src/lib/server/db/models/planning-document.ts,

2026-09-01 — feat/kanban-capture-modes — KB2-39 browser QA fixes (local dev server + Chrome)
Browser QA earned its keep again (see the KB2-35 lesson): (1) /kanban/roadmap threw
"$$props.pinned.map is not a function" — the roadmap load inserted deriveChains() in the MIDDLE
of the Promise.all array while destructuring it as the LAST element, so `pinned` received the
chains object and the canvas never mounted ("Loading canvas…" forever). Moved deriveChains() to
the end of the array. svelte-check cannot catch positional destructuring of a Promise.all.
(2) Chain rail labels sat at canvas x=12 and were clipped by the opening viewport — each rail
now anchors to its band's leftmost card (bandRects carry x). (3) KB2-38 quick-box position
field rendered full-width: .tron-input sets width:100% in unlayered CSS (layout.css), which
beats Tailwind's layered w-[72px]; replaced with an inline width. Verified live: Tier 1 By-chain
view + chips + headers, Process-chain modal (walk counter, stop-after-this, estimate field;
NOT submitted — dev points at prod Atlas), plan detail chain section, roadmap rails.
Files: src/routes/kanban/roadmap/+page.server.ts, src/lib/components/kanban/roadmap/
RoadmapCanvas.svelte, src/routes/kanban/inventory/+page.svelte. npm run check: 11 = baseline.

2026-09-02 — feat/spu-tweaks — SPU status flow collapse (SPU-INV-07)
================================================================================
The 12-status SPU enum collapsed to 6: draft → assembling → validating → released ⇄ servicing →
retired. Jacob's framing: border statuses become transition moments — the assembly e-signature now
marks assembling→validating (assemblyStatus keeps "completed"); passing validation is the event
that qualifies a unit for release, and RELEASE IS MANUAL (validating→released via transitionStatus
on the detail page — run page's Mark Validated button replaced by "all steps passed ✓ / Release on
SPU page →" and its markValidated action deleted). R&D is a location, not a status: new
spu.location field, shown on the detail page.

New src/lib/server/spu-status.ts is the single source of truth (SPU_STATUSES, LEGAL_TRANSITIONS,
RETURNABLE_STATUSES, LEGACY_STATUS_MAP, isLegalTransition) — app-level enforcement because nearly
all status writes are updateOne, which SKIPS Mongoose enum validators (how the rogue 'assigned'
write survived). transitionStatus and dashboard updateStatus now validate transitions; assignSpu no
longer writes status at all. Detail-page dropdown offers only legal next statuses (server-computed
legalNextStatuses). Servicing board: shared RETURNABLE_STATUSES, close-ladder fallback
'validated'→'validating', UI default likewise; behavior otherwise unchanged. Deleted the orphaned
/spu/servicing/** ticket routes (3 server files, pages were deleted long ago; they hard-coded 6
dead statuses and wrote unvalidated form input into spu.status). SpuStatusBadge keeps legacy keys
as render-only aliases so the immutable statusTransitions history displays; statusColor likewise.
Both MCP list_spus descriptions updated (bims-mcp version bumped 3.5.0→3.5.1 per convention, kanban
tools untouched); voided dropped from validation/servicing picker filters; batches page drops the
status==='assembled' fallback; seed-domain-data statuses rewritten; voidedAt/voidReason removed
from the Spu schema (nothing wrote them; assembly candidate query filter cleaned).

Data (scripts/diag-spu-statuses.ts): 73 SPUs — draft 37, servicing 26, released-rnd 8, retired 1,
validating 1; zero devices in any other removed status. Migration
scripts/migrate-spu-status-collapse.ts (dry-run/apply, idempotent): 8 spus released-rnd →
{status released, location R&D}; 14 service_records.previousStatus released-rnd → released;
statusTransitions history untouched (55 legacy entries render via badge aliases). Deploy order:
code → production deploy → migration --apply (new code degrades safely on old data; reverse
doesn't).

PRD: docs/prds/SPU-INV-07-status-collapse.md (approved by Jacob: manual release, voided removed).
npm run check: 11 errors / 434 warnings — unchanged baseline, 0 in touched files.

width follows the existing convention. Files: src/routes/parts/+page.server.ts (+nextPartNumbers),
+page.svelte. npm run check: 10 errors / 434 warnings — baseline.

2026-09-02 — feat/spu-tweaks — fix: new parts without a unit cost were invisible
================================================================================
Jacob created "Metal Sheet Metal Enclosure" (PT-SPU-106) and it never appeared in the SPU parts
list. Root cause: a long-standing load filter dropped any part without a positive unitCost
("Remove entries with no cost breakdown") while the Add Part form had no cost field — so EVERY
form-created part was silently hidden (also affected IFU-SPU-001 and PT-SPU-087). Fix: all parts
are listed (missing cost renders "—"; Total Parts counts all; inventory-value stat still sums only
costed parts; low-stock/lowest-inventory consider all), the Add Part form gains a Unit Cost field,
and the create action stores it (String, per schema quirk) plus explicit bomType:'spu'/isBom:true.
Files: src/routes/parts/+page.server.ts, +page.svelte.

2026-09-04 — feat/spu-tweaks — SPU data-drift audit (validation structures + sacred-record gaps)
================================================================================
Deep context load per Jacob: has the Spu "sacred final record" drifted as features were added?
Full report: docs/SPU-DATA-DRIFT-AUDIT-2026-09-04.md (code census + read-only Atlas audit via new
scripts/diag-spu-validation-drift.ts). Headlines: SPU detail-page audit trail queries a field no
audit row has (entityId vs recordId) → ALWAYS empty despite 87 rows in prod; assembly e-signature
keyed to sessionId so it never lists; magnetometer failures + all auto-poll sessions never reach
the Spu; mag override attribution silently stripped by strict mode; thermo run uploads half-written
(criteria hardcoded null); optics sessionId holds a cartridge barcode (View Session 404s) and only
latest-of-N runs syncs; validation.status rollup has zero writers; servicing board (findings,
location history, firmware changes, groups) writes essentially nothing onto the Spu — two parallel
servicing systems can't see each other; validation-run completion touches no member Spu;
finalizedAt never set + Delete-SPU button bypasses sacred middleware (source of 15 orphaned
session spuIds); lux/location/corrections are dead schema branches. Analysis only — no app code
changed. Fix order proposed in the doc (audit-trail one-liner first; append-whitelist sacred
middleware before ever setting finalizedAt).
2026-09-02 — feat/thermo-manual-verdict — thermocouple step: operator Pass/Fail is the verdict
================================================================================
The thermocouple step on the validation run detail (/validation/runs/[runId], reached
from SPU Manufacturing -> Validation) carried two competing verdict paths: manual
Approve/Reject buttons, and an "Evaluate" action that auto-judged the readings against
STANDARD_THERMO_CRITERIA. The auto path has never been reachable in production —
src/lib/server/validation/thermo-criteria.ts still exports null (PRD OQ-1, range TBD),
so the Evaluate button was permanently hidden and an orange banner told operators the
range "is not configured yet", implying the step was blocked when in fact it was not.

Per decision this session: the human verdict is the acceptance criterion for thermocouple
data — there is no automatic range check. Changes, all in the run detail page:
- Approve/Reject relabelled Pass/Fail, matching validation language elsewhere. The notes
  recorded with the verdict changed to "Passed|Failed on operator review of min/max/mode".
- Removed the Evaluate form (the unreachable auto-evaluation trigger).
- Replaced the "acceptance range is not configured" banner with a plain statement that
  the operator judges the readings and that verdict is the record.

Deliberately NOT changed: STANDARD_THERMO_CRITERIA stays null and the evaluateThermo
server action + thermo-upload.ts machinery stay in place, so a numeric range can be
switched back on later without rebuilding it. The standalone /validation/thermocouple
page (operator-entered min/max) is untouched.

The verdict path itself is unchanged and already correct: recordStepResult is sacred-gated
(refuses a finalized SPU), mirrors the outcome onto spu.validation.thermocouple.status,
pushes any prior verdict onto previous[], and writes a validation_run_step_recorded
AuditLog entry.

Files: src/routes/validation/runs/[runId]/+page.svelte (only file touched; +9/-20).
npm run check: 120 errors / 434 warnings, ZERO errors and ZERO warnings in the touched
file. Warnings match the logged baseline exactly; the error count is above the 11 logged
on 2026-09-01 because 64 sit in src/lib/server/mcp/bims-mcp.ts — untouched here, and
most likely a dependency-version artifact of the node_modules junctioned from another
worktree rather than a regression.

  (branch alias: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-bimsv2-brevitest.vercel.app)

2026-09-02 - bimsv2 - standalone thermocouple page: binary operator Pass/Fail
================================================================
The /validation/thermocouple upload page was the last surface still asking the
operator to type an acceptance range (Min/Max Temp inputs, defaults 20/40) and
then auto-judging the readings against it. Removed the range entirely; the
operator now presses Pass or Fail and that verdict IS the record — matching the
run-detail step behavior shipped earlier on this branch.

processThermoUpload() gained a third judgment mode. It was (a) criteria set =>
auto-judge at upload, (b) criteria null => record but leave 'in_progress' /
rollup 'pending'. Now also (c) criteria null + verdict => the operator's binary
call completes the session in the SAME write: status completed/failed,
overallPassed, SPU rollup thermocouple.status passed/failed + completedAt, and
NO criteriaUsed key (there is no range to cite). Stats are still computed over
an infinite window for display only, so nothing is ever auto-judged. A new
`judged = !!criteria || !!verdict` flag replaces the old `criteria ?` gates on
session status, completedAt, the rollup block, and the audit `evaluated` field;
the audit newData also carries `verdict` so a manual call is distinguishable
from a range-computed one forever. The criteria path is untouched and the new
opt is optional, so the run-detail caller (which passes the still-null
STANDARD_THERMO_CRITERIA) behaves exactly as before.

UI: Acceptance Criteria card deleted, minTemp/maxTemp state removed, chart now
renders showBands={false} (no bands without a range), and the single orange
"Save Results" submit became two submit buttons carrying name="outcome"
value="passed"/"failed". Server validates outcome is exactly passed|failed and
rejects anything else, so a verdict can't be skipped. Pass-banner copy no
longer claims "All readings within acceptable range".

Files: src/lib/server/validation/thermo-upload.ts,
src/routes/validation/thermocouple/+page.server.ts,
src/routes/validation/thermocouple/+page.svelte.
npm run check: 119 errors / 434 warnings, ZERO errors and ZERO warnings in all
three touched files; counts match the baseline logged in the entry above (that
entry's 120 included one error since resolved elsewhere).

Deployment: preview — https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-2nwvhkz0n-brevitest.vercel.app
  (branch alias: https://bioscale-operations-system-mongodb-git-bimsv2-brevitest.vercel.app)

2026-09-04 — feat/spu-tweaks — drift-audit quick wins: audit trail visible + mag failures roll up
================================================================================
First two fixes from the 2026-09-04 drift audit, approved by Jacob (signature hardening explicitly
deferred). (1) SPU detail "Record History" panel: the load queried AuditLog by entityId (a field
no row has) sorted by createdAt (not a field) — always empty. Now {tableName:'spus', recordId}
sorted by changedAt (uses the existing compound index); changedBy resolution fixed (writers store
usernames — map only when it's a user _id). 87 existing prod rows become visible; read-path only.
(2) Magnetometer rollups: the manual-read path only wrote spu.validation.magnetometer on PASS
(fails stayed 'pending' forever) — now writes both outcomes with failureReasons, qcStatus still
promoted only on pass; the auto-poll endpoint (the path the mag page actually uses) wrote NO
rollup at all — now writes the same rollup. Sessions remain the raw-evidence record.
Files: src/routes/spu/[spuId]/+page.server.ts, src/routes/validation/magnetometer/+page.server.ts,
src/routes/api/validation/magnetometer/poll/+server.ts.
